The prismatic realization functor for Shimura varieties of abelian type
Naoki Imai (1), Hiroki Kato (2), Alex Youcis (3)

Abstract
. For the integral canonical model 𝒮 𝖪 p \mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}} of a Shimura variety Sh 𝖪 0 ​ 𝖪 p ​ ( 𝐆 , 𝐗 ) \mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X}) of abelian type at hyperspecial level K 0 = 𝒢 ⁡ ( ℤ p ) K_{0}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}) , we construct a prismatic F F -gauge model for the ‘universal’ 𝒢 ⁡ ( ℤ p ) \mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}) -local system on Sh 𝖪 0 ​ 𝖪 p ​ ( 𝐆 , 𝐗 ) \mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X}) . We use this to obtain several new results about the p p -adic geometry of Shimura varieties, notably an abelian-type analogue of the Serre–Tate deformation theorem (realizing an expectation of Drinfeld in the abelian-type case) and a prismatic characterization of these models at individual level.



Introduction

Shimura varieties
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
are a class of varieties over a number field
\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
associated to a reductive
\mathbb{Q}
-group
\mathbf{G}
, and a piece of ancillary Hodge-theoretic data
\mathbf{X}
, which sit at the intersection of differential geometry, algebraic geometry, and number theory. A guiding principle concerning Shimura varieties is that they should be moduli spaces of
\mathbf{G}
-motives.
1
1

1

For general
\mathbf{G}
this is not quite correct, and one should instead consider
\mathbf{G}^{c}
-motives for a certain modified group
\mathbf{G}^{c}
. See
[
LS18
, §3]
and §
2.3
for details. For the sake of simplicity, we ignore this subtlety in the introduction and assume
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{G}^{c}
, but do not make this assumption in the main body of the article.
In particular, there ought to be a universal
\mathbf{G}
-motive
\omega_{\mathrm{mot}}
. While even the precise formulation of this is conjectural, for more well-behaved categories
\mathscr{C}
which approximate the theory of motives one might still write down
\mathscr{C}
-realization functors
. In other words,
\mathbf{G}
-objects
\omega_{\mathscr{C}}
in
\mathscr{C}
which serve the role of
R_{\mathscr{C}}\circ\omega_{\mathrm{mot}}
for
R_{\mathscr{C}}
the
\mathscr{C}
-realization functor from motives to
\mathscr{C}
.

Over
\mathbb{C}
this idea has been mostly realized. Namely, one can construct a functor

(I.1)

\omega_{\mathsf{K},\mathrm{MHM}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathbf{G})\to\mathbf{MHM}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}\mathbb{C}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})),

called the
MHM realization functor
(see
[
BW04
, §2]
). Here for a smooth complex variety
X
we denote by
\mathbf{MHM}(X)
Saito’s category of
mixed Hodge modules
on
X
(see
[
Sai90
]
), which serves as a very close approximation to the theory of
\mathbb{Q}
-motives over
X
. This is a remarkably powerful tool in studying the geometry of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathbb{C}}
(e.g., see
[
BW04
]
) but, up to non-trivial foundational issues, is easy to construct by the very definition of Shimura varieties. Indeed, Shimura varieties start out life as complex analytic spaces of a very Hodge-theoretic flavor, and only after quite sophisticated and inexplicit arguments obtain the structure of algebraic varieties over
\mathbf{E}
.

For applications of Shimura varieties to number theory one must understand Shimura varieties not just over
\mathbb{C}
, but over
p
-adic fields and their integer rings. Until recently, finding an analogue of (
I.1
) in this setting seemed completely out of reach since there was no good analogue for
\mathbf{MHM}(X)
. But, recent deep work of Drinfeld and Bhatt–Lurie on integral
p
-adic Hodge theory (e.g., see
[
Bha23
]
) has provided a good analogue of
\mathbf{MHM}(X)
over a
p
-adic formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}
. Specifically, they construct the category
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
of
prismatic
F
-gauges on
\mathfrak{X}
, closely related to the
syntomic cohomology
from
[
BMS19
]
, and built upon work of Fontaine and Messing. This category promises to form a very close approximation to the category of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-motives over
\mathfrak{X}
.

However, finding a
syntomic analogue
of (
I.1
) remains challenging. Now the analytic origins of Shimura varieties instead of helpful are a hindrance, as their Hodge-theoretic nature over
\mathbb{C}
does not lend itself well to the
p
-adic Hodge-theoretic setting. This is further complicated by the inexplicit descent from
\mathbb{C}
to
\mathbf{E}
. So, except in the rare cases that one can unconditionally get at the ‘true
\mathbf{G}
-motive’ (e.g., if the Shimura datum is of so-called PEL type) it’s not at all clear where to start. This makes the following theorem in the abelian-type setting significant.

Theorem A
.

Let
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
be of abelian type and
p
be an odd prime. Set
K_{0}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
where
\mathcal{G}
is a reductive
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-model of
\mathbf{G}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Then, for the integral canonical model
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
at a
p
-adic place of
\mathbf{E}
, there is a
syntomic realization functor
on
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
, i.e., an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functor

\omega_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})\to\mathbf{Vect}(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})^{\mathrm{syn}})\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})^{\mathrm{syn}}),

which recovers the universal
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local system
\omega_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
on the generic fiber.

There are many applications of Theorem
A
discussed below. We highlight two here.

(1)

Serre–Tate theorem:
if
R\to R/I
is a nilpotent thickening of
p
-nilpotent rings, then for an
R/I
-point
x
of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
the deformations of
x
to an
R
-point of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
are naturally in bijection with the deformations of the prismatic
F
-gauge with
\mathcal{G}
-structure
(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}})_{x}
.

(2)

A characterization of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
at individual level:

\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is the unique smooth and separated model
\mathscr{X}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
which has a syntomic model of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
such that (1) holds, and such that
\widehat{\mathscr{X}}_{\eta}
is the (potentially) crystalline locus of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
. We emphasize that this characterization works at individual level
\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}
, not requiring consideration of the full prime-to-
p
Hecke tower (i.e., letting
\mathsf{K}^{p}
vary).

For (1) the best previously-known versions of the Serre–Tate theorem (in this generality of
R
) hold in the PEL case, where (unlike the abelian-type case) one may leverage the existence of a
\mathbf{G}
-motive. With respect to (2), previously the only characterization of integral canonical models was for the entire family
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
simultaneously, and involve a ‘(strong) extension criterion’ which is less motivic in nature. We feel that both (1) and (2) make substantial progress towards understanding Shimura varieties of abelian type as parameterizing motivic objects.

Syntomic realization functor

Let
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
be a Shimura datum of abelian type with reflex field
\mathbf{E}
. Fix a prime
p>2
and let
E
be the completion of
\mathbf{E}
at a
p
-adic place. Set
G=\mathbf{G}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, and fix a reductive
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-model
\mathcal{G}
of
G
, letting
\mathsf{K}_{0}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
be the associated hyperspecial subgroup. For
\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
a compact open subgroup, write
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}
for
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E}
. Then,

\varprojlim_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{K}_{p}\subseteq\mathsf{K}_{0}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}},

is a
\underline{\mathsf{K}_{0}}
-torsor on the pro-étale site of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, and we let

\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})\to\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}),

be the associated exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functor, an object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})
. Here, for an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category
\mathcal{C}
we write
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathcal{C}
for the category of exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functors
\omega\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})\to\mathcal{C}
.

Let
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
be the integral canonical model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
as in
[
Kis10
]
and
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
its
p
-adic completion. One may then consider the open subspace
(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\eta}\subseteq\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\mathrm{an}}
, and define

\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})\to\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\eta}),\qquad\xi\mapsto\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}(\xi)\vcentcolon=\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)^{\mathrm{an}}|_{(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\eta}},

a
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local system on
(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\eta}
, i.e., an object of the category
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\eta})
.

In
[
IKY24
, Definition 2.27]
, we define when a
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local system on a smooth rigid analytic variety
X
has
prismatically good reduction
relative to smooth formal model
\mathfrak{X}
of
X
. While it requires non-trivial technical input, we show that
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}
has prismatically good reduction relative to
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
by reducing to the Siegel case (see Theorem
2.10
). Using the main results of
[
IKY24
]
we are then able to deduce the existence of a
prismatic realization functor

\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}),

where the target is the category of prismatic
F
-crystals on
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
(see
[
BS23
]
), unique with respect to the property that
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\simeq\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}
, where
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is the étale realization functor from
[
GR24
]
. But, it is initially quite unclear whether
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
can be upgraded to take values in
\mathbf{Vect}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})^{\mathrm{syn}})
.

Remark 1
.

Our construction of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
utilizes abstract
p
-adic Hodge theory from
[
IKY24
]
, ultimately relying on
[
GR24
]
or
[
DLMS24
]
. In
[
Nie21
]
, Nie constructs ‘absolute Hodge cycles’ in the prismatic cohomology of good-reduction abelian varieties over
p
-adic fields. This method currently only works over a point and with inexplicit restrictions on
p
. If these conditions were removed one might alternatively attempt to construct
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
via this method (at least in the Hodge-type case). This would yield an approach closer in spirit to that used in
[
Kis10
]
.

In §
1
we show that for a smooth formal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-scheme
\mathfrak{X}
there is a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence

(I.2)

\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}),

(see Proposition
1.28
), which is proven to be bi-exact in
[
IKY25
]
using our integral analogue
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}
of
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
from op. cit. Here
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
is the category of prismatic
F
-crystals
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
on
\mathfrak{X}
which are
locally filtered free (lff)
: the Nygaard filtration on the Frobenius pullback
\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}
is locally free (see Definition
1.24
).

Thus, to upgrade
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
to an object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})^{\mathrm{syn}})
, it suffices to show that
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
takes values in
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
. One can even hope for a stronger property:
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
is of type
-\mu_{h}
. Here
\mu_{h}
is the Hodge cocharacter of
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
and being of type
-\mu_{h}
means that locally the Frobenius
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}
is in the double coset defined by
-\mu_{h}
(see
[
IKY24
, Definition 3.12]
). By an Artin-approximation-like argument, it suffices to show
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
is of type
-\mu_{h}
over the complete local rings
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}
for each
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-point
x
of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. We achieve this by comparing the pullback of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
to
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}
and a construction of Ito from
[
Ito25
]
using our integral analogue
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}
of
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
.

Thus, by the equivalence in (
I.2
) we obtain a
syntomic realization functor
as in Theorem
A
. In fact, one may use an enhancement of (
I.2
) (see Proposition
1.39
) to show this syntomic realization functor is of type
-\mu_{h}
in an appropriate sense (see Definition
1.32
).

Theorem B

(see Theorem
2.14
)
.

There exists a unique
\mathcal{G}
-object
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}
of
\mathbf{Vect}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})^{\mathrm{syn}})
of type
-\mu_{h}
such that
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{\mathrm{syn}}}\simeq\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}
.

Remark 2
.

In
[
She24
]
, Shen constructs a
de Rham
F
-gauge
on
(\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}
when
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is of Hodge type. In the language of the next section, this is equivalent to a map
(\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu_{h}}_{1}
, whereas our syntomic realization functor is a map
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu_{h}}_{\infty}
.
That said, Shen is able to describe this object in more down-to-earth terms using the de Rham cohomology of abelian varieties and the BGG complex. Our construction recovers Shen’s after base changing along
\Spec(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{E})
and truncating from an
\infty
-truncated (i.e., untruncated) object to a
1
-truncated one, i.e., base changing the composition
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu_{h}}_{\infty}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu_{h}}_{1}
to
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
.

Syntomic integral canonical models and the Serre–Tate theorem

The family of integral canonical models
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
are uniquely characterized
as a system
by a
strong extension property
: for every regular formally smooth
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-algebra
R
, one has

\varprojlim_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{K}^{p}}\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R)=\varprojlim_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{K}^{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]).

While this characterization is sufficient for many applications, it is incapable of characterizing the models
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
for individual levels
\mathsf{K}^{p}
, and is far from a direct moduli-theoretic characterization.

Given our guiding principle for
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, it is natural to expect that a canonical model
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}\mathsf{K}_{0}}
should be a moduli space of
\mathcal{G}
-motives in some sense. If one thinks of prismatic
F
-gauges as being the ‘syntomic realization’ of (and good approximation to) the category of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-motives, it seems not unreasonable to expect a characterization of such models in syntomic terms.

To make this precise, we use the moduli stack
\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu_{h}}_{\infty}
of prismatic
F
-gauges with
\mathcal{G}
-structure of type
-\mu_{h}
suggested by Drinfeld, and developed by Gardner–Madapusi in
[
GM24
]
.

Definition

(see Definition
3.39
)
.

A
syntomic integral canonical model
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is a smooth and separated
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-model
\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
such that

(1)

(\widehat{\mathscr{X}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\eta}
is the potentially crystalline locus of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
,

(2)

there exists a syntomic model of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}
of type
-\mu_{h}
such that the resulting map

(I.3)

\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon\widehat{\mathscr{X}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu_{h}}_{\infty}

is formally étale.

Our ‘motivic’ characterization of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is then the following which additionally realizes an expectation of Drinfeld (see
[
Dri24b
, §4.3.3]
) for abelian-type Shimura varieties.

Theorem C

(see Theorem
3.34
and Theorem
3.40
)
.

The integral canonical model
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is a syntomic integral canonical model, and it is the unique such model.

Of course, the syntomic model of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}
on
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
realizing the map
\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
for the integral canonical model
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is the syntomic realization functor
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}
. And the formal étaleness of this map
\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
can be reinterpeted in terms of a
Serre–Tate theorem
.

Theorem D

(Serre–Tate theorem for abelian-type Shimura varieties, see Theorem
3.34
)
.

Let
R
be a
p
-nilpotent ring and
R\to R/I
a nilpotent thickening. Then, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu_{h}}_{\infty}(R)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R/I)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu_{h}}_{\infty}(R/I)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}

is Cartesian. In other words, the deformations of an
R/I
-point
x
of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
to an
R
-point of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
are canonically in bijection with the deformations of
\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(x)
to an
R
-point of
\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu_{h}}_{\infty}
.

Remark 3
.

In
[
PR24
]
, Pappas–Rapoport conjecture a method to characterize a system of integral models of Shimura varieties in terms of shtukas which would apply even at non-hyperspecial parahoric levels
\mathsf{K}_{0}
. But, this approach only characterizes the full system (and cannot work at individual level
\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}
). Moreover, as the theory of shtukas is based off the theory of
v
-sheaves which cannot distinguish between a ring and its reduced quotient, it is impossible to use the approach of Pappas–Rapoport to obtain a result like Theorem
D
.

To understand the relationship between our work and that of
[
PR24
]
, we observe the following. In
[
IKY24
, §3.3.4]
we construct the shtuka realization of a
\mathcal{G}
-object in prismatic
F
-crystals (or
F
-gauges) over
\mathscr{X}
. Together with Theorem
C
one is able to recover this ‘universal shtuka’ at hyperspecial level, giving a verification of the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture in this case.

Cohomological application

At the end of this introduction, we list some further applications of Theorems
B
,
C
, and
D
. But, we wish to highlight here an immediate
cohomological consequence
.

It is a well-established conjecture that the cohomology spaces
H^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}((\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}},\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])
are meant to realize the global Langlands correspondence for
G
(e.g., see
[
Kot90
]
). Thus, information about these cohomology spaces should have implications for the local and global Langlands programs. To this end, Theorem
B
recovers a result of Lovering (see Proposition
2.21
) that says that these cohomology spaces are crystalline when the Shimura variety is proper.

That said, in recent years much attention has been given to richer refinements of the classical local Langlands program, which concerns
\ell
-adic representations of
p
-adic Galois groups, allowing instead
p
-adic or even mod-
p
representations. For these purposes
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-refinements of the cohomology of Shimura varieties are required, and Theorem
B
allows us to prove results in that direction.

To this end, let
n
be an element of
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
. We say that an object
\Lambda
of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(\Gal(\overline{E}/E))
has
syntomically good reduction
if there exists an
F
-gauge
\mathcal{V}
in
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\mathrm{syn}}/p^{n})
such that
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathcal{V})\simeq\Lambda
. This is a refinement of the notion of being crystalline (cf.
[
Bha23
, Theorem 6.6.13]
).
2
2

2

In general the étale realization functor is
not fully faithful
on
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\mathrm{syn}}/p^{n})
, and so one should really view being syntomically good reduction as a piece of data instead of a condition. Thus, Theorem
2.22
should perhaps be viewed as giving a ‘canonical’ syntomic model for the étale cohomology of various
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
-local systems on
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
.

Theorem E

(see Theorem
2.22
)
.

Suppose that
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is proper. Then, for any object
\xi
of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})
such that the
{\mu}_{h}
-weights of
\xi[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
are in
[0,p-3-i]
, the
\Gal(\overline{E}/E)
-representation
H^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}((\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)/p^{n})
has syntomically good reduction. In fact, there is an isomorphism

H^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}((\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)/p^{n})\simeq T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\left(\mathcal{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{syn}}(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}/\mathcal{O}_{E},\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}(\xi))/p^{n})\right).

Enhancement of Lovering’s crystalline realization functor

We return now to the setting of abelian-type Shimura varieties. In
[
Lov17a
]
, Lovering constructs a functor

\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{crys}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})\to\mathbf{VectF}^{\varphi,\mathrm{div}}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\mathrm{crys}})

called the
crystalline realization functor
, where the target is the category of strongly divisible filtered
F
-crystals on
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. There is a natural identification

D_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{crys}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}].

Moreover, he shows that the lattices
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)
and
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{crys}}(\xi)
are matched by Fontaine–Laffaille theory when it applies (i.e., when
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)
has Hodge–Tate weights in
[0,p-2]
). Lovering’s functor has found multiple applications (e.g., in
[
Lee21
]
and
[
SZ22
]
).

One main motivation for the construction of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}
was to refine this construction to the prismatic/syntomic setting and to remove the weight restrictions on the lattice-comparison aspects of Lovering’s results. We can make precise the fact that
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}
recovers
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{crys}}
and allows one to remove the weight restrictions on Lovering’s results using the integral analogue
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}
of
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
from
[
IKY25
]
.

Theorem F

(see Theorem
2.18
)
.

There are canonical identifications

\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}=\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{crys}}.

In particular for all
\xi
, the lattices
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)
and
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{crys}}(\xi)
are matched by
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}
.

Remark 4
.

The functor
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}\colon\mathbf{Vect}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}})^{\mathrm{syn}})\to\mathbf{VectF}^{\varphi,\mathrm{div}}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\mathrm{crys}})
from
[
IKY25
]
is an equivalence on the Fontaine–Laffaille range, i.e. when restricted to objects with ‘weights’ in
[0,p-2]
. But, it fails to be fully faithful outside of that range. As
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{crys}}
(being a tensor functor) necessarily takes values outside of the Fontaine–Laffaille range, the existence of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}
satisfying Theorem
F
is far from formal.

Further applications

Finally, we mention some further applications of the above results.

•

In the forthcoming work
[
Mad24
]
, Theorem
B
is applied to understand derived cycles on Shimura varieties with applications to special values of
L
-functions/automorphic forms.

•

In the forthcoming work
[
LM24
]
, Theorem
B
is used to produce
p
-integral Hecke operators on Shimura varieties of abelian type. This is used to solve a conjecture of Fakhruddin–Pilloni and to give a conceptual construction of the fiber product diagram conjectured by Scholze, and studied in
[
Zha23
]
and
[
DvHKZ24
]
.

•

Theorem
B
gives rise to a smooth morphism
\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{k}\to\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathsf{Zip}^{-\mu_{h}}
, the zip period map, where
k
is the residue field
E
and the target is the category of
(\mathcal{G},\mu_{h})
-zips (see Theorem
3.41
). This is applied in
[
Rep24
]
to study the coherent cohomology of Shimura varieties.

•

In
[
Ino25
]
, the ideas developed here are applied to construct a prismatic realization functor for toroidal compactifications of integral canonical models of Shimura varieties of Hodge type.

•

In
[
Yan25
]
, Theorem
B
is further applied to obtain a refinement of the zip period map.

Notation and conventions

\diamond

The symbol
p
will always denote a (rational) prime.

\diamond

The functor
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}
is the integral analogue of
D_{\mathrm{crys}}
from
[
IKY25
]
, and we use the notation and conventions concerning various categories of (crystalline)
F
-crystals as in
[
IKY25
, §2.1]
.

\diamond

By a
(derived) formal stack over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
we mean a stack
\mathcal{X}
on the big fpqc site of
p
-nilpotent (animated) rings
R
, which we may view as a stack on the adic flat site
\Spf(\mathbb{Z}_{p})_{\mathrm{fl}}^{\mathrm{adic}}
(see
[
IKY24
, §A.4]
) by declaring
\mathcal{X}(R)\vcentcolon=\lim\mathcal{X}(R/p^{n})
. For a formal stack
\mathcal{X}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
we have the induced stack
\mathcal{X}_{n}
on the fpqc site of
\Spec(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
, and we say that
\mathcal{X}
is a
p
-adic formal Artin stack
if each
\mathcal{X}_{n}
is an Artin stack over the fpqc site of
\Spec(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
.

\diamond

For a property
P
of morphisms of schemes (resp. Artin stacks), an adic morphism of formal schemes
\mathfrak{X}\to\mathfrak{Y}
where
\mathfrak{Y}
has an ideal sheaf of definition
\mathcal{I}
(resp. a morphism of
p
-adic formal Artin stacks
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
), is
adically
P
(or
\mathcal{I}
-adically
P
)
if the reduction modulo
\mathcal{I}^{n}
(resp. the morphism
\mathcal{X}_{n}\to\mathcal{Y}_{n}
) is
P
for all
n
. If
A\to B
is an adic morphism of rings with the
I
-adic topology, for
I\subseteq A
an ideal, then we make a similar definition.

\diamond

For an Artin stack
\mathscr{X}
on the fpqc site of
\Spec(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and a subset
T\subseteq|\mathscr{X}|
we define the
formal completion along
T
to be the substack on the fpqc site of
\Spec(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
to be

\widehat{\mathscr{X}}_{T}(R)\vcentcolon=\left\{f\colon\Spec(R)\to\mathscr{X}:f(|\Spec(R)|)\subseteq T\right\}.

If
T
is clear from context we shall often omit it from the notation.

\diamond

For a morphism
f\colon X\to Y
we denote
R^{i}f_{\ast}\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
and
R^{i}f_{\ast}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
by
\mathcal{H}^{i}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X/Y)
and
\mathcal{H}^{i}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(X/Y)
, respectively. Similar notation will be applied for other cohomology theories.

\diamond

Our notation and conventions concerning derived algebraic geometry are as in
[
GM24
]
.

\diamond

For a non-archimedean field
K
, a
rigid
K
-space

X
is an adic space locally of finite type over
K
. We denote the set of
classical points
by
|X|^{\mathrm{cl}}:=\left\{x\in X:[k(x):K]<\infty\right\}
.

\diamond

For an
R
-module
M
and an ideal
I\subseteq R
(resp. principal ideal
(a)\subseteq R
) we often write
M/I
(resp.
M/a
) as shorthand for
M/IM
(resp.
M/aM
).

\diamond

A filtration always means a decreasing and exhaustive
\mathbb{Z}
-filtration (i.e.,
\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\Fil^{i}=M
).

\diamond

For a ring
A
and an element
a
of
A
, denote by
\Fil^{\bullet}_{a}
the filtration with
\Fil^{r}_{a}=a^{r}A
for
r>0
, and
\Fil^{r}_{a}=A
for
r\leqslant 0
. Define
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{triv}}\vcentcolon=\Fil^{\bullet}_{0}
.

\diamond

A filtration of (sheaves of) modules is
locally split
if its graded pieces are locally free.

\diamond

For an
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra
R
(resp.
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-scheme
X
), we denote by
F_{R}
(resp.
F_{X}
) its absolute Frobenius.

1.
The Tannakian framework for prismatic
F
-gauges

In this section we discuss the Tannakian aspects of Drinfeld and Bhatt–Lurie’s theory of prismatic
F
-gauges and compare it with the Tannakian theory of prismatic
F
-crystals as developed in
[
IKY24
]
. We refer the reader to
[
IKY24
, §1]
,
[
Bha23
]
, and
[
IKY24
, Appendix A]
for preliminary discussions of prismatic
F
-crystals, Tannakian theory, and stack-theoretic notions, respectively.

1.1.
Some preliminaries on graded and filtered algebra

The extra structure present in a prismatic
F
-gauge versus a prismatic
F
-crystal is that of a filtration with good properties. So, we first describe some general results in the algebra of filtered rings/modules.

1.1.1.
Basic definitions and results

We use standard terminology concerning filtered rings
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
on topoi
\mathscr{T}
and filtered modules over them (e.g., see
[
Tsu20
, Definition 10]
), and only comment on two pieces of terminology/notation not explicitly stated there:

•

For filtered modules
(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
and
(N,\Fil^{\bullet}_{N})
over
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
the
filtered tensor product

(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})\otimes_{(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})}(N,\Fil^{\bullet}_{N})

is the module
M\otimes_{R}N
equipped with the filtration where

(1.1.1)

\Fil^{r}_{M\otimes_{R}N}\vcentcolon=\sum_{a+b=r}\mathrm{im}(\Fil^{a}_{M}\otimes_{R}\Fil^{b}_{N}\to M\otimes_{R}N).

When
(N,\Fil^{\bullet}_{N})=(S,\Fil^{\bullet}_{S})
is a filtered ring with the structure of a filtered module over
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
via a filtered ring map, then this tensor product is a filtered module over
(S,\Fil^{\bullet}_{S})
.

•

A
filtered crystal
over
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
is a filtered module
(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
over
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
such that for every morphism
T\to T^{\prime}
in
\mathscr{T}
the natural morphism

(M(T^{\prime}),\Fil^{\bullet}_{M}(T^{\prime}))\otimes_{(R(T^{\prime}),\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}(T^{\prime}))}(R(T),\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}(T))\to(M(T),\Fil^{\bullet}_{M}(T))

is an isomorphism of filtered modules over
(R(T),\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}(T))
.
3
3

3

For a filtered ring
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
, we observe that there is a natural equivalence between filtered modules over
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
and filtered crystals for the filtered ring
(\mathcal{O}_{\Spec(R)},\widetilde{\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}})
in the Zariski topos on
\Spec(R)
. Thus, hereon out we will treat the theory of filtered modules over a ring as a special case of the theory of filtered crystals.

With the obvious notion of morphisms, denote by
\mathbf{MF}(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
the category of filtered crystals over
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
. Note that
\mathbf{MF}(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
has the structure of an exact
R
-linear
\otimes
-category where we define the tensor product by the same formula as in (
1.1.1
), and where

0\to(M_{1},\Fil^{\bullet}_{M_{1}})\to(M_{2},\Fil^{\bullet}_{M_{2}})\to(M_{3},\Fil^{\bullet}_{M_{3}})\to 0,

is exact if

0\to\Fil^{r}_{M_{1}}\to\Fil^{r}_{M_{2}}\to\Fil^{r}_{M_{3}}\to 0

is an exact sequence of
R
-modules for every
r
in
\mathbb{Z}
.

The following freeness condition will play an important role in our paper.

Definition 1.1

(cf.
[
Tsu20
, Definition 10]
)
.

Let
\mathscr{T}
be a topos with final object
\ast
, and
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
a filtered ring in
\mathscr{T}
. A filtered module
(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
over
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
is
(finite) free
if there exists a
filtered basis
: a collection
(e_{\nu},r_{\nu})_{\nu=1}^{n}
where
(e_{\nu})_{\nu=1}^{n}
is a basis of
M
as an
R
-module, and
r_{\nu}
are integers, such that

(1.1.2)

\Fil^{r}_{M}=\sum_{\nu=1}^{n}\Fil^{r-r_{\nu}}_{R}\cdot e_{\nu}.

We say that
(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
is
locally filtered free (lff)
if there exists a cover
\{T_{i}\to\ast\}
such that the restriction of
(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
to each slice topos
\mathscr{T}/T_{i}
is (finite) free for all
i
. We denote the full subcategory of
\mathbf{MF}(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
consisting of lff objects by
\mathbf{MF}^{\mathrm{lff}}(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
.

For a filtered crystal
(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
over the filtered ring
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
in a topos
\mathscr{T}
, we recall that the
r^{\text{th}}
-graded piece (for
r
in
\mathbb{Z}
) is defined as the
R
-module

\mathrm{Gr}^{r}(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})=\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{M}/\Fil^{r+1}_{M}.

Example 1.2
.

When
\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}=\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{triv}}
a filtered crystal
(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
is (locally) filtered free over
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{triv}})
if and only if finitely many graded pieces of
\Fil^{\bullet}_{M}
are non-zero and the graded pieces are (locally) free
R
-modules, i.e., that
\Fil^{\bullet}_{M}\subseteq M
is a locally split filtration.

It is not hard to show that the category
\mathbf{MF}^{\mathrm{lff}}(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
is closed under tensor product, and so inherits the structure of an exact
R
-linear
\otimes
-subcategory from
\mathbf{MF}(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
.

We end by making an elementary, but useful, observation about short exact sequences of filtered modules. First recall that a map
f\colon(M_{1},\Fil^{\bullet}_{1})\to(M_{2},\Fil^{\bullet}_{2})
of filtered
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
-modules is called
strict
if the equality
f(\Fil^{j}_{1})=f(M)\cap\Fil^{j}_{2}
for all
j
(cf.
[
SP
,
Tag 0120
]
and
[
SP
,
Tag 05SI
]
).

Lemma 1.3
.

Let
R
be a ring, and let

(1.1.3)

0\to(M_{1},\Fil_{1}^{\bullet})\to(M_{2},\Fil_{2}^{\bullet})\to(M_{3},\Fil_{3}^{\bullet})\to 0,

be a sequence of filtered
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{triv}})
-modules, which is a short exact sequence on the underlying
R
-modules. Then, the following are equivalent:

(1)

the maps
(M_{1},\Fil_{1}^{\bullet})\to(M_{2},\Fil_{2}^{\bullet})
and
(M_{2},\Fil_{2}^{\bullet})\to(M_{3},\Fil_{3}^{\bullet})
are strict,

(2)

the sequence

(1.1.4)

0\to\Fil^{j}_{1}\to\Fil^{j}_{2}\to\Fil^{j}_{3}\to 0

is exact for all
j
(i.e., (
1.1.3
) is an exact sequence of filtered
R
-modules).

Suppose further that each of these filtered modules is strictly exhaustive
(i.e.,
\Fil^{j}=M
for a small enough
j
), then (1) and (2) are further equivalent to

(3)

the sequence

(1.1.5)

0\to\mathrm{Gr}^{j}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{1})\to\mathrm{Gr}^{j}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{2})\to\mathrm{Gr}^{j}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{3})\to 0

is exact for all
j
.

Proof.

The equivalence of (1) and (2) follows from the definition of strictness. By the snake lemma, (2) implies (3). We can check the converse by induction on
j
using the snake lemma.
∎

1.1.2.
The Rees algebra construction

We now recall the Rees construction for a filtered ring/module, and relate it to the notion of lff filtered modules.

We begin by reviewing some terminology concerning graded rings.

•

A
graded ring
is a ring
R
together with a decomposition
R=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}R_{r}
as abelian groups such that
R_{r}\cdot R_{s}\subseteq R_{r+s}
for all
r
and
s
in
\mathbb{Z}
. For a ring
A
, we say that
R
is a
graded
A
-algebra
if there is a ring map
A\to R
with image in
R_{0}
.

•

A
graded module

M
over
R=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}R_{r}
consists of an
R
-module
M
, and a decomposition
M=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}M_{r}
as abelian groups such that
R_{r}\cdot M_{s}\subseteq M_{r+s}
for all
r
and
s
in
\mathbb{Z}
.

•

We say that a graded module
M=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}M_{r}
over
R=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}R_{r}
is
finite projective
if its underlying
R
-module is finite projective.
4
4

4

See
[
Lau21
, Lemma 3.0.1]
for why this terminology is unambiguous

•

A graded ring map
R\to S
is a ring map
f\colon R\to S
with
f(R_{r})\subseteq S_{r}
for all
r
.

•

For graded
R
-modules
M
and
N
, we define their
graded tensor product
by declaring

(M\otimes_{R}N)_{n}=\left\{\sum_{i}m_{i}\otimes n_{i}\in M\otimes_{R}N:m_{i}\in M_{r},\,n_{i}\in N_{s},\text{ and }r+s=n\right\}.

When
N=S
is a graded ring equipped with the structure of a graded
R
-module via a graded ring map, then this tensor product is a graded
S
-module.

With the obvious notion of morphisms, denote by
\mathbf{MG}(R)
the category of graded modules over
R=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}R_{r}
. Note that
\mathbf{MG}(R)
has the structure of an exact
R
-linear
\otimes
-category where

0\to\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}M_{1,r}\to\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}M_{2,r}\to\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}M_{3,r}\to 0,

is said to be exact if

0\to M_{1,r}\to M_{2,r}\to M_{3,r}\to 0

is an exact sequence of abelian groups for every
r
in
\mathbb{Z}
.

We denote by
\mathbf{MG}^{\mathrm{fp}}(R)
the full subcategory of
\mathbf{MG}(R)
consisting of finite projective objects. This is clearly closed under tensor products, and therefore
\mathbf{MG}^{\mathrm{fp}}(R)
inherits the structure of an exact
R
-linear
\otimes
-subcategory from
\mathbf{MG}(R)
.

For a filtered ring
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
we now wish to relate
\mathbf{MF}^{\mathrm{lff}}(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
to
\mathbf{MG}^{\mathrm{fp}}(S)
for a certain graded ring
S
associated to
R
which we now discuss.

Definition 1.4
.

Let
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
be a filtered ring. Then, its
Rees algebra
(e.g., see
[
LvO96
, Chapter I, §4.3, Definition 5]
) is the graded ring

\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})\vcentcolon=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}\Fil^{r}_{R}t^{-r}\subseteq R[t^{\pm 1}].

For a filtered
R
-module
(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
we define its
Rees module
(see loc. cit.) to be

\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})\vcentcolon=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}\Fil^{r}_{M}t^{-r}\subseteq M\otimes_{R}R[t^{\pm 1}],

which is a graded
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
-module.

Suppose that
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})\to(S,\Fil^{\bullet}_{S})
is a map of filtered rings, and
(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
is a filtered
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
-module. Then, by functoriality of the Rees algebra construction we obtain a natural map
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})\to\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{S})
of graded rings. One thus obtains a canonical morphism

(1.1.6)

\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})\otimes_{\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})}\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{S})\to\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{M\otimes_{R}S}),

of graded
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{S})
-modules. This map is an isomorphism if the source has no non-trivial
t
-torsion
(e.g., if
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
is a flat module over
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
). Indeed, it suffices to verify this map induces an isomorphism after applying the inverse to the equivalence in
[
LvO96
, Chapter I, §4.3, Proposition 7]
(see also
[
LvO96
, Chapter I, §4.3, Observation 6 (a)]
). But, this is trivial.

Observe that as
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
is a graded
R
-algebra, we have a natural action of the group
R
-scheme
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}=\Spec(R[x^{\pm 1}])
on
\Spec(\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}))
corresponding to the coaction map

\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})\to\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})\otimes_{R}R[x^{\pm 1}],

uniquely specified by declaring that an element
a
in
\Fil^{r}_{R}t^{-r}
in
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
maps to
a\otimes x^{r}
. We may then consider the Artin stack over
R
given by

\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})\vcentcolon=[\Spec(\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}))/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}],

called the
Rees stack
of
(R,\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{R})
. Given a graded
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
-module
M=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}M_{r}
there is a natural action of
\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
on
M
corresponding to the coaction map

M\to M\otimes_{R}R[x^{\pm 1}]

defined in the analogous way. This defines a quasi-coherent sheaf on
\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
by the construction in
[
SP
,
Tag 06WT
]
. We denote this functor by
(-)/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
.

Finally, suppose that
R
is
J
-adically complete with respect to a finitely generated ideal
J\subseteq R
. We define the
completed Rees stack

\widehat{\mathcal{R}}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
(leaving the ideal
J
implicit) to be the completion of
\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
along
\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})\times_{\Spec(R)}\Spec(R/J)
. We then have a natural pullback functor

\widehat{(-)}\colon\mathbf{Vect}(\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}))\to\mathbf{Vect}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}))=\twolim\mathbf{Vect}(\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})\times_{\Spec(R)}\Spec(R/J^{n})),

which is an
R
-linear
\otimes
-functor.

Proposition 1.5
.

Suppose that
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
is a filtered ring. Then, the functors

\mathbf{MF}^{\mathrm{lff}}(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})\to\mathbf{MG}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})),\qquad(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})\mapsto\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{M}),

and

(-)/\mathbb{G}_{m}\colon\mathbf{MG}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}))\to\mathbf{Vect}(\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}))

are
2
-funtorial bi-exact
R
-linear
\otimes
-equivalences. Suppose further that
R
is
J
-adically complete with respect to a finitely generated ideal
J\subseteq R
and that
\Fil^{i}_{R}\subset R
is closed with respect to the
J
-adic topology, for every
i
. Then, the functor

\widehat{(-)}\colon\mathbf{Vect}(\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}))\to\mathbf{Vect}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}))

is a
2
-functorial bi-exact
R
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence.

Before proving this proposition, we first establish that the Rees construction preserves and reflects locally-free-like conditions. More precisely, we have the following.

Proposition 1.6
.

Let
(R,\Fil_{R}^{\bullet})
be a filtered ring. Then a filtered module
(M,\Fil_{M}^{\bullet})
over
(R,\Fil_{R}^{\bullet})
is lff if and only if its Rees module
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{M}^{\bullet})
is finite projective over
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{R}^{\bullet})
.

Proof.

Since the problem is local on
R
,
5
5

5

Indeed, this follows from the observation that if
y
is an element of
R
, then the Rees module for
(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})|_{D(y)}
, where
D(y)
is the non-vanishing locus of
y
, is canonically identified with
\mathrm{Rees}(R,\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{y}}]
.
we may assume that
R
is a local ring. Then
(M,\Fil_{M}^{\bullet})
being lff is equivalent to it being finite free, and hence easily implies that
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{M}^{\bullet})
is free.

We show the converse. When
\Fil_{R}^{\bullet}=\Fil_{(1)}^{\bullet}
, i.e., when
\Fil_{R}^{i}=R
for all
i
in
\mathbb{Z}
, the assertion is obvious. So, we assume
\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}\neq\Fil_{(1)}^{\bullet}
, i.e., that
\Fil^{1}_{R}
is contained in the maximal ideal of the local ring
R
.
We first observe that the assertion holds when
\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}=\Fil_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\bullet}
, i.e., when
\Fil^{1}_{R}=0
. Indeed, note that
\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}\Gr^{r}_{\Fil_{M}}
, being the specialization of
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
to
t=0
(see
[
LvO96
, Chapter I, §4.3, Proposition 7]
), is finite projective over
R
, and hence so is each
\Gr^{r}_{\Fil_{M}}
. As
R
is local, this implies that each
\Gr^{r}_{\Fil_{M}}
is finite free, and hence
(M,\Fil_{M}^{\bullet})
is filtered free.

In general, consider the map
(R,\Fil_{R}^{\bullet})\to(R/\Fil^{1}_{R},\Fil_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\bullet})
and the corresponding map

\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{R}^{\bullet})\to\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\bullet})\simeq(R/\Fil_{R}^{1})[t].

We observe that the scalar extension along this map is described as follows.

Lemma 1.7
.

We have a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{M}^{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{R}^{\bullet})}\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\bullet})\simeq\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\Fil^{r}_{M}}{\sum_{i\geqslant 1}\Fil^{i}_{R}\cdot\Fil^{r-i}_{M}}t^{-r}

of graded
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\bullet})
-modules, where the multiplication-by-
t
map on the right-hand side is defined by the canonical maps induced by
\Fil^{r+1}_{M}\to\Fil_{M}^{r}
.

Proof.

This follows by considering the short exact sequence

0\to\bigoplus_{i\geqslant 0}\Fil_{R}^{1}t^{i}\oplus\bigoplus_{i\leqslant-1}\Fil^{-i}_{R}t^{i}\to\underbrace{\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}\Fil_{R}^{-i}t^{i}}_{\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{R}^{\bullet})}\to\underbrace{\bigoplus_{i\geqslant 0}R/\Fil^{1}_{R}t^{i}}_{\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\bullet})}\to 0,

and tensoring it with
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{M}^{\bullet})
over
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{R}^{\bullet})
.
∎

Now assume that
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
is finite projective over
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
. Then the tensor product
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{M}^{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{R}^{\bullet})}\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{\mathrm{triv}})
is finite projective over
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{triv}})
. In particular, the multiplication-by-
t
map

\overline{\Fil}^{r+1}\vcentcolon=\frac{\Fil^{r+1}_{M}}{\sum_{i\geqslant 1}\Fil^{i}_{R}\cdot\Fil^{r+1-i}_{M}}\to\overline{\Fil}^{r}=\vcentcolon\frac{\Fil^{r}_{M}}{\sum_{i\geqslant 1}\Fil^{i}_{R}\cdot\Fil^{r-i}_{M}}

is injective. This implies by
[
LvO96
, Chapter I, §4.3, Proposition 7]
that this graded
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{triv}})
-module is the Rees module of some module with filtration of the form
(\overline{M},\overline{\Fil}^{\bullet})
over
(R/\mathrm{Fil}^{1}_{R},\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{triv}})
. By loc. cit., the underlying module
\overline{M}
is given by
M/\Fil^{1}_{R}\cdot M
. Since we have already seen that the assertion holds in the case where
\Fil_{R}^{\bullet}=\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{triv}}
, we deduce that
(\overline{M},\overline{\Fil}^{\bullet})
is actually free.

Let
(\bar{e}_{\nu},r_{\nu})_{\nu=1}^{n}
be a filtered basis of
(\overline{M},\overline{\Fil}^{\bullet})
over
(R/\Fil^{1}_{R},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{triv}})
, and take a lift
(e_{\nu})_{\nu=1}^{n}
with
e_{\nu}
in
\Fil^{r_{\nu}}
, which is a basis of the
R
-module
M
.

Claim 1.8
.

The tuple
(e_{\nu},r_{\nu})_{\nu=1}^{n}
is a filtered basis of
(M,\Fil_{M}^{\bullet})
over
(R,\Fil_{R}^{\bullet})
. In other words,

\Fil^{r}_{M}=\sum_{\nu=1}^{n}\Fil_{R}^{r-r_{\nu}}e_{\nu},

for every
r
in
\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

When
r\leqslant r_{\nu}
for every
\nu
, we have
\Fil^{r}_{M}=M
by Nakayama’s lemma, and this is clearly equal to the right-hand side of the claimed equality.

We show the equality by induction on
r
. Fix an
r
in
\mathbb{Z}
and assume that the equality holds for
r
. We show
\Fil^{r+1}_{M}=\sum_{\nu=1}^{n}\Fil_{R}^{r+1-r_{\nu}}e_{\nu}
. The right-hand side is evidently contained in the left. Conversely, consider
x=\sum_{\nu}a_{\nu}e_{\nu}
in
\mathrm{Fil}^{r+1}_{M}
. Since
x
is in
\Fil^{r}_{M}
, we know by induction that
a_{\nu}
belongs to
\Fil^{r-r_{\nu}}_{R}
. For
\nu
with
r=r_{\nu}
, we have that
\bar{e}_{\nu}
is in
\overline{\Fil}^{r}\setminus\overline{\Fil}^{r+1}
, and hence that
a_{\nu}
belongs to
\Fil^{1}_{R}=\Fil^{r+1-r_{\nu}}_{R}
.
We now consider
x_{1}=\sum_{\nu:r>r_{\nu}}a_{\nu}e_{\nu}
, which we know is both in
\Fil^{r+1}_{M}
and
\sum_{i\geqslant 1}\Fil_{R}^{i}\Fil^{r-i}_{M}
. Thus, by the injectivity of the map
\overline{\Fil}^{r+1}\to\overline{\Fil}^{r}
, we get that
x_{1}
is in
\sum_{i\geqslant 1}\Fil_{R}^{i}\Fil_{M}^{r+1-i}
, which by induction, implies that
a_{\nu}
is in
\Fil^{r+1-r_{\nu}}_{R}
for
\nu
with
r>r_{\nu}
.
∎

In particular, this claim implies the filtered module
(M,\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
is filtered free as desired.
∎

Remark 1.9
.

The proof of Proposition
1.6
shows the following. Assume that
\Fil^{1}_{R}
is contained in the Jacobson radical of
R
and that
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})\otimes_{\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})}\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\bullet})
is free over
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{\mathrm{triv}}^{\bullet})\simeq R/\Fil^{1}_{R}[t]
. Then
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{M})
is free over
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
, or equivalently, the filtered module
(M,\Fil_{M}^{\bullet})
is filtered free over
(R,\Fil_{R}^{\bullet})
.

Proof of Proposition
1.5
.

Given Proposition
1.6
, the fact that the first functor is an
R
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence follows from
[
LvO96
, Chapter I, §4.3, Proposition 7]
together with the isomorphism given by (
1.1.6
). Moreover, an explicit quasi-inverse to the first functor is given by by taking a graded
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
-module
N=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}N_{r}
to the module
M=N/(t-1)
with

\Fil^{r}_{M}\vcentcolon=(N_{r}+(t-1)N)/(t-1)N.

The fact that this functor and its quasi-inverse are exact follows from Lemma
1.3
,
[
LvO96
, Chapter I, §4.3, Proposition 8]
, together with the observation that an exact sequence of projective
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
-modules is split and thus exactness is preserved by any additive functor.

The fact that the second functor is an exact
R
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence follows from the general theory of stacks (e.g., see again
[
SP
,
Tag 06WT
]
). Its bi-exactness is clear by inspection.

Finally, to show the last functor is a bi-exact
R
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence we begin by defining a functor
\mathbf{Vect}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}))\to\mathbf{Vect}(\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}))
. Noting that there is a canonical identification

\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})\times_{\Spec(R)}\Spec(R/J^{n})\simeq\left[\Spec\left(\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})\otimes_{R}R/J^{n}\right)/\mathbb{G}_{m,R}\right],

so giving such a functor is equivalent to giving a functor

(1.1.7)

\twolim_{n}\mathbf{MG}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})/J^{n})\to\mathbf{MG}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})),

which we now do.

In the following, we denote by
\mathcal{O}_{n}(d)
the graded
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})/J^{n}
-module with underlying
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})/J^{n}
-module free of rank one, generated by a homogeneous element of degree
d
.

Claim 1.10
.

Let
(M_{n}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}M_{n,i})_{n\geqslant 1}
be an object of the source of (
1.1.7
). Then, the graded
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{R}^{\bullet})
-module
M\vcentcolon=\varprojlim_{n}M_{n}\simeq\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}(\varprojlim_{n}M_{n,i})
is finite projective.
6
6

6

Note that this first inverse limit is taken in the category of graded
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
-modules, which is indeed computed via this last direct sum.
Moreover, the natural map
M\otimes_{R}R/J^{n}\to M_{n}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By taking a finite set of homogeneous generators of
M_{1}
, we may produce a graded surjection
\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r}\mathcal{O}_{1}(d_{j})\to M_{1}
for some
r
and some
d_{1},\ldots,d_{r}
which induces generators
m_{1,1},\ldots,m_{1,r}
of
M_{1}
. This surjection of graded
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})/J
-modules is split by the projectivity of
M_{1}
.

Choosing compatible homogeneous lifts
m_{n,i}
of
m_{1,j}
for each
n\geqslant 1
and
j=1,\ldots,r
we obtain a compatible system of homogeneous maps
(\bigoplus_{j=1}^{r}\mathcal{O}_{n}(d_{j})\to M_{n})_{n}
of
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})/J^{n}
-modules. As
J
is nilpotent in
R/J^{n}
we further see by Nakayama’s lemma that these maps are surjective for all
n
and thus, using the projectivity of
M_{n}
, split surjections. By the assumption that
\Fil^{i}_{R}
is closed in
R
for all
i
, the inverse limit
\lim_{n}\bigoplus_{j}\mathcal{O}_{n}(d_{j})
is identified with
\bigoplus_{j}\mathcal{O}(d_{j})
as a graded
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R})
module, which is finite projective.
As we have a split surjection
\bigoplus_{j}\mathcal{O}(d_{j})\to M
, the inverse limit
M
is also finite projective as desired.

Finally, thanks to the system of split surjection constructed above, the natural morphism
M\otimes_{R}R/J^{n}\to M_{n}
being an isomorphism is reduced to case when
M=\mathcal{O}(d_{j})
, which is clear.
∎

Claim
1.10
allows us to produce a functor as in (
1.1.7
) which we claim is quasi-inverse to
\widehat{(-)}
. On filtered finite free modules (i.e., the modules of the form
\bigoplus_{j}\mathcal{O}(d_{j})
), this follows as
\Fil^{i}_{R}\subseteq R
is closed for all
i
. In general, let
M
be an object of
\mathbf{MG}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{R}))
. Then showing that
M\to\lim_{n}M\otimes_{R}R/J^{n}
is an isomorphism can be reduced to the filtered finite free case by taking a split surjection
\bigoplus_{j}\mathcal{O}(d_{j})\to M
. That the other composition is naturally isomorphic to the identity follows from Claim
1.10
. The fact that both of these functors are exact
R
-linear
\otimes
-functors is clear by inspection.
∎

1.2.
Prismatic
F
-gauges in vector bundles

We now recall the two ways of describing prismatic
F
-gauges in vector bundles: in terms of Rees algebras and in terms of formal stacks.

Description in terms of Rees algebras

We begin by recalling the natural filtration on a prism used to construct the operative Rees algebras for prismatic
F
-gauges.

Definition 1.11
.

For a prism
(A,I)
, we define the
Nygaard filtration
on
A
as follows:

A\supseteq\Fil^{r}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(A,I)\vcentcolon=\begin{cases}\phi_{A}^{-1}(I^{r})&\mbox{if}\quad r\geqslant 0\\
A&\mbox{if}\quad r<0.\end{cases}

We often write
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(A)
or just
\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}
when there is no chance for confusion.

Lemma 1.12
.

Let
(A,I)
be a bounded prism. Then,
\mathrm{Fil}^{r}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(A,I)\subseteq A
is closed for all
r
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the ideals
I^{r}
are closed, and that
\phi_{A}\colon A\to A
is
(p,I)
-adically continuous. To see the former, it suffices by
[
Mat80
, §23.B]
to show that
A/I^{r}
is
J
-adically complete with
J=(p,I)A/I^{r}
. But, as
J^{r}\subseteq pA/I^{r}\subseteq J
we have that the
J
-adic and
p
-adic topologies coincide. Thus, the claim follows from
[
IKY24
, Lemma 1.2]
. For the latter claim, it suffices to observe that
\phi_{A}
stabilizes
(p,I)
(see
[
GR24
, Lemma 3.4]
).
∎

Let
R
be a qrsp ring (see
[
BMS19
, Definition 4.20]
), and consider the initial object
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R},I_{R})
of
R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
(see
[
BS22
, Proposition 7.2]
). Associated to
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}))
are the following two maps:

(1)

the map of
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
-algebras

\tau\colon\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}))\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R},\qquad t\mapsto 1,

(2)

the graded homomorphism

\sigma\colon\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}))\to\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{R}^{r}t^{-r},\qquad\sum_{r}a_{r}t^{-r}\mapsto\sum_{r}\phi(a_{r})t^{-r},

where the target is considered as a graded
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
-subalgebra of
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I_{R}}}][t^{\pm 1}]
.

Remark 1.13
.

The functor

\mathbf{MG}\left(\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{R}^{r}t^{-r}\right)\to{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}\text{-}\mathbf{Mod},\qquad M=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}M_{r}\mapsto M_{0},

is a bi-exact
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence whose quasi-inverse is given by

L\mapsto L\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}}\left(\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}I_{R}^{r}t^{-r}\right),

with the obvious grading. In fact,
[\Spec(\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb{Z}}I^{r}_{R}t^{-r})/\mathbb{G}_{m,{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}_{R}}]
is isomorphic to
\Spec({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})
.

We now give the Rees-algebra-theoretic definition of prismatic
F
-gauges over
R
.

Definition 1.14

(Drinfeld and Bhatt–Lurie)
.

Let
R
be a qrsp ring. A
prismatic
F
-gauge (in vector bundles)
over
R
is a pair
(M,\varphi_{M})
where
M
is an object of
\mathbf{MG}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})))
and
\varphi_{M}
is an isomorphism of
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
-modules
(\sigma^{\ast}M)_{0}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\tau^{\ast}M
.

A
morphism

(M_{1},\varphi_{M_{1}})\to(M_{2},\varphi_{M_{2}})
of prismatic
F
-gauges over
R
is a morphism
f\colon M_{1}\to M_{2}
of graded
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}))
-modules such that
\varphi_{M_{2}}\circ\sigma^{\ast}(f)=\tau^{\ast}(f)\circ\varphi_{M_{1}}
. We denote the category of prismatic
F
-gauges in vector bundles over
R
by
F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(R)
. The category
F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(R)
is an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category with structure essentially inherited from
\mathbf{MG}^{\mathrm{fp}}(\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}))
, but where we decree that

\varphi_{M_{1}\otimes_{\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}))}M_{2}}\vcentcolon=\varphi_{M_{1}}\otimes\varphi_{M_{2}},

(which makes sense as
(\sigma^{\ast}(M_{1}\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}}M_{2}))_{0}\simeq(\sigma^{\ast}M_{1})_{0}\otimes_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}}(\sigma^{\ast}M_{2})_{0}
by Remark
1.13
).

A morphism of qrsp rings
R\to S
gives a map
\mathrm{Rees}(\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}))\to\mathrm{Rees}(\mathrm{Fil}^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S}))
of graded rings compatible with both
\tau
and
\sigma
. Thus, base extension provides an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functor
F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(R)\to F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(S)
. So, if
\mathfrak{X}
is a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme,
F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}
forms a natural prestack on
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qsyn}}
which is a stack by
[
GL23
, Proposition 2.29]
.

Definition 1.15

(Drinfeld, Bhatt–Lurie)
.

For a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}
, the category of
prismatic
F
-gauges (in vector bundles)
over
\mathfrak{X}
is given by the
2
-limit

F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(\mathfrak{X})=\twolim_{\Spf(R)\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}}F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(R),

equipped with the structure of an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category defined term-by-term.

Suppose that
\mathcal{G}
is a smooth group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme. One can then make sense of the category
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathcal{C}
of
\mathcal{G}
-objects in a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category
\mathcal{C}
(e.g., see
[
IKY24
, §A.5]
).

Definition 1.16
.

For a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}
, the category
\mathcal{G}\text{-}F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(\mathfrak{X})
of
prismatic
F
-gauges with
\mathcal{G}
-structure over
\mathfrak{X}
is the category of
\mathcal{G}
-objects in
F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(\mathfrak{X})
.

Prismatic
F
-gauges in terms of formal stacks

We now compare Definition
1.16
to the notion of a prismatic
F
-gauge with
\mathcal{G}
-structure which implicitly appears in
[
Bha23
]
. Throughout this section we fix a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}
.

Attached to
\mathfrak{X}
are the following formal stacks over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
:

•

the
prismatization

\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
as in
[
BL22b
, Construction 7.1]
(cf.
[
Bha23
, Definition 5.1.6]
)
classifying Cartier–Witt divisors (see
[
Bha23
, Definition 5.1.3]
) which is equipped with a Frobenius
F_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}\to\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
(see
[
Bha23
, Remark 5.1.10]
),

•

the
Nygaard filtered prismatization

\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}
as in
[
Bha23
, Definition 5.3.10]
and
[
GM24
, 6.4]
(which classifies filtered Cartier–Witt divisors as in
[
Bha23
, Definition 5.3.1]
) which has a structure map
\pi_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}\to\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
,

•

the
Hodge embedding
and
de Rham embedding

j_{\mathfrak{X},\mathrm{HT}}
and
j_{\mathfrak{X},\mathrm{dR}}
(see loc. cit.) which are open embeddings
\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}
.

One has the equalities
\pi_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ j_{\mathfrak{X},\mathrm{dR}}=\id_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
and
\pi_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ j_{\mathfrak{X},\mathrm{HT}}=F_{\mathfrak{X}}
. Whenever
\mathfrak{X}
is clear from context, we shall omit the decoration of
\mathfrak{X}
on these maps.

As in
[
Bha23
, Definition 6.1.1]
, we define the formal stack
\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, the
syntomification
of
\mathfrak{X}
, and the maps
j_{{{\mathcal{N}}}}
and
j_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
so that the following diagram is cocartesian:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}\sqcup\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{taut.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathrm{HT}}\sqcup j_{\mathrm{dR}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}

If
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
we shorten the notation of these objects to
R^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
,
R^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}
, and
R^{\mathrm{syn}}
. These constructions can be further extended to the case when
R
is a
p
-complete animated ring (see
[
GM24
]
).

The following shows that these formal stacks over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
are more manageable when
\mathfrak{X}
is quasi-syntomic and, in particular, are classical (i.e., don’t have non-trivial derived structure).

Proposition 1.17

(Bhatt–Lurie)
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{X}
is quasi-syntomic. Then, one has a canonical identification of formal stacks over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
:

\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}\simeq\twocolim_{\Spf(R)\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}}\Spf({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}),\qquad\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}=\twocolim_{\Spf(R)\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})).

where for each
R
the topology on
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}
and the completion
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}))
are in terms of the
(p,I_{R})
-adic topology. Moreover, under these identifications
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
and
j_{\mathrm{HT}}
are obtained by taking the colimit over
R
of the maps
\tau
and
\sigma
, respectively.

Proof.

When
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
is qrsp, this follows from
[
BL22b
, Theorem 7.17]
,
[
GM24
, Theorem 6.11.5]
and
[
Bha23
, Remark 5.5.5]
. In general, it suffices to observe that if
\mathcal{S}
is a quasi-syntomic cover over
\mathfrak{X}
with each constituent of the form
\Spf(R)
for a qrsp ring
R
then

\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}=\twocolim_{S\in\mathcal{S}^{\bullet}}S^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}},\qquad\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}=\twocolim_{S\in\mathcal{S}^{\bullet}}S^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}

where
\mathcal{S}^{\bullet}
is the Čech nerve of
\mathcal{S}
in the topos of formal stacks over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, which follows from the covering properties discussed in
[
BL22b
, Proposition 7.5]
and
[
GM24
, Corollary 6.12.8]
(cf.
[
Bha23
, Remark 5.5.18]
). But, as
{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R_{2}}}{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R_{3}}\simeq{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R_{1}\widehat{\otimes}_{R_{2}}R_{3}}
(see
[
ALB23
, Proposition 3.30]
) and a similar tensor-product compatibility
holds for completed Rees stacks, the claim follows.
∎

Combining this with Proposition
1.5
and Lemma
1.12
we obtain the following.

Corollary 1.18
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{X}
is quasi-syntomic and that
\mathcal{G}
is a smooth group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme. Then, there are natural bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalences

F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(\mathfrak{X})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\twolim_{\Spf(R)\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}}\mathbf{Vect}(R^{\mathrm{syn}}).

and natural equivalences

\mathcal{G}\text{-}F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(\mathfrak{X})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\twolim_{\Spf(R)\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}}\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}(R^{\mathrm{syn}})

Due to Corollary
1.18
, we shall use the notation
F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(\mathfrak{X})
(resp.
\mathcal{G}\text{-}F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(\mathfrak{X})
) and
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
(resp.
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
) interchangeably when
\mathfrak{X}
is quasi-syntomic.

Remark 1.19
.

For a (classical) formal stack
\mathcal{X}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
a
vector bundle
on
\mathcal{X}
is a vector bundle on
(\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{fpqc}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
where
\mathcal{X}_{\mathrm{fpqc}}
is as in
[
SP
,
Tag 06NU
]
and
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
is as in
[
SP
,
Tag 06TU
]
. Formally, one has a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence

\mathbf{Vect}(\mathcal{X})\simeq\twolim_{\Spec(R)\to\mathcal{X}}\mathbf{Vect}(R),

where
R
is a (variable)
p
-nilpotent ring (one can also replace this with
\Spf(R)\to\mathcal{X}
where now
\Spf(R)
is an object of
\Spf(\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\mathrm{adic}}_{\mathrm{fl}}
), where the right-hand side is endowed with the term-by-term exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-structure. So one may formally apply
[
IKY24
, Theorem A.18]
to deduce that if
(\Lambda_{0},\mathds{T}_{0})
is a tensor package for
\mathcal{G}
(see
[
IKY24
, §A.5]
) then
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathcal{X})\simeq\mathbf{Twist}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}(\Lambda_{0},\mathds{T}_{0})
. In particular, this applies when
\mathcal{X}=\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}}
for
\mathfrak{X}
a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme.

1.3.
Relationship to prismatic
F
-crystals

We now clarify the relationship between prismatic
F
-crystals and prismatic
F
-gauges on a base formal
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-scheme. We refer the reader to
[
IKY24
, §1.1]
for standard terminology and notation concerning base formal schemes.

Notation 1.20
.

We fix the following notation:

•

k
is a perfect extension of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
,
W\vcentcolon=W(k)
, and
K_{0}\vcentcolon=\mathrm{Frac}(W)
,

•

K
is a finite totally ramified extension of
K_{0}
, with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{K}
and ramification index
e
,

•

\pi
is a uniformizer of
K
and
E=E(u)
in
W[u]
is the minimal polynomial for
\pi
over
K_{0}
,

•

for a formally framed base
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra
R
we set
(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))
to be the Breuil–Kisin prism.

Construction 1.21

(
[
Bha23
, Remark 6.3.4]
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme. Then, there is a natural
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear exact
\otimes
-functor

\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathbf{Perf}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})\to\mathbf{D}^{\varphi}_{\mathrm{perf}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}),\qquad\mathcal{V}\mapsto\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}(\mathcal{V})=(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}),

which we imprecisely call the
forgetful functor
, constructed as follows.

Step 1:
Set
\mathcal{E}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}\vcentcolon=j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{\ast}\mathcal{V}
, which we interpret as an object
\mathcal{E}
of
\mathbf{Perf}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
via
[
BL22b
, Theorem 6.5]
so then
F^{\ast}\mathcal{E}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
corresponds to
\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}
. More explicitly, for an object
(A,I)
of
\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
, one can build a morphism
\rho_{(A,I)}\colon\Spf(A)\to\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
as in
[
BL22b
, Construction 3.10]
and then
\mathcal{E}(A,I)=\rho_{(A,I)}^{\ast}\mathcal{E}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
.

Step 2:
Observe that there are natural morphisms

j_{\mathrm{HT}}^{\ast}\mathcal{V}\leftarrow F^{\ast}\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\to F^{\ast}j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{\ast}\mathcal{V},

where the second map is the pullback along
F
of the natural map
\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\to j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{\ast}\mathcal{V}
coming from the fact that
j_{\mathrm{dR}}
is a section of
\pi
, and the first map is obtained by adjunction from the map
\pi_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\to F_{\ast}j_{\mathrm{HT}}^{\ast}\mathcal{V}
using the fact that
\pi\circ j_{\mathrm{HT}}=F
. By
[
Bha23
, Remark 6.3.4]
, this induces an isomorphism after inverting the invertible ideal
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
(see
[
Bha23
, Construction 5.1.18]
).

Step 3:
By the construction of
\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}}
, we have a canonical identification
j_{\mathrm{HT}}^{\ast}\mathcal{V}\simeq j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{\ast}\mathcal{V}
. Thus, altogether, we get an isomorphism

F^{\ast}\mathcal{E}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}}]\simeq j^{\ast}_{\mathrm{HT}}\mathcal{V}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}}]\simeq j_{\mathrm{dR}}^{\ast}\mathcal{V}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}}]=\mathcal{E}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}}].

which corresponds to an isomorphism
\varphi_{\mathcal{E}}\colon\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}]\to\mathcal{E}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}]
.

Finally, we observe by construction that
\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}
restricts to give a functor

(1.3.1)

\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}).

Remark 1.22
.

When
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
for a qrsp ring
R
, one may understand
\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}
as in (
1.3.1
) as the functor
F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}^{\mathrm{vect}}(R)\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})
explicitly described in
[
Ito23
, Propsition 8.1.9]
.

In
[
GL23
, Corollary 2.31]
(see also
[
Ito23
, Propsition 8.1.9]
), it is shown that
\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is fully faithful. We now wish to describe the essential image when
\mathfrak{X}
is a base formal
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-scheme.

Definition 1.23
.

We make the following definitions.

(1)

For a prism
(A,I)
, and an object
(M,\varphi_{M})
of
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(A,I)
, set

\Fil^{r}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(\phi_{A}^{\ast}M)\vcentcolon=\left\{x\in\phi_{A}^{\ast}M:\varphi_{M}(x)\in I^{r}M\right\},

which defines a filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(\phi_{A}^{\ast}M)
by
A
-submodules, called the
Nygaard filtration
.

(2)

For a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}
and an object
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
of
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
, we define the filtration
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E})\subseteq\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}
by
\mathcal{O}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
-submodules, called the
Nygaard filtration
, so that
\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E})(A,I)=\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}(A,I))
, functorially in an object
(A,I)
of
\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
.
7
7

7

See
[
IKY25
, Remark 1.13]
for a remark about the terminology ‘Nygaard filtration’.

By design
(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}))
is a filtered module over
(\mathcal{O}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}})
.

Definition 1.24
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme. We call a prismatic
F
-crystal
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
on
\mathfrak{X}

locally filtered free (lff)
if
(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}))
is lff over
(\mathcal{O}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}})
.

Remark 1.25
.

Note that
(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{E}))
is, a priori, only a filtered module over
(\mathcal{O}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}})
and not a filtered crystal. But, in the lff case this is true (see
[
Ito23
, Proposition 3.1.13]
).

Denote the full subcategory of
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
consisting of lff objects by
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
. It is stable under tensor products, and so it inherits the structure of an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-category.

Example 1.26
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a base formal
W
-scheme. If
\omega
belongs to
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mu}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
for a cocharacter
\mu\colon\mathbb{G}_{m,W}\to\mathcal{G}_{W}
(see
[
IKY24
, Definition 3.12]
or Definition
1.36
below), then
\omega(\Lambda)
belongs to
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
for all objects
\Lambda
of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})
, as can be easily checked by hand.

Proposition 1.27
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme, and
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
a prismatic
F
-crystal on
\mathfrak{X}
. Then, the following are equivalent:

(1)

the prismatic
F
-crystal
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
is lff,

(2)

for any object
(A,I)
of
\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
, the filtered module
(\phi^{*}\mathcal{E}(A,I),\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(\phi^{*}\mathcal{E})(A,I))
is lff over the filtered ring
(A,\Fil_{I}^{\bullet})
.

If
\mathfrak{X}
is a base formal
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-scheme, the above conditions are additionally equivalent to the following:

(3)

there exists an open cover
\{\Spf(R_{i})\}
of
\mathfrak{X}
with each
R_{i}
a base (formally framed)
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra, such that if
\mathfrak{M}_{i}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{E}(\mathfrak{S}_{R_{i}},(E))
then
(\phi^{\ast}\mathfrak{M}_{i},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(\phi^{\ast}\mathfrak{M}_{i}))
is lff over
(\mathfrak{S}_{R_{i}},\Fil^{\bullet}_{E})
,

(4)

there exists a quasi-syntomic cover
\{\Spf(S_{i})\to\mathfrak{X}\}
with each
S_{i}
qrsp, such that if
\mathcal{M}_{i}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{E}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{i}},I_{S_{i}})
then
(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{M}_{i},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}(\phi^{\ast}\mathcal{M}_{i}))
is lff over
({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{S_{i}},\Fil^{\bullet}_{I_{S_{i}}})
.

Proof.

Clearly (2) implies (1). For the converse it suffices to show that the lff condition is flat local on an object of
\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
. This follows from the fact that for a
(p,I)
-adically faithfully flat map of prisms
(A,I)\to(B,IB)
the natural map
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet}A)\to\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{IB}^{\bullet}B)
is
(p,I)
-adically faithfully flat. Indeed, the natural map
I^{n}\otimes_{A}B\to I^{n}B
is an isomorphism as
IB
is an invertible ideal, and hence
\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet}A)\otimes_{A}B\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{IB}B)
. The final claims concerning base formal
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-schemes follows from
[
IKY24
, Propositions 1.11 and 1.16]
.
∎

We now come to the precise relationship between prismatic
F
-crystals and prismatic
F
-gauges (compare with
[
Ito23
, Corollary 8.2.13]
). In the following, let
\mathcal{G}
be a smooth group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme.

Proposition 1.28
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme. Then the essential image of
\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is contained in
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
.
If
\mathfrak{X}
is a base formal
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-scheme, then
\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}

induces a bi-exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-equivalence

\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}).

In particular,
\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}
induces an equivalence

\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}).

Proof.

Let
\Pi_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})\to\mathbf{Perf}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
denote the functor from
[
GL23
, Theorem 2.31]
.
8
8

8

While this functor is only constructed in loc. cit. when
\mathfrak{X}
is smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, the construction goes through, mutatis mutandis, for
\mathfrak{X}
a base scheme using the cover from
[
IKY24
, Lemma 1.15]
.

Let
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
be an object of
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
. We first prove the following claim.

Claim 1.29
.

The object
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}\vcentcolon=\Pi_{\mathfrak{X}}(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
is in
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
if and only if
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
is in
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
.

Proof of Claim
1.29
.

For each perfectoid ring
S
with a map
\Spf(S)\to\mathfrak{X}
, consider the conditions:

(1)

the restriction
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}|_{S^{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}
is a vector bundle on
S^{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}
,

(2)

\Fil_{\mathrm{Nyg}}^{\bullet}(\phi^{*}\mathcal{E}(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(S)))
is lff over
(\mathrm{A}_{\mathrm{inf}}(S),\Fil^{\bullet}_{\tilde{\xi}})
(where
\tilde{\xi}
is as in
[
IKY24
, §1.1.1]
).

Then
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
being in
