\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
is equivalent to (1) being satisfied for any such
S
by
[
IKY24
, Proposition 1.11 and Lemma 1.15]
and
[
Bha23
, Remark 5.5.18]
. On the other hand by Proposition
1.27
,
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
being lff is equivalent to (2) being satisfied for any such
S
. By Proposition
1.6
, conditions (1) and (2) are equivalent, as desired.
∎

This together with the construction of
\Pi_{\mathfrak{X}}
and
[
GL23
, Proposition 2.52]
gives an isomorphism

(1.3.2)

\displaystyle(\Pi_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}})(\mathcal{F})\simeq\mathcal{F}

for any
\mathcal{F}
an object of
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
. Thus,
\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}
induces a functor
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
.
Again by Claim
1.29
, the functor
\Pi_{\mathfrak{X}}
induces a functor
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})\to\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
. These functors are quasi-inverse to each other: for an object
\mathcal{F}
in
\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
, we have the isomorphism (
1.3.2
); on the other hand, for an object
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
of
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
, we have a functorial isomorphism
(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})\simeq(R_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ\Pi_{\mathfrak{X}})(\mathcal{E},\varphi_{\mathcal{E}})
by the constructions of
\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}
and
\Pi_{\mathfrak{X}}
. This proves the claim that
R_{\mathfrak{X}}
is an equivalence, and its bi-exactness is
[
IKY25
, Proposition 2.17]
.
∎

1.4.
Prismatic
F
-gauges with
\mathcal{G}
-structure of type
\mu

In this subsection, we introduce the notion of prismatic
F
-gauge with
\mathcal{G}
-structure of type
\mu
.

Convention 1.30
.

We make the following three conventions throughout this section.

(1)

The

tautological line bundle

\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}\{1\}
on
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
corresponds to
the trivial line bundle on
\Spec(\mathbb{Z})
with the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action given by the
inverse
of the natural scalar multiplication of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
So
\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{{m}}}\{1\}
corresponds to the graded line bundle
\mathbb{Z}(-1)
concentrated in degree
-1
.
9
9

9

This convention agrees with
[
Bha23
, Construction 2.2.1]
and
[
GM24
]
, but is opposite to that in
[
Ito23
]
.

(2)

We normalize the isomorphism
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Pic}
of stacks via
\id_{B\mathbb{G}_{{m}}}\mapsto\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{{m}}}\{1\}
.

10
10

10

Here
\mathbf{Pic}
is the Picard stack, denoted by
\mathcal{P}ic_{\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}}
in
[
SP
,
Tag 0372
]
.
So
L
in
\mathbf{Pic}(\Spec(S))
corresponds to the natural map
\Spec(S)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\Spec(\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}L^{\otimes i}t^{i})/\mathbb{G}_{{m}}\to B\mathbb{G}_{{m}}
, where
t
sits in degree
1
.

(3)

We identify
B\mathbb{G}_{{m}}
and the the moduli stack of
\mathbb{G}_{{m}}
-torsors in the standard way.

Notation 1.31
.

In addition to Notation
1.20
we fix the following notation:

•

\mathcal{G}
is a smooth affine group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme,

•

\mu\colon\mathbb{G}_{m,W}\to\mathcal{G}_{W}
is a
1
-bounded cocharacter (see
[
Lau21
, Definition 6.3.1]
),

•

for a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on a stack
\mathscr{X}
together with a map
a\colon\mathscr{X}\to\Spf(W)
, we denote the induced
\mathcal{G}
-torsor on
\mathscr{X}
via
\mu
by
\mu_{*}(\mathcal{L},a)
(we omit the
a
if it is clear from context or write
\mathrm{can.}
to emphasize that it is the structure map which is canonical in the given context),

•

for
b\colon\mathcal{L}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
we denote the induced isomorphism by
\mu_{\ast}(b)\colon\mu_{*}(\mathcal{L},a)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mu_{*}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime},a)
.

Additionally, we recall the following standard notions of Breuil–Kisin twists on a bounded
p
-adic formal scheme
\mathfrak{X}
. Denote by
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\{1\}
the pullback of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\{1\}
from
[
Dri24a
, Definition 4.9.4]
(cf.
[
BL22a
, Construction 2.2.11]
and
[
Bha23
, Remark 5.1.19]
) along
\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
. Similarly, let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}\{1\}
be the pullback to
\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}\{1\}\vcentcolon=\pi^{*}\mathcal{O}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}\{1\}\otimes t^{*}\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{{m}}}\{-1\}
from
[
Bha23
, Remark 5.5.15]
.
11
11

11

The definition of
\mathcal{O}_{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}\{1\}
agrees with that in
[
GM24
]
:
\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{{m}}
is formed using the
inverse
of the natural action so the degree of the Rees variable
t
is
-1
in
[
GM24
]
, and
\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{{m}}}\{1\}
corresponds to
\mathbb{Z}(1)
; while we use the natural action so here
\deg(t)=1
, and
\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{{m}}}\{1\}
corresponds to
\mathbb{Z}(-1)
, as in
[
Bha23
]
.

If
\mathfrak{X}
is clear from context we shorten
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\{1\}
and
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}\{1\}
to
\mathcal{O}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}\{1\}
and
\mathcal{O}_{{{\mathcal{N}}}}\{1\}
, respectively.

Let notation be as in Notation
1.31
. We define

\mathcal{P}_{\mu}\vcentcolon=\mu_{\ast}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{{m}}}\{1\}),

a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor on
B\mathbb{G}_{m,W}
. Equivalently,
\mathcal{P}_{\mu}
corresponds to the trivial
\mathcal{G}
-torsor on
\Spec(W)
with the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m,W}=\Spec(W[z^{\pm 1}])
given by left multiplication by
\mu(z)^{-1}
in
\mathcal{G}(W[z^{\pm 1}])
.
Denote by
\mu^{(-1)}
the base change of
\mu
along
\phi_{W}^{-1}\colon W\to W
and by

\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}
the pullback of
\mathcal{P}_{\mu^{(-1)}}
by the map
W^{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,W}
that corresponds to the Breuil–Kisin twist
\mathcal{O}_{W^{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}\{1\}
.

Definition 1.32
.

Fix an
n
in
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
. Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a bounded
p
-adic formal
W
-scheme. Write
\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{n}\vcentcolon=\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
, and similarly for
\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}_{n}
.

(1)

An
n
-truncated prismatic
F
-gauge with
\mathcal{G}
-structure
on
\mathfrak{X}
is a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
\mathcal{F}
on the (derived) formal stack
\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{n}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(giving
\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}}
if
n=\infty
, whence we drop the prefix ‘
\infty
-truncated’).

(2)

An
n
-truncated prismatic
F
-gauge with
\mathcal{G}
-structure
\mathcal{F}
is
of type
\mu
if the restriction to
\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}_{n}
(again giving
\mathfrak{X}^{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}
itself when
n=\infty
) is of type
\mu
, i.e., flat locally (equiv. quasi-syntomically locally) on
\mathfrak{X}
, the restriction
\mathcal{F}|_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}_{n}}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}|_{\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}_{n}}
.

Denote by
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G},n}^{\mu}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
the
\infty
-groupoid of
n
-truncated prismatic
F
-gauges with
\mathcal{G}
-structure of type
\mu
, a full
\infty
-subgroupoid of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}}_{n})
. If
n=\infty
we drop it from the notation.
12
12

12

When
\mathfrak{X}
is quasi-syntomic, observe that
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
is a groupoid.

Remark 1.33
.

The Frobenius twist in the definition of
\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}
is necessary for
\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}
to have filtration of type
\mu
(cf. Proposition
1.39
).

Remark 1.34
.

Similarly to Remark
1.19
, if
\mathfrak{X}
is quasi-syntomic, one may identify
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
with
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
, and consequently
\mathbf{Tors}^{\mu}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
may be identified with a full subcategory of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
which we denote by
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\mu}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
. In particular, if
\mathfrak{X}
is quasi-syntomic, combining Proposition
1.17
and Corollary
1.18
we have natural identifications

\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})\simeq\twolim_{\Spf(R)\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}}\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}({R}^{\mathrm{syn}}),\quad\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}^{{{\mathcal{N}}}})\simeq\twolim_{\Spf(R)\in\mathfrak{X}_{\mathrm{qrsp}}}\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{Nyg}}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}))).

We first compare the notion of
n
-truncated prismatic
F
-gauges with
\mathcal{G}
-structure (of type
\mu
) to the notion of prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-torsors with
F
-structure of type
\mu
as in
[
IKY24
, Definition 3.12]
, which we now recall using a slightly different presentation.

Let
(A,\Fil^{\bullet})
be a filtered ring. Then, we have the natural closed immersion

\iota\colon B\mathbb{G}_{{m}}\times\Spec(A/\Fil^{1})\to\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}),

defined by the surjection of graded rings

\displaystyle\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet})\to\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathrm{triv}}(A/\Fil^{1}))=A/\Fil^{1}[t]\xrightarrow{t\mapsto 0}A/\Fil^{1}.

Definition 1.35
.

We define the
punctured Rees stack

\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil^{\bullet})
for
(A,\Fil^{\bullet})
to be the open substack of the Rees stack
\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet})
given as the complement of the above closed embedding
\iota
.

In the situation that
\Fil^{\bullet}=\Fil_{I}^{\bullet}
for some invertible ideal
I
, the punctured Rees stack
\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{I})
is also obtained as follows. Consider the open embeddings

\Spec(A)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\left[\Spec\left(\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}I^{-i}t^{i}\right)/\mathbb{G}_{{m}}\right]\to\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{I}),\quad\Spec(A)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\{t\neq 0\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{I}).

These two open embeddings
induce an isomorphism

\displaystyle\Spec(A)\sqcup_{\Spec(A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}])}\Spec(A)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet}).

In particular, we obtain a
2
-functorial identification of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{I}))
with the category of triples
(Q,P,\psi\colon Q[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}P[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}])
where
Q
and
P
are
\mathcal{G}
-torsors on
A
and
\psi
is an isomorphism of
\mathcal{G}
-torsors on
A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]
, with the obvious notion of morphisms. We fix the direction of the isomorphism
\psi
so that the restriction of such a triple to
\{t\neq 0\}
gives the
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
P
.

Definition 1.36
.

Let
(A,I)
be an object of
W_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
(in particular,
A
is naturally a
W
-algebra) and
\mu\colon\mathbb{G}_{m,W}\to\mathcal{G}_{W}
be a cocharacter.

(1)

We say a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor on
\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet})
presented as
(Q,P,\psi\colon Q[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}P[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}])
is
of type

\mu
if there exists a
(p,I)
-adically faithfully flat cover
A\to A^{\prime}
such that
IA^{\prime}
is principal, and there exists trivializations
\theta\colon\mathcal{G}_{A^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}Q
and
\theta^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{G}_{A^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}P
such that the isomorphism
\theta^{\prime-1}\circ\psi\circ\theta
is given by left multiplication by
\mu(d)
for a generator
d
of
IA^{\prime}
.

(2)

We say a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
\mathcal{P}
on
\mathcal{R}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet})
is
of type

\mu
if there exists a
(p,I)
-adically flat cover
A\to A^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{P}
restricted to
\mathcal{R}(\Fil_{IA^{\prime}}^{\bullet})
is isomorphic to the
\mu
-typical
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
, i.e., the pullback of
\mathcal{P}_{\mu}
along the natural map
\mathcal{R}(\Fil_{IA^{\prime}}^{\bullet})\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,W}
.

We denote these categories by
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mu}(\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{I}))
and
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mu}(\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{I}))
, respectively.

Lemma 1.37

(cf.
[
Bha23
, Proposition 6.6.3]
)
.

Let
(A,I)
be a prism.

(1)

Restriction along
j\colon\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet})\to\mathcal{R}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet})
induces a fully faithful functor

\displaystyle j^{*}\colon\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{R}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet}))\to\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet})).

Fix a
W
-structure on
(A,I)
(i.e., let it be an object of
W_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
), and a cocharacter
\mu\colon\mathbb{G}_{m,W}\to\mathcal{G}_{W}
.

(2)

The functor from (1) induces an equivalence

(1.4.1)

\displaystyle\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mu}(\mathcal{R}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet}))\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mu}(\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet})).

Proof.

To prove Claim (1), we observe that by the Tannakian formalism it suffices to prove the claim for
\mathcal{G}=\GL_{n,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
. Then it suffices to prove that

j^{\ast}\colon\mathbf{Vect}(\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{I}))\to\mathbf{Vect}(\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{I})),

is fully faithful. Using
[
SP
,
Tag 06WT
]
, we are reduced to showing that pullback along

\{t\neq 0\}\cup\Spec\left(\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}I^{-i}t^{i}\right)\to\Spec(\mathrm{Rees}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{I}))

induces a fully faithful functor between the categories of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant vector bundles on source and target. But, the conditions of
[
ČS24
, Lemma 7.2.7]
are satisfied, and so the claim follows by applying loc. cit. with
Y=\mathcal{H}om(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{V}^{\prime})
for vector bundles
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
on
\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{I})
.

For Claim (2), first observe that
\mathcal{P}_{\mu}|_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{I})}
is of type
\mu
: using notation
1.31
, it is presented as

(\mu_{*}(I,\mathrm{can.}),\mu_{*}(A,\mathrm{can.}),\psi\vcentcolon=\mu_{*}(\id\colon I\cdot A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}])).

Replacing
A
by a Zariski cover on which
I
becomes principal, we may pick a generator
d
of
I
. Then, with the tautological trivialization
\theta\colon\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mu_{*}(A,\mathrm{can.})
and the trivialization

\theta^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{G}=\mu_{*}(A,\mathrm{can.})\xrightarrow{\mu_{*}(d\cdot)}\mu_{*}(I,\mathrm{can.})

determined by
d
, the isomorphism
{\theta^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ\psi\circ\theta
is given by
\mu(d)
.
Thus, the functor
j^{*}
does indeed carry objects of type
\mu
to those of type
\mu
. We then only have to show that (
1.4.1
) is essentially surjective. Let
\mathcal{P}^{\circ}=(Q,P,\psi\colon Q[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}P[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}])
be an object of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mu}(\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet}))
.
By definition, there exists a
(p,I)
-adically faithfully flat cover
A\to A^{\prime}
such that
IA^{\prime}
is principal and there exist trivializations
\theta\colon\mathcal{G}_{A^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}Q_{A^{\prime}}
and
\theta^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{G}_{A^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}P_{A^{\prime}}
such that
\theta^{\prime-1}\circ\psi\circ\theta
is given by left multiplication of
\mu(d)
for a generator
d
of
IA^{\prime}
. We consider the
\mu
-typical
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{P}_{\mu,\mathcal{R}(\Fil_{IA^{\prime}}^{\bullet})}
on
\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{IA^{\prime}})
. Note
\theta
and
\theta^{\prime}
induce an isomorphism
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}|_{\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil_{IA^{\prime}}^{\bullet})}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{A^{\prime}}
. Then the descent datum

\sigma\colon\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A^{\prime},\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}A^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{A^{\prime}}\otimes_{A^{\prime},\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}}(A^{\prime}\otimes_{A}A^{\prime})

for
\mathcal{P}^{\circ}
along
A\to A^{\prime}
induces, by the full faithfulness from Claim (1), a descent datum on
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
, which then gives a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
\mathcal{P}
on
\mathcal{R}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet})
such that
j^{\ast}\mathcal{P}\simeq\mathcal{P}^{\circ}
as desired.
∎

To relate the above consideration to the type
\mu
condition for prismatic
F
-crystals, we consider the two
A
-lattices associated to an object of
\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(A,I)
. More precisely, we consider the functor

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lat}_{\mathcal{G}}\colon\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi}(A,I)\to\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet}))

by sending
(\mathcal{P},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})
to
(\phi^{*}\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}\colon\phi^{*}\mathcal{P}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{P}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}])
.

Definition 1.38

(cf.
[
IKY24
, Definition 3.12]
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme and
(\mathcal{P},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})
be an object of
\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
. We say that
(\mathcal{P},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})
is
of type
\mu
if for any object
(A,I)
of the site
\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
, the object
\mathrm{Lat}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{P},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})
of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil_{I}^{\bullet}))
is of type
\mu
. Denote the full subcategory of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
of objects of type
\mu
by
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi,\mu}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme over
W
and consider the forgetful functor

\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})\to\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}),

from Construction
1.21
, which can also be interpreted in terms of
\mathcal{G}
-objects as in Remark
1.34
. The result below says this induces an equivalence on objects of type
\mu
if
\mathfrak{X}
is sufficiently regular.

Proposition 1.39
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme over
W
.

(1)

Let
\mathcal{P}
be an object of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})
of type
\mu
. Then the associated object
(\mathcal{P}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}})=\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}(\mathcal{P})
of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
is also of type
\mu
.

(2)

Assume that
\mathfrak{X}
is either (a)
\Spf(R)
for a perfectoid ring
R
or (b) a base formal scheme over
\Spf(W)
. Then the forgetful functor induces an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}\colon\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mu}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi,\mu}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}).

Proof.

For Claim (1), by the definitions of the type
\mu
conditions,
we may assume that
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
for a qrsp ring
R
. We consider the following commutative diagram of categories

\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(R^{\mathrm{syn}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{P}\mapsto\phi^{*}\mathcal{P}_{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R},I_{R})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Lat}_{\mathcal{G}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{R}(\Fil_{I_{R}}^{\bullet}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{R}^{\circ}(\Fil_{I_{R}}^{\bullet})),}

where the left vertical arrow is the natural pullback functor along the composition

\mathcal{R}(\Fil^{\bullet}_{I_{R}})\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathcal{R}(\Fil_{\mathrm{Nyg}}^{\bullet})=R^{{{\mathcal{N}}}}\to R^{\mathrm{syn}}.

Recall that
\mathcal{P}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
being of type
\mu
means that
\mathrm{Lat}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathcal{P}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
is of type
\mu
, which, by the commutative diagram, happens if and only if
j^{*}\phi^{*}\mathcal{P}_{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}
is of type
\mu
. Now, by Lemma
1.37
, this is equivalent to
\phi^{*}\mathcal{P}_{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}
being of type
\mu
. On the other hand,
\mathcal{P}
being of type
\mu
means that
\mathcal{P}_{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}
is of type
\mu
.
As scalar extension preserves the type
\mu
condition, Claim (1) follows.

To prove Claim (2), observe that as in Example
1.26
any object of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi,\mu}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
corresponds to an object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
, and so, by Proposition
1.28
and its proof, we only have to show that the converse of Claim (1) also holds under the assumption of Claim (2). We may now assume that
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
for a perfectoid ring
R
(in case (b), take a perfectoid cover as in
[
IKY24
, Lemma 1.15]
). Then the assertion follows since
\phi
is now an isomorphism.
∎

1.5.
Relationship to the theory of displays

To utilize the works
[
Ito23
]
and
[
Ito25
]
, we need to compare the notion of prismatic
F
-gauge with
\mathcal{G}
-structure of type
\mu
to that of prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-
F
-gauge of type
\mu
introduced by Ito in
[
Ito23
, Definition 8.2.5]
. We maintain the notation from Notation
1.31
throughout.

1.5.1.
Prismatic
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mu
-displays

Let
R
be a quasi-syntomic
W
-algebra. Following
[
Ito23
]
, for an object
(A,I)
of
R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
, and a generator
d
of
I
, we define the sheaf

\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(A,I)}\colon\Spec(A)_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\to\mathbf{Grp},\qquad B\mapsto\left\{g\in\mathcal{G}(B):\mu(d)g\mu(d)^{-1}\in\mathcal{G}(B)\subseteq\mathcal{G}(B[\nicefrac{{1}}{{d}}])\right\},

which does not depend on
d
. For
d
generating
I
, define an action of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(A,I)}
on
\mathcal{G}_{d}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{G}
by

\mathcal{G}_{d}\times\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(A,I)}\to\mathcal{G}_{d},\qquad(x,g)\mapsto g^{-1}x\phi(\mu(d)g\mu(d)^{-1}).

For another generator
d^{\prime}
of
I
there exists a unique unit
u
of
A
with
d=ud^{\prime}
and the morphism
\mathcal{G}_{d}\to\mathcal{G}_{d^{\prime}}
given by sending
x
to
x\phi(\mu(u))
is a
\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(A,I)}
-equivariant isomorphism of sheaves. Thus,
\mathcal{G}_{{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}},(A,I)}\vcentcolon=\varprojlim\mathcal{G}_{d}
, is a sheaf of sets carrying a canonical action of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(A,I)}
.

As in
[
Ito23
]
, a
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mu
-display
on
(A,I)
is a pair
(\mathcal{Q}_{(A,I)},\alpha_{\mathcal{Q}_{(A,I)}})
where
\mathcal{Q}_{(A,I)}
is a
\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(A,I)}
-torsor and
\alpha_{\mathcal{Q}_{(A,I)}}\colon\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{G}_{{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}},(A,I)}
is a
\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(A,I)}
-equivariant map of sheaves. There is an evident notion of morphism of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mu
-displays on
(A,I)
, and we denote by
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Disp}_{\mu}(A,I)
the category of prismatic
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mu
-displays on
(A,I)
. For a morphism
(A,I)\to(B,J)
, where both
I
and
J
are principal, there is an obvious pullback morphism
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Disp}_{\mu}(A,I)\to\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Disp}_{\mu}(B,J)
and Ito defines a
prismatic
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mu
-display
on
R
to be an object
(\mathcal{Q},\alpha_{\mathcal{Q}})
of the category

\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Disp}_{\mu}(R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})\vcentcolon=\twolim_{\scriptscriptstyle(A,I)\in R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Disp}_{\mu}(A,I),

which makes sense as every object
(A,I)
of
R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
has a cover
(A,I)\to(B,J)
where
J
is principal.

This definition extends, mutatis mutandis, to a quasi-syntomic formal scheme, and we denote the resulting category by
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Disp}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
.

1.5.2.
Relationship to prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-torsors with
F
-structure

We now show that
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mu
-displays are precisely the prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-torsors with
F
-structure bounded by
\mu
. More precisely, we construct

\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Disp}_{\mu}(A,(d))\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi,\mu}(A,(d)),

functorial in an object
(A,(d))
of
R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
.

We first construct an equivalence between the category of banal (see
[
Ito23
, §5.1]
)
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mu
-displays on
(A,(d))
and the full-subcategory of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi,\mu}(A,(d))
consisting of those
(\mathcal{A},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}})
with
\mathcal{A}
trivializable. Let
(\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(A,I)},X\colon\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(A,I)}\to\mathcal{G}_{{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}},(A,I)})
be a banal
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mu
-display on
(A,I)
.
Choose a generator
d
of
I
, and write
X_{d}
in
\mathcal{G}(A)
for the
d
-component of
X(1)
. Define
\mathcal{P}_{X,d}
to be the trivial
\mathcal{G}_{A}
-torsor and
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}_{X,d}}
to be the composition

\phi^{*}\mathcal{G}_{A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{d}}]}=\mathcal{G}_{A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{d}}]}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{G}_{A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{d}}]}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{G}_{A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{d}}]},

where the first map is left multiplication by
X_{d}
and the second map is left multiplication by
\mu(d)
. For another generator
d^{\prime}
of
I
with
d=ud^{\prime}
with
u\in A
a unit, the left multiplication by
\mu(u)
defines an isomorphism
(\mathcal{P}_{X,d^{\prime}},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}_{X,d^{\prime}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}(\mathcal{P}_{X,d},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}_{X},d})
. Define
(\mathcal{P}_{X},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}_{X}})
to be the inverse limit
\varprojlim_{d}(\mathcal{P}_{X,d},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}_{X,d}})
with transition maps given by
\mu(u)
.

The pair
(\mathcal{P}_{X},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}_{X}})
is then seen to be an object of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi,\mu}(A,(d))
whose underlying
\mathcal{G}
-torsor is trivalizable. This defines a functor as for an element
g
of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu,(A,(d))}
, we have an induced morphism
(\mu(d)g\mu(d)^{-1})_{d}\colon\mathcal{P}_{X\cdot g}\to\mathcal{P}_{X}
, which is functorial and preserves the Frobenius structures by construction. This functor is clearly fully faithful.

Stackifying this association gives us a functor

\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Disp}_{\mu}(A,(d))\to\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi,\mu}(A,(d)),

which is compatible in
\mathcal{G}
and
(A,(d))
. So, for a quasi-syntomic
W
-algebra
R
, we obtain a functor

(1.5.1)

\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Disp}_{\mu}(R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})\to\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi,\mu}(R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}),

functorial in
\mathcal{G}
and
R
.

Proposition 1.40
.

The functor (
1.5.1
) defines an equivalence of categories

\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Disp}_{\mu}(R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi,\mu}(R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})

functorial in
\mathcal{G}
and
R
.

Proof.

As we have already observed, this functor is fully faithful, it remains to show that it is essentially surjective. As both the source and target are stacks on
\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
, this fully faithfulness allows us to reduce ourselves to showing that the functor is essentially surjective on banal objects over some
(A,(d))
. Let
(\mathcal{P},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})
be an object of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi,\mu}(A,(d))

with
\mathcal{P}
trivializable. Then by definition,
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}
is defined by
Y\mu(d)X
for some
X
and
Y
in
\mathcal{G}(A)
. But left multiplication by
Y
then defines an isomorphism
\mathcal{P}_{X\phi(Y),d}\to\mathcal{P}
in
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi,\mu}(A,(d))
,
from where the claim follows.
∎

Remark 1.42

\textstyle{\Aut(\tau^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Proof.

Claim (1) follows from the definition of the weight decomposition. Indeed, the natural map

Proof of Proposition
1.43
.

By Proposition
1.47
and quasi-syntomic descent, it suffices to construct an equivalence

\mathcal{P}

\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}
. Suppose

. Consider the element

\mathcal{A}(R)
that defines

via

. We then define a choice-free prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-

of type
