\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}
-torsor on
R_{\mathrm{qrsp}}
. Further define a
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}
-equivariant map
\alpha_{\mathscr{Q}}\colon\mathscr{Q}=\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}\to\mathcal{A}
by sending
1
to
X
. By the definition of the action of
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}(R)
on
\mathcal{A}(R)
, the object
(\mathscr{Q},\alpha_{\mathscr{Q}})
is independent of the choice of
\theta
in the sense that, if we pick another isomorphism
\theta^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{P}_{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{\mathscr{Q}}
denotes the similarly defined map, then
{\theta^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ\theta
, viewed as an element of
\mathcal{G}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}^{\prime}(R)
via Lemma
1.45
, gives an isomorphism
(\mathscr{Q},\alpha_{\mathscr{Q}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}(\mathscr{Q},\alpha^{\prime}_{\mathscr{Q}})
.
One may check this defines an equivalence
\mathbf{Tors}^{\mu}_{\mathcal{G}}(R^{\mathrm{syn}})_{\mathrm{banal}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{G}\mathchar 45F\mathchar 45\mathbf{Gauge}^{\prime}_{\mu}(R)_{\mathrm{banal}}
. This equivalence is
2
-functorial in
R
, and so we obtain the desired equivalence passing to the quasi-syntomic stackifications.
∎

1.5.5.
Summary of equivalences

We summarize the above results as follows.

Proposition 1.48
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}
be a quasi-syntomic
p
-adic formal scheme.

(1)

There exists a commutative diagram of categories

(1.5.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\mu}(\mathfrak{X}^{\mathrm{syn}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}\text{-}F\text{-}\mathbf{Gauge}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi,\mu}_{\mathcal{G}}(\mathfrak{X}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Disp}_{\mu}(\mathfrak{X})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
1.43
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{R}_{\mathfrak{X}}^{\mathrm{disp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
1.40

2
-bi-functorial in
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathfrak{X}
, where the horizontal arrows are equivalences and the vertical arrows are fully faithful.

(2)

Assume further that
\mathfrak{X}
is either, (a)
\Spf(R)
for a perfectoid ring
R
or, (b) a base formal
W
-scheme. Then the vertical arrows in (
1.5.2
) are equivalences.

Proof.

The only remaining part which is potentially unclear is the
2
-commutativity of (
1.5.2
). It suffices to work with objects
\mathcal{P}=(\mathcal{P}_{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}},\varphi_{\mathcal{P}})
over
\mathfrak{X}=\Spf(R)
with
R
a qrsp ring admitting a map from
\mathcal{O}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle\mu_{p^{\infty}}\rangle
and which are banal, i.e., there exists an isomorphism
\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{P}_{{{\mathcal{N}}}}
.
Choose such an isomorphism
\theta\colon\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{P}_{{{\mathcal{N}}}}
and let
X_{\theta}
denote the element of
\mathrm{Isom}(\sigma^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}},\tau^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}})
that defines
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}
via
\theta
.
Then the Frobenius structure of the associated prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-torsor with
F
-structure is, via
\theta
given by the composition

\phi^{*}\tau^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]\xrightarrow{\mathrm{can.}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]\xrightarrow{X_{\theta}}\tau^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}].

Choose a generator
\pi
of the ideal
L_{R}
and set
d_{\pi}\vcentcolon=\phi(\pi)/\pi
. Then, via the identification

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}}=\mu_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})\xrightarrow{\mu^{\prime}_{*}(\pi\cdot)}\mu_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}\{1\})=\tau^{*}\mathcal{P}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}},

the above composition is identified with the composition

\phi^{*}\mathcal{G}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]\simeq\mathcal{G}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]\xrightarrow{\mu(d_{\pi})}\mathcal{G}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}]\xrightarrow{X_{\theta}^{\pi}}\mathcal{G}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{I}}],

where
X_{\theta}^{\pi}\vcentcolon=\mu^{\prime}(\pi)^{-1}X_{\theta}\mu(\pi)
.

On the other hand, the associated object
(\mathscr{Q},\alpha_{\mathscr{Q}})
in
\mathcal{G}\mathchar 45F\mathchar 45\mathbf{Gauge}_{\mu}(R)
is canonically isomorphic to
(\mathcal{G}_{\mu,{{{{\mathcal{N}}}}}}\to\mathcal{G}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}):1\mapsto X^{\pi}_{\theta})
, and hence the associated object of
\mathcal{G}\mathchar 45\mathbf{Disp}_{\mu}(R)
is given by
(\mathcal{G}_{\mu,{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\to\mathcal{G}({{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R})\colon 1\mapsto\phi(X_{\theta}^{\pi}))
, whose associated prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-torsor is canonically isomorphic to
(\mathcal{G}_{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}_{R}},\varphi)
where
\varphi
corresponds to left multiplication by
\mu(d_{\pi})\phi(X_{\theta}^{\pi})
.
Left multiplication by
X_{\theta}^{\pi}
on
\mathcal{G}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
gives the desired commutativity isomorphism.
∎

2.
Prismatic realization functors on Shimura varieties of abelian type

In this section we construct an object
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
, called the
prismatic realization functor
, where
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is the integral canonical model of a Shimura variety of abelian type, and show it can be upgraded to a
syntomic realization functor

\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{\mathrm{syn}}}
in
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})^{\mathrm{syn}})
.

2.1.
Notation and basic definitions

Throughout this section, we fix the following.

Notation 2.1
.

Define the following notation:

\diamond

\mathbf{G}
is a reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
,

\diamond

\mathbf{Z}
denotes the center
Z(\mathbf{G})
of
\mathbf{G}
,

\diamond

\mathbb{S}\vcentcolon=\mathrm{Res}_{\mathbb{C}/\mathbb{R}}\,\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}
is the Deligne torus,

\diamond

(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is a Shimura datum (see
[
Mil05
, Definition 5.5]
),

\diamond

\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\subseteq\mathbb{C}
denotes the reflex field of
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
(see
[
Mil05
, Definition 12.2]
),

\diamond

\mathsf{K}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is a (variable) neat (cf.
[
Mil05
, p. 288]
) compact open subgroup.

As in
[
Del79
]
(cf.
[
Moo98
]
), associated to this data is the (canonical model of the) Shimura variety
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
, which is a smooth and quasi-projective
\mathbf{E}
-scheme. For
\mathsf{K}
and
\mathsf{K}^{\prime}
of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, and
g
in
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that
g^{-1}\mathsf{K}g\subseteq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}
, denote by
t_{\mathsf{K},\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}(g)
the unique finite étale morphism of
\mathbf{E}
-schemes
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
given on
\mathbb{C}
-points by

t_{\mathsf{K},\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}(g)\left(\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{Q})(x,g^{\prime})\mathsf{K}\right)=\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{Q})(x,g^{\prime}g)\mathsf{K}^{\prime}.

We shorten
t_{\mathsf{K},\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}(\id)
to
\pi_{\mathsf{K},\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}
and
t_{\mathsf{K},g^{-1}\mathsf{K}g}(g)
to
[g]_{K}
. The morphisms
\pi_{\mathsf{K},\mathsf{K}^{\prime}}
form a projective system
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\}
with finite étale transition maps, and the morphisms
[g]_{K}
endow

\mathrm{Sh}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\vcentcolon=\varprojlim_{\mathsf{K}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})

(cf.
[
SP
,
Tag 01YX
]
) with a continuous action of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
(in the sense of
[
Del79
, 2.7.1]
).

We shall often fix the following additional data/notation/assumptions:

\diamond

p
is a rational prime and
\mathfrak{p}
a prime of
\mathbf{E}
lying over
p
,

\diamond

E
is the completion
\mathbf{E}_{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\mathcal{O}_{E}
its ring of integers, and
k
its residue field,

\diamond

G\vcentcolon=\mathbf{G}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, and
\mathcal{G}
is a parahoric model of
G
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,

\diamond

\mathsf{K}_{0}\subseteq G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
the parahoric subgroup given by
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
,

\diamond

{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}^{p}_{f})
a neat compact open subgroup.

The triple
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is a
parahoric Shimura datum
, and is an
unramified Shimura datum
if
\mathcal{G}
is reductive. For an unramified Shimura datum, the extension
E/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is unramified (see
[
Mil94
, Corollary 4.7]
) and we identify
\mathcal{O}_{E}
with
W=W(k)
. Moreover,
G
is quasi-split and split over
\breve{E}
. We shorten
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{E}
) to
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}
).

Let
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
be a parahoric Shimura datum. Associated to
\mathbf{X}
is a unique conjugacy class of coharacters
\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{C}}\to\mathbf{G}_{\mathbb{C}}
(see
[
Mil05
, p. 344]
) whose field of definition is
\mathbf{E}
. Using
[
Kot84
, Lemma 1.1.3]
this corresponds to a unique conjugacy class
\bm{\mu}_{h}
of cocharacters
\mathbb{G}_{m,\overline{E}}\to G_{\overline{E}}
which one checks has field of definition
E
. If
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is unramified then one may use loc. cit. to show the existence of a unique conjugacy class
\bbmu_{h}
of cocharacters
\mathbb{G}_{m,\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\to\mathcal{G}_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
modeling
\bm{\mu}_{h}
.

We often denote other Shimura data with numerical subscripts (e.g.
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
) and use the same numerical subscripts to denote the objects defined above (or below) for this Shimura datum (e.g.
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0,1}\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}
or
\mathcal{G}_{1}
). A morphism of Shimura data
\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is a morphism of group
\mathbb{Q}
-schemes
\alpha\colon\mathbf{G}_{1}\to\mathbf{G}
such that
\alpha_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbf{X}_{1})\subseteq\mathbf{X}
, and is an
embedding
if
\alpha
is a closed embedding. By
[
Del79
, §5]
, for a morphism
\alpha
one has
\mathbf{E}\subseteq\mathbf{E}_{1}
and there is a morphism
\mathrm{Sh}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\to\mathrm{Sh}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}
of
\mathbf{E}_{1}
-schemes equivariant for
\alpha\colon\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and such that if
\alpha(\mathsf{K}_{1})\subseteq\mathsf{K}
then the induced map
\alpha_{\mathsf{K}_{1},\mathsf{K}}\colon\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{1}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}
is given by

\alpha_{\mathsf{K}_{1},\mathsf{K}}\left(\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})(x,g_{1})\mathsf{K}_{1}\right)=\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{Q})(\alpha\circ x,\alpha(g_{1}))\mathsf{K}

on
\mathbb{C}
-points. If the induced map
\alpha\colon G_{1}^{\mathrm{der}}\to G^{\mathrm{der}}
is an isogeny, then each
\alpha_{\mathsf{K}_{1},\mathsf{K}}
is finite étale, as can be checked on connected components (cf.
[
She17
, p. 6620]
).

By a morphism
\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
of parahoric Shimura data we mean a morphism
\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
of Shimura data together with a specified model
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}
of
G_{1}\to G
, which we also denote
\alpha
. We say that
\alpha
is an embedding if
\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}
is a closed embedding.

2.2.
Integral canonical models

We consider the following objects:

\diamond

a symplectic space
\bm{\Lambda}_{0}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
,

\diamond

set
\mathbf{V}_{0}\vcentcolon=\bm{\Lambda}_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}
,

\diamond

set
\Lambda_{0}\vcentcolon=\mathbf{\Lambda}_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,

\diamond

set
V_{0}\vcentcolon=\mathbf{V}_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}=\Lambda_{0}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
.

We then have the
Siegel Shimura datum

(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}_{0}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})
(see
[
Mil05
, §6]
) with reflex field
\mathbb{Q}
. For a neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{L}\subseteq\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}_{0})(\mathbb{A}_{f})
there is an identification of
\mathbf{Sh}_{\mathsf{L}}(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}_{0}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})
with Mumford’s moduli space of principally polarized abelian schemes with level
\mathsf{L}
-structure (see
[
Del71
, §4]
). Set
\mathsf{L}_{0}=\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda_{0})
. Then,
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{L}_{0}\mathsf{L}^{p}}
admits a smooth model
\mathscr{M}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\Lambda_{0})
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with a similar moduli description (see loc. cit.).

Recall that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is of
Hodge type
if there exists an embedding (called a
Hodge embedding
)
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}_{0}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})
for some symplectic space
\mathbf{V}_{0}
over
\mathbb{Q}
, and of
abelian type
if there exists
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
of Hodge type and an isogeny
\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{der}}\to\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{der}}
inducing an isomorphism of adjoint Shimura data
(\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\mathrm{ad}})\to(\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mathbf{X}^{\mathrm{ad}})
. As in
[
Lov17b
, 2.5.14]
(cf. the proof of
[
Kis10
, Corollary 3.4.14]
), if
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type then
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
may be further chosen so that
G_{1}^{\mathrm{der}}\to G^{\mathrm{der}}
admits a central isogeny model
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{der}}\to\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{der}}
. For such well-chosen data, we say that
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
(resp.
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
) is
adapted
to
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
(resp.
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
).

Suppose now that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type. Set

\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}}=\varprojlim_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}=\mathrm{Sh}/\mathsf{K}_{0},

which is a scheme with a continuous action of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. In
[
Kis10
]
, there is constructed an
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-scheme
\mathscr{S}=\mathscr{S}_{K_{0}}
with a continuous action of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
whose generic fiber recovers
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}}
with its
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-action. For a neat compact open subgroup
{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
write
\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}\vcentcolon=\mathscr{S}/{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, and for neat compact open subgroups
{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
and
{\mathsf{K}^{{}^{\prime}p}}
of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and an element
g^{p}
of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that
(g^{p})^{-1}{\mathsf{K}^{p}}g^{p}\subseteq{\mathsf{K}^{{}^{\prime}p}}
denote by
t_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},{\mathsf{K}^{{}^{\prime}p}}}(g^{p})
the induced map
\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{{}^{\prime}p}}}
, subject to the same notational shortenings as in the generic fiber case. Then,
\mathscr{S}
is a so-called
integral canonical model
: the
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-schemes
\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
are smooth (and quasi-projective), the maps
t_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},{\mathsf{K}^{{}^{\prime}p}}}(g^{p})
are finite étale, and for any regular and formally smooth
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-scheme
\mathscr{X}
any morphism
\mathscr{X}_{\eta}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}}
of
E
-schemes lifts uniquely to a morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-schemes
\mathscr{X}\to\mathscr{S}
(the
extension property
).

Example 2.2
.

When
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})=(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}_{0}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})
, and
\mathsf{L}_{0}=\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda_{0})
, then the integral canonical model is precisely the system
\{\mathscr{M}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\Lambda_{0})\}
(cf.
[
Moo98
, Corollary 3.8]
).

If
\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is a morphism of unramified Shimura data of abelian type, then the morphism
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0,1}}\to(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}})_{E_{1}}
has a unique model
\mathscr{S}_{1}\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}
equivariant for the map
\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\to\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. If
\alpha({\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}})\subseteq{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
we denote by
\alpha_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}},{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
the induced morphism
\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}}\to(\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}
.

Lemma 2.3
.

If
\alpha\colon\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{der}}\to\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{der}}
is a central isogeny, then each
\alpha_{\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p},\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is finite étale.

Proof.

It suffices to show the maps
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}}(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{der}},\mathbf{X}_{1}^{+})\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{der}},\mathbf{X}^{+})
(with notation as in
[
Kis10
, (3.4.9)]
) are finite étale. Let
(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2})
be an unramified Shimura datum of Hodge type adapted to
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
and thus to
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
and fix a sufficiently small neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}
. As the map
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}}(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{der}},\mathbf{X}_{1}^{+})\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{der}},\mathbf{X}^{+})
fits into a commutative triangle with maps of the form
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{2}^{p}}(\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\mathrm{der}},\mathbf{X}_{2}^{+})\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}}(\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{der}},\mathbf{X}_{1}^{+})
and
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{2}^{p}}(\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\mathrm{der}},\mathbf{X}_{2}^{+})\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{der}},\mathbf{X}^{+})
it suffices to show these maps are finite étale. But, this follows from
[
Lov17b
, 2.5.14]
as the group
\Delta^{N}
is finite and acts freely by
[
Lov17b
, Proposition 2.5.9 and Lemma 2.5.10]
.
∎

For an unramified Shimura datum
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
of Hodge type, an
integral Hodge embedding
is an embedding
\iota\colon(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}_{0}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm},\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda_{0}))
. By
[
Kim18b
, 3.3.1]
, such an integral Hodge embedding always exists. As each
\mathscr{M}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\Lambda_{0})
is a fine moduli space of principally polarized abelian varieties it has a universal abelian scheme
\mathscr{A}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}
compatible in
\mathsf{L}^{p}
. If
\iota(\mathsf{K}^{p})\subseteq\mathsf{L}^{p}
, we (suppressing
\iota
from the notation) denote by
\mathscr{A}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
the pullback of
\mathscr{A}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}
along
\iota_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathsf{L}^{p}}
. Denote by
\widehat{\mathscr{A}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}\to\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
its
p
-adic completion (equiv. the pullback of
\mathscr{A}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
along
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
), and by
A_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
the generic fiber of
\mathscr{A}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}\to\mathscr{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
.

We finally observe that the connected components of
\mathscr{S}
are homogeneous in a suitable sense.

Lemma 2.4

(cf.
[
Kis10
, Lemma 2.2.5]
)
.

The action of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
on
\pi_{0}(\mathscr{S}_{\mathcal{O}_{\breve{E}}})
is transitive.

2.3.
Étale realization functors

Following
[
KSZ21
, Definition 1.5.4]
, for a multiplicative
\mathbb{Q}
-group
\mathbf{T}
denote by
\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{a}}
the largest
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic subtorus of
\mathbf{T}
, and by
\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{ac}}
the smallest subtorus of
\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{a}}
whose base change to
\mathbb{R}
contains the maximal split subtorus of
(\mathbf{T}_{a})_{\mathbb{R}}
. For a reductive
\mathbb{Q}
-group
\mathbf{G}
denote by
\mathbf{G}^{c}
the
\mathbb{Q}
-group
\mathbf{G}/\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{ac}}
, and by
G^{c}
the group
\mathbf{G}^{c}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.

Fix a parahoric Shimura datum
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
. There is a canonical map of Bruhat–Tits buildings
B(G,F)\to B(G^{c},F)
. Let
x
denote a point of
B(G,F)
corresponding to
\mathcal{G}
, and
x^{c}
its image in
B(G^{c},F)
(see
[
KP18
, §1.1–1.2]
and the references therein). Set
\mathcal{G}^{c}
to be the parahoric group scheme associated to
x^{c}
(denoted by
\mathcal{G}_{x^{c}}^{\circ}
in
[
KP18
, §1.2]
). By
[
KP18
, Proposition 1.1.4]
,
\mathcal{G}
is a central extension of
\mathcal{G}^{c}
, and so one is reductive if and only if the other is.
15
15

15

For the reader less familiar with Bruhat–Tits theory,
[
KP18
, Proposition 1.1.4]
shows that when
\mathcal{G}
is reductive,
\mathcal{G}^{c}=\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{Z}
, where
\mathcal{Z}
is the Zariski closure of
(\mathbf{Z})_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
.
Denote by
\bm{\mu}^{c}_{h}
the conjugacy class of cocharacters of
G^{c}
induced by
\bm{\mu}_{h}
, and if
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is unramified let
\bbmu_{h}^{c}
be conjugacy class of cocharacters of
\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
induced by
\bbmu_{h}
.

Lemma 2.5
.

A morphism
\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
(resp.
\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
) of Shimura data (resp. parahoric Shimura data) induces a morphism
\mathbf{G}_{1}^{c}\to\mathbf{G}^{c}
(resp.
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{c}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}
).

Proof.

The claim concerning Shimura data would follow from
\alpha(\mathbf{Z}_{1,\mathrm{ac}})\subseteq\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{ac}}
. To show this, it suffices to show that if
\mathbf{S}=(\alpha^{-1}(\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{ac}})\cap\mathbf{Z}_{1,\mathrm{ac}})^{\circ}
, then
\mathbf{S}_{\mathbb{R}}
contains the split component of
(\mathbf{Z}_{1,\mathrm{a}})_{\mathbb{R}}
. Suppose not and that
\mathbb{R}^{\times}\subseteq(\mathbf{Z}_{1,\mathrm{a}})(\mathbb{R})
is not contained in
\mathbf{S}(\mathbb{R})
. If
\alpha(\mathbb{R}^{\times})
is not contained in
\mathbf{Z}_{\mathbb{R}}
then we arrive at a contradiction as in the proof of
[
Lov17b
, Lemma 3.1.3]
. As
\mathbb{R}^{\times}\subseteq(\mathbf{Z}_{1,\mathrm{a}})(\mathbb{R})
this then implies that
\alpha(\mathbb{R}^{\times})\subseteq\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{ac}}(\mathbb{R})
which is again a contradiction. The claim concerning parahoric Shimura data then follows by applying
[
KP18
, Proposition 1.1.4]
.
∎

Remark 2.6
.

If
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is of Hodge type, then
\mathbf{G}
is equal to
\mathbf{G}^{c}
. Indeed, this can be checked explicitly for Siegel datum, and follows by functoriality for arbitrary
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
. Shimura data of abelian type need not enjoy this equality in general.

For a Shimura datum
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
, and a neat compact open
\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, the map

\varprojlim_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{K}_{p}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{K}_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}^{\prime}\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}

is a
\mathsf{K}_{p}/\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{\mathsf{K}}
-torsor on
(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\mathrm{pro\acute{e}t}}
, where
\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{\mathsf{K}}
is the closure of
\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{Q})\cap\mathsf{K}
in
\mathsf{K}
(see
[
KSZ21
, §1.5.8]
). If
K_{p}^{c}
denotes the image of
K_{p}
in
G^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, loc. cit. shows that
K_{p}\to K_{p}^{c}
factorizes through
K_{p}/\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{Q})_{K}^{-}
. Denote by
\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{K}}
the
K_{p}^{c}
-torsor obtained by pushing forward
\varprojlim_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{K}_{p}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{K}_{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}^{\prime}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
along
\mathsf{K}_{p}/\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{\mathsf{K}}\to K_{p}^{c}
. We obtain an object
\nu_{\mathsf{K},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
of
G^{c}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}})
given by sending
\rho\colon G^{c}\to\GL(V)
to the pushforward of
\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{K}}
along
\rho\colon K_{p}^{c}\to\GL(V)
. Fix
g
in
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, and suppose
g^{-1}\mathsf{K}g\subseteq\mathsf{K}^{\prime}
. If
g=g_{p}g^{p}
, and
\Int(g_{p}^{c})
is the inner automorphism of
G^{c}
associated to the image
g_{p}^{c}
of
g_{p}
in
G^{c}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, then

(2.3.1)

t_{\mathsf{K},\mathsf{K}^{{}^{\prime}}}(g)^{\ast}(\nu_{{\mathsf{K}^{{}^{\prime}}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\rho)))=\nu_{{\mathsf{K}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\rho\circ\mathrm{Int}((g_{p}^{c})^{-1})).

We call the system
\nu_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\vcentcolon=\{\nu_{{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\}
the
(rational
p
-adic) étale realization functor
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{p}}}
.

Let
\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
be a morphism of Shimura data. If
\alpha(\mathsf{K}_{1})\subseteq\mathsf{K}
one obtains a morphism
\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{K}_{1}}\to\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{K}}\times_{(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}})_{E_{1}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{1}}}
equivariant for
\alpha^{c}\colon K_{p,1}^{c}\to K_{p}^{c}
and, thus an isomorphism of
K_{p}^{c}
-torsors
\alpha_{\ast}^{c}(\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{K}_{1}})\to\mathsf{T}_{\mathsf{K}}\times_{(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}})_{E_{1}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{1}}}
. This is compatible in
{\mathsf{K}}
in the obvious way. Equivalently, for
\rho
in
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(G^{c})
there is an identification

(2.3.2)

\alpha_{{\mathsf{K}_{1}},{\mathsf{K}}}^{\ast}(\nu_{\mathsf{K},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\rho)_{E_{1}})=\nu_{{\mathsf{K}_{1}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\rho\circ\alpha^{c}),

compatible in
{\mathsf{K}_{1}}
,
{\mathsf{K}}
, and
\xi
in the obvious sense.

For a parahoric Shimura datum
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
, and
\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}
(recall
K_{0}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
), there are analogous integral objects. Again by
[
KSZ21
, §1.5.8]
,
\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{Q})_{\mathsf{K}}\subseteq\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{ac}}(\mathbb{Q})
and so
\mathsf{K}_{0}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
factorizes through
\mathsf{K}_{0}/\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{\mathsf{K}}
.

Denote by
\mathsf{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
the push forward of
\varprojlim_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathsf{K}_{p}\subseteq\mathsf{K}_{0}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
along
\mathsf{K}_{0}/\mathbf{Z}(\mathbb{Q})^{-}_{\mathsf{K}}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. From the contents of
[
IKY24
, §2.1.1]
, we obtain an associated object of
\mathcal{G}^{c}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}})
:

\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{c})\to\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}}).

Fix
g=g_{p}g^{p}
in
K_{0}\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and suppose
(g^{p})^{-1}\mathsf{K}^{p}g^{p}\subseteq\mathsf{K}^{{}^{\prime}p}
. If
\Int(g_{p}^{c})
is the inner automorphism of
\mathcal{G}^{c}
associated to the image
g_{p}^{c}
of
g_{p}
in
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, then

(2.3.3)

t_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\mathsf{K}_{0},{\mathsf{K}^{{}^{\prime}p}}\mathsf{K}_{0}}(g)^{\ast}(\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{{}^{\prime}p}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)))=\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi\circ\mathrm{Int}((g_{p}^{c})^{-1})).

We call the system
\{\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\}
the
(integral) étale realization functor
on
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0}}}
. That this is an integral model of
\nu_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is made precise by the observation that

(2.3.4)

\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]=\nu_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}

compatibly in a neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
.

Let
\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
be a morphism of parahoric Shimura data. If
\alpha(\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p})\subseteq{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
one obtains a morphism
\mathsf{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{0,1}\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}\to\mathsf{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\times_{(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{E_{1}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0,1}\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}}
equivariant for
\alpha^{c}\colon\mathcal{G}_{1}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and, thus an isomorphism of
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-torsors
\alpha_{\ast}^{c}(\mathsf{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{1}_{p}})\to\mathsf{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\times_{(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{E_{1}}}\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0,1}\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}}
. This is compatible in
{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
in the obvious way. Equivalently, for
\xi
in
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{c})
there is an identification

(2.3.5)

\alpha_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}},{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}^{\ast}(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)_{E_{1}})=\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi\circ\alpha^{c}),

compatible in
{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}
,
{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, and
\xi
in the obvious sense.

Proposition 2.7
.

The
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
-local system
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
belongs to
\mathcal{G}^{c}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\bm{\mu}^{c}_{h}}^{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})
.

Proof.

That
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is de Rham follows from
[
LZ17
, Corollary 4.9]
and the claim about cocharacters is reduced to the case of special points which follows from
[
LZ17
, Lemma 4.8]
.
∎

Let
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
be an unramified Shimura datum of Hodge type, and fix an integral Hodge embedding
\iota\colon(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}_{0}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm},\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda_{0}))
. By
[
IKY24
, Theorem A.14]
there is a tensor package
(\Lambda_{0},\mathds{T}_{0})
with
\mathcal{G}=\mathrm{Fix}(\mathds{T}_{0})
(in the sense of loc. cit.). As in
[
Kim18b
, §3.1.2]
, one may construct from
\mathds{T}_{0}\otimes 1\subseteq V_{0}^{\otimes}
tensors
\mathds{T}_{0,p}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
on
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(A_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee}
as an object
of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}})
, which are compatible in
{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
.

Proposition 2.8
.

There is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local systems
\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\Lambda_{0})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(A_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee}
carrying
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathds{T}_{0})\otimes 1
in
\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\Lambda_{0})[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
to
\mathds{T}_{0,p}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
in
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(A_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee}
.

Proof.

First suppose that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})=(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}_{0}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm},\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda_{0}))
and
\iota
is the identity embedding. Then, by the moduli description of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
one observes that there is an identification

\mathsf{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}=\underline{\Isom}\left((\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}},t_{0}),(T_{p}(A_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}),t)\right),

where
t_{0}
is the tensor as in
[
Kim18a
, Example 2.1.6]
, and
t
is the analogous tensor built from the Weil pairing coming from the principal polarization on
A_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. We deduce a natural identification between
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\Lambda_{0})
and
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(A_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}/\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})^{\vee}
. The desired isomorphism for general
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
comes from the compatability in (
2.3.5
). To prove that the induced isomorphism of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
-local systems takes
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathds{T}_{0})\otimes 1
to
\mathds{T}_{0,p}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
, we observe that these constructions admit globalizations over
\mathbf{E}
in the obvious way, in which case it suffices to check the claim on
\mathbb{C}
-points. But, this then follows from
[
Mil05
, Theorem 7.4]
.
∎

As a result of Proposition
2.8
, we see that
\mathds{T}_{0,p}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
actually lies in the image of the injective map
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(A_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee}\to\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(A_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee}
. We deduce from the contents of
[
IKY24
, §2.1.1]
that
\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is the object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}})
associated to the torsor

\underline{\Isom}\left((\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}},\mathds{T}_{0}\otimes 1),(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(A_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee},\mathds{T}_{0,p}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})\right),

which thus is independent of any choices. Similar claims may be verified for
\nu_{\mathsf{K},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

We end this section by describing a method, applying ideas from
[
Lov17b
, §4.6–4.7]
, which will allow us to reduce statements about Shimura data of abelian type to those of Hodge type.

Lemma 2.9
.

Let
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
be an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type, and
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
an adapted unramified Shimura datum of Hodge type. Then, there exists an unramified Shimura datum
(\mathbf{T},\{h\},\mathcal{T})
of special type and an unramified Shimura datum
(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2})
of abelian type, both with reflex field
\mathbf{E}_{1}
, such that

(1)

there exists a morphism of unramified Shimura data

\alpha=(\alpha^{1},\alpha^{2})\colon(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2})\to(\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{T},\mathbf{X}_{1}\times\{h\},\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T})

such that
\alpha^{c}\colon\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}\to\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T}^{c}
is a closed embedding,

(2)

there exists a morphism of unramified Shimura data
\beta\colon(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
such that
\beta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2},\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}}\to(\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\mathcal{O}_{E_{1}}}
is finite étale.

Proof.

Choose a connected component
\mathbf{X}_{1}^{+}
of
\mathbf{X}_{1}
and an element
h_{G}
of
\mathbf{X}_{1}^{+}
. Let
(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2})
be the unramifed Shimura datum of abelian type obtained by applying the construction in
[
Lov17b
, §4.6]
to
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
and the choice of
\mathbf{X}_{1}^{+}
, and set

(\mathbf{T},\{h\},\mathcal{T})\vcentcolon=\left(\mathrm{Res}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}/\mathbb{Q}}\,\,\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbf{E}_{1}},\{h_{E}\},\left(\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}/\mathbb{Z}}\,\,\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{E}_{1}}}\right)_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\right),

with
h_{E}
as in
[
Lov17b
, 4.6.4]
. The map
\alpha
is then constructed from the natural inclusion of
\mathbf{G}_{2}=\mathbf{G}_{1}\times_{\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}}}\mathbf{T}
into
\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{T}
. To prove that
\alpha^{c}
is a closed embedding, observe that as
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})
is of Hodge type that
(\mathbf{Z}_{1})_{\mathrm{ac}}
is trivial, and as
\mathbf{Z}_{1}\to\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}}
is an isogeny that
(\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ab}})_{\mathrm{ac}}
is also trivial. From this we deduce that
\mathbf{Z}_{2}^{c}=\mathbf{T}_{\mathrm{ac}}
and so the claim follows. The map
\beta
is constructed as in
[
Lov17b
, §4.7.2]
, the second claim follows from Lemma
2.3
.
∎

2.4.
Prismatic and syntomic
realization functors

For an unramified Shimura datum
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
of abelian type, and a neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, we associate the smooth
p
-adic formal scheme
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, and the open embedding

\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}\vcentcolon=(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})_{\eta}\subseteq\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}^{\mathrm{an}},

with quasi-compact source, which is an isomorphism when
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\Spec(\mathcal{O}_{E})
is proper (see
[
Hub94
, Remark 4.6 (iv)]
). The morphisms
t_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},{\mathsf{K}^{{}^{\prime}p}}}(g^{p})
induce morphisms on the adic spaces
\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
compatible with those maps on the
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}^{\mathrm{an}}
, and we use similar notational shortenings for them. We may also consider the functors

\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{c})\to\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}),\qquad\Lambda\mapsto\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\Lambda)^{\mathrm{an}}|_{\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}},

which enjoy the same compatabilities for varying level structure and morphisms of unramified Shimura varieties as the
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-local systems
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

Prismatic realization functors

By a
prismatic (
F
-crystal) realization functor
at level
\mathsf{K}^{p}
, we mean an exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functor (unique up to unique isomorphism, as
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
is fully faithful)

\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{c})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}),

together with
\jmath_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}
. If the isomorphisms
\jmath_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
are chosen compatibly in
\mathsf{K}^{p}
, we call the collection
\{(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}},\jmath_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})\}
a
prismatic (
F
-crystal) canonical model
of
\{\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}\}
, which is unique up to unique isomorphism. We often omit the data of
\jmath_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
from the notation.

Fix prismatic canonical models
\{\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\}
and
\{\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\}
for unramified Shimura data
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
and
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
respectively. If
\alpha\colon(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})\to(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is a morphism, then for any
\xi
in
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{c})
, and neat compact open subgroups
\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}\subseteq\mathbf{G}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
with
\alpha({\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}})\subseteq{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, one has canonical, compatible in
\mathsf{K}^{p}
,
\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}
, and
\xi
, identifications

(2.4.1)

\alpha_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}},{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}^{\ast}(\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}(\xi)_{\mathcal{O}_{1}})=\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}(\xi\circ\alpha^{c}).

This follows by appropriately applying
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}
and the isomorphisms
\jmath_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
and
\jmath_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}
to (
2.3.5
).

Theorem 2.10
.

Suppose that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type. Then, for any
{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, the functor
\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}
takes values in
\mathbf{Loc}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}\text{-}\mathrm{gr}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})
. In particular, the collection

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}\circ\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}=\vcentcolon\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{c})\to\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})

forms a prismatic canonical model of
\{\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}\}
.

Remark 2.11
.

If
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is of special type this theorem was (implicitly) obtained by Daniels in
[
Dan22
]
, and his construction agrees with ours by the unicity of canonical prismatic models.

We first prove a refined version of this theorem when
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is of Hodge type using
[
IKY24
, Theorem 2.28]
. Choose an integral Hodge embedding
\iota\colon(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})\to(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}_{0}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm},\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda_{0}))
and write
\mathcal{A}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}\to\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
for the generic fiber of
\widehat{\mathscr{A}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}\to\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
. Define

\mathds{T}_{0,p}^{\mathrm{an}}\vcentcolon=(\mathds{T}_{0,p}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})^{\mathrm{an}}|_{\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}}\subseteq(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{A}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee})^{\otimes}.

By Proposition
2.8
, we have a canonical identification

(\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}(\Lambda_{0}),\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}(\mathds{T}_{0}))\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{A}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee},\mathds{T}_{0,p}^{\mathrm{an}}).

Combining
[
ALB23
, Corollary 4.64]
and
[
GR24
, Theorem 1.10 (i)]
, we deduce that
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{A}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee}
has prismatically good reduction with a canonical identification

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{A}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee})=\mathcal{H}^{1}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}(\widehat{\mathscr{A}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee},

compatible in
\mathsf{K}^{p}
. Applying
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{-1}
to
\mathds{T}^{\mathrm{an}}_{0,p}
gives rise to a set
\mathds{T}_{0,p}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
of tensors on the object
\mathcal{H}^{1}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}(\widehat{\mathscr{A}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee}
of
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
. The following is a consequence of
[
IKY24
, Theorem 2.28]
, and immediately implies Theorem
2.10
for
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
of Hodge type by
[
IKY24
, Proposition 1.28]
.

Theorem 2.12
.

Suppose that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is an unramified Shimura datum of Hodge type. Then,

\underline{\Isom}\left((\Lambda_{0}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}},\mathds{T}_{0}\otimes 1),(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}(\widehat{\mathscr{A}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee},\mathds{T}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}_{0,p})\right)

is a prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-torsor with
F
-structure on
(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
, compatible in
{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
.

To reduce from the abelian type case to the Hodge type case, we require a simple lemma concerning prismatically good reduction local systems. We use the notation from §
1
.

Lemma 2.13
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{X}_{2}\to\mathfrak{X}_{1}
is a finite étale cover where
\mathfrak{X}_{1}\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{K})
is smooth. Then, an object
\mathbb{L}_{1}
of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(X_{1})
has prismatically good reduction if and only if
\mathbb{L}_{2}\vcentcolon=\mathbb{L}_{1}|_{X_{2}}
does.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the if condition. We may assume that
\mathfrak{X}_{i}=\Spf(R_{i})
where
R_{i}
are (framed) small
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebras. That
\mathbb{L}_{1}
is crystalline is clear. Let
(\mathcal{V}^{i},\varphi_{\mathcal{V}^{i}})
denote the object
T^{-1}_{\mathfrak{X}_{i}}(\mathbb{L}_{i})
of
\mathbf{Vect}^{\mathrm{an},\varphi}((\mathfrak{X}_{i})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
. Then, by the flatness of
\mathfrak{X}_{2}\to\mathfrak{X}_{1}
, we have that

(j_{(\mathfrak{S}_{R_{1}},(E))})_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{1}_{(\mathfrak{S}_{R_{1}},(E))}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}_{R_{1}}}\mathfrak{S}_{R_{2}}=(j_{(\mathfrak{S}_{R_{2}},(E))})_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{2}_{(\mathfrak{S}_{R_{2}},(E))}.

The right-hand side is a vector bundle by
[
IKY24
, Proposition 1.26]
and thus so is the sheaf
(j_{(\mathfrak{S}_{R_{1}},(E))})_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{1}_{(\mathfrak{S}_{R_{1}},(E))}
. Thus,
\mathbb{L}_{1}
has prismatically good reduction again by loc. cit.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.10
.

We freely use notation from Lemma
2.9
. We first prove the claim for
(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2})
. Choosing a faithful representation
\xi_{2}^{\prime}=\xi_{1}\otimes\xi_{t}
of
\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T}^{c}
, where
\xi_{1}
(resp.
\xi_{t}
) is a faithful representation of
\mathcal{G}_{1}
(resp.
\mathcal{T}^{c}
), we obtain the faithful representation
\xi_{2}\vcentcolon=\xi_{2}^{\prime}\circ\alpha^{c}
of
\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}
. Choosing neat compact open subgroups
\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
\mathsf{K}^{p}_{t}\subseteq\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that
\alpha(\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2})\subseteq{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}\times{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{t}}
, we see from (
2.3.5
) that

\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2},\mathrm{an}}(\xi_{2})=(\alpha^{1}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}K_{0,2},{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}\mathsf{K}_{0,1}})^{\ast}(\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}},\mathrm{an}}(\xi_{1}))\otimes_{\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}}(\alpha^{2}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}K_{0,2},\mathsf{K}^{p}_{t}\mathsf{K}_{0,t}})^{\ast}(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{t},\mathrm{an}}(\xi_{t})).

But,
\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}(\xi_{1})
has prismatically good reduction by Theorem
2.12
. Moreover, as
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{t}}
is of the form
\coprod\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}})
, for connected finite étale
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-algebras
\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}
(e.g. see
[
DY24
, Proposition 3.22]
),
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{t},\mathrm{an}}(\xi_{t})
has prismatically good reduction by
[
GR24
, Proposition 3.7]
. Thus, as having prismatically good reduction is preserved by pullbacks and tensor products, the claim follows from
[
IKY24
, Corollary 2.30]
.

Now, to prove the claim for
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
it suffices to prove that for each compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}^{p}
and each connected component
\mathscr{C}
of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
that
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}(\xi)|_{\widehat{\mathscr{C}}_{\eta}}
has prismatically good reduction. By Lemma
2.4
and Equation (
2.3.3
), we may assume that there exists some neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}_{2}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
such that
\beta(\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2})\subseteq\mathsf{K}^{p}
and
\mathscr{C}
lies in the image of
\beta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2},\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. As
\beta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2},\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is finite étale the claim follows from (
2.3.5
) and Lemma
2.13
.
∎

Syntomic realization functor

We now discuss the existence of an upgrade of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
to a
syntomic realization functor
. By a
syntomic realization functor
at level
\mathsf{K}^{p}
, we mean an object
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}
of
\mathcal{G}^{c}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})^{\mathrm{syn}})
(which is unique up to unique isomorphism, as
\mathrm{R}_{\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
is fully faithful) with
\jmath_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon\mathrm{R}_{\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}\circ\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{syn}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
. Such
\jmath_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
are unique if they exist, and so we often omit them from the notation. The collection
\{\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{\mathrm{syn}}}\}
is called a
syntomic canonical model
of
\{\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}\}
.

By Proposition
1.28
to show such
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}
exist it suffices to show that
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
takes values in
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
. This is true (see Corollary
3.7
) but we delay its proof until §
3.2
.

Theorem 2.14
.

Suppose that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type. Then, for any
{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, the functor
\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
is of type
-\mu_{h}^{c}
, and so takes values in
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
. In particular, the collection

\mathrm{R}_{\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}^{-1}\circ\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}=\vcentcolon\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{syn}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{c})\to\mathbf{Vect}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})^{\mathrm{syn}})

forms a syntomic canonical model of
\{\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}\}
.

2.5.
Potentially crystalline loci and comparison of stratifications

Let
K
be a complete discrete valuation field with perfect residue field, and let
X
be a quasi-separated adic space locally of finite type over
K
, and
\Sigma
be either
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
or
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. For an object
\mathbb{L}
of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\Sigma}(X)
we call a point
x
of
|X|^{\mathrm{cl}}

(potentially) crystalline
for
\mathbb{L}
if
\mathbb{L}_{x}
is a (potentially) crystalline representation of
\Gamma_{k(x)}
.

There exists at most one quasi-compact open subset
U\subseteq X
such that
|U|^{\mathrm{cl}}
is the set of potentially crystalline points of
\mathbb{L}
(cf.
[
Hub93
, Corollary 4.3]
). In this case we call
U
the
potentially crystalline locus
of
\mathbb{L}
. For a
K
-scheme
S
locally of finite type, and an object
\mathbb{L}
of
\mathbf{Loc}_{\Sigma}(S)
, if we speak of the potentially crystalline locus of
\mathbb{L}
we mean the potentially crystalline locus of
\mathbb{L}^{\mathrm{an}}
. These definitions apply equal well for
\mathcal{G}
-objects (or
G
-objects)
\omega
in these categories.
16
16

16

Although as observed in
[
IKY24
, Proposition 2.20]
, this will coincide with the set of potentially crystalline points for the value of
\omega
on any faithful representation of
\mathcal{G}
or
G
.

Observe that potentially crystalline points satisfy pullback stability: for a map
f\colon X^{\prime}\to X
, a classical point
x^{\prime}
of
X^{\prime}
is potentially crystalline for
f^{\ast}(\mathbb{L})
if and only if
x=f(x^{\prime})
is a potentially crystalline point for
\mathbb{L}
. If
k(x^{\prime})/k(x)
is unramified (e.g.
f=\mathfrak{f}_{\eta}
for a finite étale model
\mathfrak{f}\colon\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}\to\mathfrak{X}
), one may replace ‘potentially crystalline’ by ‘crystalline’.

For a Shimura datum
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
of (pre-)abelian type, and a neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, the existence of a potentially crystalline locus
U_{\mathsf{K}}\subseteq\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}}^{\mathrm{an}}
for
\nu_{\mathsf{K},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
was established in
[
IM20
, Theorem 5.17]
(see
[
IM20
, Remark 2.12]
and
[
LZ17
, Theorem 1.2]
). If
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type, and
\mathsf{K}=\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}
, we abbreviate
U_{\mathsf{K}}
to
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
which coincides with the potentially crystalline locus of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

We now describe
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
for unramified Shimura data of abelian type, generalizing results of Imai–Mieda in the PEL setting (see
[
IM20
, Corollary 2.11 and Proposition 5.4]
and
[
IM13
, §7]
).

Proposition 2.15
.

Let
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
be an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type, and
\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
a neat compact open subgroup. Then,
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}=(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\eta}
and all the classical points of
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
are crystalline for
\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

Proof.

We know that all the classical points of
\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
are crystalline for
\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
by Theorem

2.10
.
Moreover, the full claim holds in the Siegel-type case by
[
IM20
, Theorem 5.17]
and its proof.

Assume that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is of Hodge type and choose an integral Hodge embedding
\iota\colon(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}_{0}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm},\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda_{0}))
, and a level
\mathsf{L}^{p}
with
\iota(\mathsf{K}^{p})\subseteq\mathsf{L}^{p}
and
\iota_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathsf{L}_{0}\mathsf{L}^{p}}
is a closed embedding. By the construction of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
(see
[
Kis10
, Theorem (2.3.8)]
),
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is obtained as the normalization of a closed subscheme of
\mathscr{M}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\Lambda_{0})
, and so finite over
\mathscr{M}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\Lambda_{0})
.
17
17

17

More precisely, as a closed subscheme of
\mathscr{M}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\Lambda_{0})
is finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, it is excellent (see
[
SP
,
Tag 07QW
]
), and thus Nagata (see
[
SP
,
Tag 07QV
]
) and so one may apply
[
SP
,
Tag 035S
]
.
So, if
x
is a classical point of
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}^{\mathrm{an}}
not in
\mathcal{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
then the image of
x
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}^{\mathrm{an}}
is a point outside
\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{L}^{p}}}
(see
[
Hub96
, Proposition 1.9.6]
). Hence
x
is not a potentially crystalline point by the Siegel case, and pullback stability.

Assume now that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is of abelian type.
We use notation from Lemma
2.9
.
Let
x
be a classical point of
\mathrm{Sh}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}^{\mathrm{an}}
not in
\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
. By Lemma
2.4
and Equation (
2.3.3
), we may assume that
there is a lift
x_{2}
of
x
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{2}^{p}}^{\mathrm{an}}
, but not in
\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}_{2}^{p}}}
, for some neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}_{2}^{p}\subset\mathbf{G}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. The image
x_{1}
of
x_{2}
in
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}}^{\mathrm{an}}
for an appropriate
\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}\subset\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
is a point outside
\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}}}
because
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{2}^{p}}
is finite over
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}}
(cf.
[
Hub96
, Proposition 1.9.6]
). Take a faithful representation
\xi_{1}^{\mathrm{ad}}
of
\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ad}}
, inducing faithful representations
\xi^{\mathrm{ad}}
and
\xi_{2}^{\mathrm{ad}}
of
\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}
and
\mathbf{G}_{2}^{\mathrm{ad}}
respectively as
\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}\cong\mathbf{G}_{2}^{\mathrm{ad}}\cong\mathbf{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ad}}
. By
[
IM20
, Corollary 2.11 and Proposition 5.4]
,
\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}},\mathrm{an}}(\xi_{1}^{\mathrm{ad}})_{x_{1}}
is not potentially crystalline. Hence
\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}(\xi^{\mathrm{ad}})_{x}
is not potentially crystalline too by pullback stability, as the pullbacks of
\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}(\xi^{\mathrm{ad}})_{x}
and
\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}},\mathrm{an}}(\xi_{1}^{\mathrm{ad}})_{x_{1}}
to
x_{2}
are both isomorphic to
\omega_{\mathsf{K}_{2}^{p},\mathrm{an}}(\xi_{2}^{\mathrm{ad}})_{x_{2}}
. Thus,
x
is not potentially crystalline.
∎

For neat compact open subgroups
K^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
K_{p}\subseteq G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, we may define functions

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}

\displaystyle\colon|U_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K^{p}}}|^{\mathrm{cl}}\to B(G^{c})

\displaystyle\bigg(\text{resp. }\Sigma^{\circ}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}

\displaystyle\colon|U_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}|^{\mathrm{cl}}\to C(\mathcal{G}^{c})\bigg)

(where
C(\mathcal{G}^{c})
is the quotient of
G^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
by the action of
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
by
\sigma
-conjugacy), associating to
x
the element of
B(G^{c})
(resp.
C(\mathcal{G}^{c})
) associated with the
F
-isocrystal (resp.
F
-crystal) with
G^{c}
-structure (resp.
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-structure) given by
\underline{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ(\nu_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{x}
(resp.
\underline{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{x}
) (see
[
IKY25
, Example 1.5]
for this latter notation). These functions are equivariant via the map
\Gamma_{E}\to\Gamma_{k}
, when the source (resp. target) is endowed with the natural action of
\Gamma_{E}
(resp.
\Gamma_{k}
). On the other hand, we may define functions

\displaystyle\overline{\Sigma}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{k})\to B(G^{c})

\displaystyle\bigg(\text{resp. }\overline{\Sigma}^{\circ}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}

\displaystyle\colon\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{k})\to C(\mathcal{G}^{c})\bigg)

in the analogous way using the the
\mathcal{G}
-object in
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}((\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},k})_{\mathrm{crys}})
given by
\underline{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ\omega_{\mathsf{K^{p}},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
which is equivariant with respect to the actions of
\Gamma_{k}
.

In the following, we use the notion of an
overconvergent
(also known as
wide
,
partially proper
, or
Berkovich
) open subset of a rigid
E
-space, as in
[
FK18
, Chapter II, §4.3]

.

Proposition 2.16
.

The functions
\Sigma_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
and
\Sigma^{\circ}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
are overconvergent locally constant.
18
18

18

i.e., for every classical point
x
there exists an overconvergent open neighborhood
U_{x}\subseteq U_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
such that these functions are constant on
|U_{x}|^{\mathrm{cl}}
.
Moreover,

(2.5.1)

\Sigma_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}=\overline{\Sigma}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\circ\mathrm{sp},\quad\Sigma^{\circ}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}=\overline{\Sigma}^{\circ}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\circ\mathrm{sp},

where
\mathrm{sp}\colon|(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\eta}|^{\mathrm{cl}}=|U_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}|^{\mathrm{cl}}\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{k})
is the specialization map.

Proof.

The second equality in (
2.5.1
) follows essentially by construction. To prove the first equality, it suffices to check that for a point
x
of
|U_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}|^{\mathrm{cl}}
the
F
-isocrystals with
G
-structure given by
\underline{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ(\nu_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{x}
and that induced by
\underline{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{x}
agree. It suffices to find an isomorphism between their values at
\Lambda_{0}
matching the tensors
\mathds{T}_{0}
. But, this can be reduced to the second equality in (
2.5.1
) considering (
2.3.3
).

The claim concerning overconvergent local constancy for
\Sigma_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
and
\Sigma^{\circ}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
follows from the equations in (
2.5.1
) as the the tube open subsets
\mathrm{sp}^{-1}(x)^{\circ}
, for
x
in
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{k})
, are overconvergent open and contain every classical point of
(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\eta}
(see
[
ALY22
, Proposition 2.13]
). To prove that
\Sigma_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is overconvergent locally constant for all
\mathsf{K}_{p}
, observe that for
\mathsf{K}_{p}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{K}_{p}
we have that

\Sigma_{\mathsf{K}_{p}^{\prime}\mathsf{K}^{p}}=\Sigma_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}\circ\pi_{\mathsf{K}_{p}^{\prime}\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}.

Using this, and that
\pi_{\mathsf{K}_{p}^{\prime}\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is finite étale and so preserves overconvergent opens under both preimage and image (cf.
[
Hub96
, p. 427 (a)]
), one reduces to the previous case
\mathsf{K}_{p}=\mathsf{K}_{0}
.
∎

Remark 2.17
.

Beware that the overconvergent local constancy in Proposition
2.16
does not imply constancy on each connected component as the overconvergent subset
\bigcup_{x}U_{x}
, with notation as in Footnote
18
, need not equal
U_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
(and may not even have the same number of components). This is because an overconvergent open subset of a rigid space
U
which contains all classical points need not be all of
U
. For example, it is possible that this union could be
\mathrm{sep}^{-1}(U^{\mathrm{Berk}}-\{y\})
, where
\mathrm{sep}\colon U\to U^{\mathrm{Berk}}
is the separation map as in
[
FK18
, Chapter 0, §2.3.(c)]
, and
y
is a non-classical rank
1
point. For example, compare with the overconvergent open subset of the closed unit ball
\mathbb{B}^{1}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
given by the complement of the
closure
of the Gauss point, which is not even a connected subset.

2.6.
Comparison with work of Lovering

In this subsection we compare our work to that in
[
Lov17a
]
, and derive several consequences about Shimura varieties of abelian type when
p>2
.

2.6.1.
Comparison result

In
[
Lov17a
]
, Lovering constructs a so-called
crystalline canonical model

\{\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{crys}}\}
of the system
\{\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}\}
for an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type. More precisely, with notation as in
[
IKY25
, §2.1.2]
, he constructs exact
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-linear
\otimes
-functors

\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{crys}}\colon\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{c})\to\mathbf{VectF}^{\varphi,\mathrm{div}}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})_{\mathrm{crys}})

compatible in
{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, together with compatible identifications of filtered objects of
\mathcal{G}^{c}\text{-}\mathbf{MIC}(\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})
:

i_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon D_{\mathrm{dR}}\circ\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{an}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\omega_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathrm{crys}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}].

Moreover, he shows that for all finite unramified
E^{\prime}/E
, and points
x\colon\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}})\to\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
that:

(ICM1)

for all
\xi
in
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{c})
, the morphism of isocrystals on
\Spa(E^{\prime})

is Frobenius equivariant,

(ICM2)

Theorem 2.18

There is an identification

(2.6.1)

Proposition 2.19
.

There is an isomorphism

\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}(\Lambda_{0}))\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widehat{\mathscr{A}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}/\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})^{\vee}

{\mathsf{K}^{p}}

.

Thus, it suffices to show that this isomorphism carries

, there is an injection

2.6.2.
Cohomological consequences

To obtain cohomological implications of Theorem
2.18
, it is useful to recall that the group-theoretic description of when a Shimura variety is proper.

Proposition 2.20
.

For a neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic,

(2)

\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\Spec(E)
is proper,

(3)

\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\Spec(\mathcal{O}_{E})
is proper.

Proof.

The equivalence of of the first two conditions is classical (e.g. see
[
Pau04
, Lemma 3.1.5]
). The equivalence of the first and third conditions is
[
MP19
, Corollary 4.1.7]
when
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is of Hodge type, and one quickly reduces to this case using Lemma
2.3
.
∎

So combining
[
IKY25
, Corollary 3.11]
and Corollary
3.7
reproves
[
Lov17a
, Theorem 3.6.1]
.

Proposition 2.21

(
[
Lov17a
, Theorem 3.6.1]
)
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{der}}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic. Then, for any object
\xi
of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{c})
, the Galois representation
H^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}((\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])
of
E
is crystalline, and there is a canonical isomorphism of filtered
F
-isocrystals

(2.6.3)

D_{\mathrm{crys}}\left(H^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}((\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])\right)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}H^{i}_{\mathrm{crys}}\left(((\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}/\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})_{\mathrm{crys}},\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{crys}}(\xi)[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\right).

If the

-weights of

are in

.

We obtain a syntomic refinement of the above result. To this end, let us say that a
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
-representation of
\Gal(\overline{E}/E)
, with

if it belongs to the essential image of the functor
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathbf{Vect}(\mathcal{O}_{E}^{\mathrm{syn}}/p^{n})\to\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}}(\Gal(\overline{E}/E))
, which when

is a refinement of the crystalliness condition (cf.
[
Bha23
, Theorem 6.6.13]
and Footnote
2
). The following is an immediate corollary of
[

, Proposition 3.12]
.

Theorem 2.22
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{der}}
is
\mathbb{Q}
-anisotropic. Then, for any object
\xi
of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G}^{c})
with
\bm{\mu}^{c}_{h}
-weights of
\xi[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
in
[0,p-3-i]
the
\Gal(\overline{E}/E)
-representation
H^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}((\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)/p^{n})
has syntomically good reduction for any

in
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
. In fact, there is an isomorphism

H^{i}_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}((\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}},\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\xi)/p^{n})\simeq T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\left(\mathcal{H}^{i}_{\mathrm{syn}}(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}/\mathcal{O}_{E},\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}(\xi))/p^{n})\right).

2.6.3.
Comparison of stratifications

In
[
SZ22
]
, Newton stratifications and central leaves are defined on the special fiber of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, extending all previously known cases (see the references in op. cit.). This gives functions

\Upsilon_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{k})\to B(G^{c}),\qquad\Upsilon^{\circ}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{k})\to C(\mathcal{G}^{c}).

equivariant with respect to the actions of
\Gamma_{k}
. By the results of
[
SZ22
, §5.4.2 and §5.4.5]
,
\Upsilon_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
agrees with the function to
B(G^{c})
defined using
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{crys}}
, and agrees with the function
C(\mathcal{G}^{c})
defined using
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{crys}}
when
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is of Hodge type or
Z(\mathbf{G})
is connected. Combining this with Proposition
2.16
and Theorem
2.18
then gives the following corollary.

Corollary 2.23
.

Suppose that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type (resp. of Hodge type or of abelian type and
Z(\mathbf{G})
is connected). Then, for any neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}_{p}\subseteq\mathsf{K}_{0}
and neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
we have that

\Sigma_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p}}=\Upsilon_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\circ\mathrm{sp}\circ\pi_{\mathsf{K}_{p}\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}},\quad\bigg(\emph{resp.}\Sigma^{\circ}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}=\Upsilon_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\circ}\circ\mathrm{sp}\bigg).

Remark 2.24
.

While some of the rational (i.e.,
B(G^{c})
related) results in Proposition
2.16
and Corollary
2.23
could have been proven using results in
[
Lov17a
]
, the integral (i.e.,
C(\mathcal{G}^{c})
related) results could not, as Lovering is only able to establish matching between the lattices
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\Lambda)
and
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{crys}}(\Lambda)
for low Hodge–Tate weights.

3.
Prismatic characterizations of integral models and Serre–Tate theory

We formulate and prove two characterizations of integral canonical models for unramified Shimura data of abelian type: one in terms of prismatic
F
-crystals and the other in terms of prismatic
F
-gauges. The first is both stronger and less sophisticated, but the second is more conceptual and naturally leads us to a version of the Serre–Tate deformation theorem for such integral Shimura varieties. Throughout this section we assume that
p>2
.

3.1.
The universal deformation spaces of Ito

To formulate our prismatic characterization of Shimura varieties, we need a deformation space of prismatic
F
-crystals with
\mathcal{G}
-structure of type
\mu
. This is furnished by a construction of Ito which we now recall.
20
20

20

Ito’s work is in terms of prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-
\mu
-displays. That said, given the contents of §
1.5.2
we glibly state his work in terms of prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-torsors with
F
-structure.

Notation 3.1
.

We fix the following notation:

•

k
is a perfect field of characteristic
p
,

•

W=W(k)
,

•

\mathcal{G}
is a smooth group
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme (assumed reductive in §
3.1.2
),

•

\mu\colon\mathbb{G}_{m,W}\to\mathcal{G}_{W}
is a
1
-bounded cocharacter (see
[
Lau21
, Definition 6.3.1]
),

•

\mathcal{C}_{W}
is the category of complete Noetherian local rings
R
equipped with a local ring map
W\to R
with
k\to R/\mathfrak{m}_{R}
an isomorphism, and with morphisms maps of local
W
-algebra,

•

\mathcal{C}_{W}^{\mathrm{reg}}
is the full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}_{W}
consisting of regular local rings.

We consider objects
\mathcal{C}_{W}
of as
p
-adic topological rings unless stated otherwise.

3.1.1.
Universal deformation spaces

Denote by
\mathcal{U}_{\mu}
the unipotent group scheme over
W
associated to
\mu
via the dynamic method (see
[
Con14
, Theorem 4.1.7]
). Set
R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{O}(\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{\mu})
, which is a
p
-adically complete ring non-canonically isomorphic to
W\llbracket t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}\rrbracket
for some
d
(see
[
Ito23
, Lemma 4.2.6]
). If
f\colon\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}
is a morphism of reductive groups over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
mapping
\mu_{1}
to
\mu
then we obtain an induced continuous morphism of
W
-algebras
R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\to R_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu_{1}}
. Furthermore, if the map
\mathcal{G}_{1}^{\mathrm{ad}}\to\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}
induced by
f
is an isomorphism, then the map
R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\to R_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu_{1}}
is an isomorphism.

Fix an element
b
of
\mathcal{G}(W)\mu(p)^{-1}\mathcal{G}(W)
. The pair
(\mathcal{G}_{W},\varphi_{b})
, where
\varphi_{b}
corresponds to left multiplication by
b
, defines an element of
\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi,-\mu}_{\mathcal{G}}(k_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
. As in
[
Ito25
, Definition 1.1.2]
, for an object
R
of
\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{reg}}_{W}
, a
deformation
of
(\mathcal{G}_{W},\varphi_{b})
over
R
is a pair
((\mathcal{A},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}}),\gamma)
where
(\mathcal{A},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}})
is an object of
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}}^{\varphi,-\mu}(R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
, and
\gamma\colon(\mathcal{A},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}})|_{k_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}(\mathcal{G}_{W},\varphi_{b})
is an isomorphism. In
[
Ito25
, Theorem 1.1.3]
(using Proposition
1.40
), Ito shows that there is a
universal deformation

((\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b}}),\gamma^{\mathrm{univ}})
over
R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
, which means that for any other deformation
((\mathcal{A},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}}),\gamma)
over an object
R
of
\mathcal{C}_{W}^{\mathrm{reg}}
, there exists a unique morphism
h\colon R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\to R
such that
h^{\ast}((\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b}}),\gamma^{\mathrm{univ}})
is isomorphic to
((\mathcal{A},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}}),\gamma)
. Define
\omega_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}
to be the object of
\mathcal{G}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
associated to
(\mathcal{A}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}})
.

Lemma 3.2
.

For a morphism of reductive groups
f\colon\mathcal{G}_{1}\to\mathcal{G}
sending
\mu_{1}
to
\mu
, and
b_{1}
to
b
, the induced map
f\colon\Spf(R_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu_{1}})\to\Spf(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})
satisfies the following, with
\xi
an object of
\mathbf{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(\mathcal{G})
:

(\mathcal{A}_{b_{1}}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}_{b_{1}}^{\mathrm{univ}}})\times^{\mathcal{G}_{1,{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}\mathcal{G}_{{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}f^{\ast}(\mathcal{A}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}}),\quad f^{\ast}(\omega_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}(\xi))=\omega_{b_{1}}^{\mathrm{univ}}(\xi\circ f).

Proof.

It suffices to show the first isomorphism.
By
[
Ito23
, Theorem 6.1.3]
, it suffices to construct an isomorphism after evaluating at
(R_{\mathcal{G}_{1},\mu_{1}}\llbracket t\rrbracket,(p-t))
. By the construction in the proof of
[
Ito25
, Theorem 4.4.2]
, both evaluations are isomorphic to
\mathcal{Q}_{f(X_{1}^{\mathrm{univ}})}
, where
X_{1}^{\mathrm{univ}}
is
X^{\mathrm{univ}}
in the proof of
[
Ito25
, Theorem 4.4.2]
, when applied to
(\mathcal{G}_{W},\varphi_{b_{1}})
,
\mathcal{G}_{1}
and
\mu_{1}
.
∎

If
g
is in
\mathcal{G}(W)
then conjugation by
g^{-1}
induces an isomorphism
c_{g}\colon R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\to R_{\mathcal{G},g\mu g^{-1}}
and
c_{g}^{\ast}(\omega_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}})=\omega_{gbg^{-1}}^{\mathrm{univ}}
. If
b^{\prime}
is another element of
\mathcal{G}(W[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])
and
g
is an element of
\mathcal{G}(W)
such that
b^{\prime}=gb\phi(g)^{-1}
then left multiplication by
g
induces an isomorphism
(\mathcal{G}_{W},\varphi_{b})\to(\mathcal{G}_{W},\varphi_{b^{\prime}})
and thus there is an isomorphism
\omega_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\omega_{b^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{univ}}
. Thus, the pair
(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu},\omega_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}})
only depends, up to isomorphism, on the
\mathcal{G}(W)
-conjugacy class of
\mu
, and the
\sigma
-
\mathcal{G}(W)
-conjugacy class of
b
.

3.1.2.
Comparison to Faltings universal deformations with Tate tensors

We now wish to use the functor
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}
to compare the work of Ito to that of Faltings on universal deformations of
p
-divisible groups with Tate tensors in the reductive case. Below we use the notation for Dieudonné theory as in
[
IKY25
, §4]
. Most specifically,
\mathbb{D}(H)
denotes the filtered Dieudonné crystal of a
p
-divisible group
H
(see
[
IKY25
, Definition 4.6]
).

We use the notation
3.1.1
, but now assume that
k
is algebraically closed and
\mathcal{G}
is reductive. We write
\omega_{b,\mathrm{crys}}^{\mathrm{univ}}
for the composition
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ\omega_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}
, which is a
\mathcal{G}
-object in
\mathbf{VectF}^{\varphi}((R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})_{\mathrm{crys}})
.

3.1.2.1
Universal deformation of
p
-divisible groups

Fix a
p
-divisible group
H_{0}
over
k
, and choose any lift
\widetilde{H}_{0}
of
H_{0}
over
W
. Set
M_{0}\vcentcolon=\underline{\mathbb{D}}(H_{0})(W\twoheadrightarrow k)
, and let
b_{0}
in
\GL(M_{0}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])
such that
\sigma(b_{0})
corresponds to the Frobenius on
M_{0}
. From
\widetilde{H}_{0}
we obtain the Hodge filtration:

\Fil^{1}_{\widetilde{H}_{0},\mathrm{Hodge}}\subseteq\mathbb{D}(\widetilde{H}_{0})(\id\colon W\to W)=\mathbb{D}(H_{0})(W\twoheadrightarrow k).

From the Cartan decomposition, we know that
b_{0}
lies in
\mathcal{G}(W)\mu_{0}^{-1}(p)\mathcal{G}(W)
for a cocharacter
\mu_{0}\colon\mathbb{G}_{m,W}\to\GL(M_{0})
uniquely determined up to conjugacy. We take the unique cocharacter
\mu_{0}
such that
\Fil^{1}_{\widetilde{H}_{0},\mathrm{Hodge}}
is induced by
\mu_{0}
in the sense of
[
Kim18a
, Definition 2.2.1]
.

Choose an isomorphism
R_{\GL(M_{0}),\mu_{0}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}W\llbracket t_{1},\ldots,t_{d}\rrbracket
and equip it with the usual Frobenius
\phi_{0}
. From the above considerations we obtain the following data on
R_{\GL(M_{0}),\mu_{0}}
:

\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}\vcentcolon=R_{\GL(M_{0}),\mu_{0}}\otimes_{W}M_{0},\quad\Fil^{1}{\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}}\vcentcolon=1\otimes\Fil^{1}{M_{0}},\qquad\varphi_{\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}}\vcentcolon=u_{t}^{-1}\circ(1\otimes b_{0}),

where
u_{t}
corresponds to the tautological element of
\widehat{\mathcal{U}}_{\mu_{0}}(R_{\GL(M_{0}),\mu_{0}})
, and
\varphi_{\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}}
is considered as a map
\phi_{0}^{\ast}\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}\to\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}
. As explained in
[
Moo98
, §4.5]
, Faltings produced a (unique)
\varphi_{\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}}
-horizontal integrable connection
\nabla_{\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}}
on
\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}
with
(\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}},\nabla_{\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}})
a filtered Dieudonné crystal on
R_{\GL(M_{0}),\mu_{0}}
and so corresponds to an object
H^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b_{0}}
of
\mathrm{BT}_{p}(R_{\GL(M_{0}),\mu_{0}})
.

As explained in
[
Kim18a
, §3.3]
, this notation is not misleading as
H^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b_{0}}
is a universal deformation of
H_{0}
(in the sense that it (pro)represents the functor in
[
Kim18a
, Definition 3.1]
).

Proposition 3.3
.

There is a natural isomorphism

\omega_{b_{0},\mathrm{crys}}^{\mathrm{univ}}(M_{0})(R_{\GL(M_{0}),\mu_{0}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}(\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}},\Fil^{\bullet}_{\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}}).

Proof.

This follows from
[
IKY25
, Theorem 4.8]
and
[
Ito25
, Theorem 6.2.1]
.
∎

3.1.2.2
Deformations with Tate tensors

Fix a triple
(\mathcal{G},b,\mu)
as in §
3.1.1
. We assume that
(\mathcal{G},b,\mu)
is of
Hodge type
: there exists a faithful representation
\iota\colon\mathcal{G}\to\GL(\Lambda_{0})
such that
\iota\circ\mu
has only weights
0
and
1
. Set
(b_{0},\mu_{0})\vcentcolon=(\iota(b),\iota\circ\mu)^{\vee}
. Further fix isomorphisms

R_{\GL(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee}),\mu_{0}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}W\llbracket t_{1}\ldots,t_{d}\rrbracket,\qquad R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}W\llbracket s_{1},\ldots,s_{k}\rrbracket

such that the natural map
R_{\GL(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee}),\mu_{0}}\to R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
is Frobenius equivariant when the source and target are given the (usual) Frobenii induced by these isomorphisms.
21
21

21

This is possible, for instance, by the discussion in
[
Ito23
, §4.2]
, which shows that
U_{\mu}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Lie}(U_{\mu})
as
W
-schemes, and
\mathrm{Lie}(U_{\mu})
is a direct summand of
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathrm{GL}(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee}))
.
Finally, fix a tensor package (in the sense in
[
IKY24
, §A.5]
)
(\Lambda_{0},\mathds{T}_{0})
with
\mathcal{G}=\mathrm{Fix}(\mathds{T}_{0})
.

As explained in
[
Kim18a
, §2.5]
associated to
(\mathcal{G},b,\mu)
and
\iota
is a
p
-divisible group
H_{b_{0}}
over
k
together with an identification
\mathbb{D}(H_{b_{0}})(W)=\Lambda_{0}^{\vee}
where Frobenius acts by
b_{0}
. Moreover, under this identification the set
