\mathds{T}_{0}
is a set of tensors on
\mathbb{D}(H_{b_{0}})
as an
F
-crystal. Set

(\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}},\Fil^{1}_{\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}})\vcentcolon=(\mathbf{M}_{{b_{0}}}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}},\Fil^{1}_{\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}})\otimes_{R_{\GL(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee}),\mu_{0}}}R_{\mathcal{G},\mu},

and let
H_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}
be the pullback of
H_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}
to
R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
. Then,

\mathbb{D}(H_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}})(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})=(\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}},\Fil^{1}_{\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}},\nabla_{\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}}),

for some connection
\nabla_{\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}}
. Observe that
\mathds{T}_{0}
naturally defines a set of tensors on
\mathbf{M}_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}
and (by base change) on
\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}
, which we denote
\mathds{T}_{0}^{\mathrm{Fal},^{\prime}}
and
\mathds{T}_{0}^{\mathrm{Fal}}
, respectively. The tensors
\mathds{T}_{0}^{\mathrm{Fal}}
on
\mathbb{D}(H_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}})
are Frobenius equivariant and lie in the
0^{\text{th}}
-part of the filtration (see
[
Kim18a
, §3.5]
).

By work of Faltings (see
[
Kim18a
, Theorem 3.6]
)
H_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}
satisfies a universality property. Suppose that
R_{0}=W\llbracket u_{1},\ldots,u_{r}\rrbracket
for some
r
, and
X
is a
p
-divisible group over
R_{0}
deforming
H_{b_{0}}
. By the universality of
H_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}
, there exists a unique map
f_{X}\colon R_{\GL(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee}),\mu_{0}}\to R_{0}
such that
f_{X}^{\ast}(H_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}})
is isomorphic (as a deformation) to
X
. Then,
f_{X}
factorizes through
R_{\GL(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee}),\mu_{0}}\to R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
if and only if there exists tensors
\{t_{\alpha}\}
on
\mathbb{D}(X)(R_{0})
lifting those on
\mathds{T}_{0}
on
\mathbb{D}(H_{b_{0}})
, and which are Frobenus equivariant and lie in the
\text{0}^{\text{th}}
-part of the filtration. In this case
\{t_{\alpha}\}=f_{X}^{\ast}(\mathds{T}_{0}^{\mathrm{Fal,^{\prime}}})
.

Consider now the obvious morphism
f\colon(\mathcal{G},b,\mu)\to(\GL(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee}),b_{0},\mu_{0})
. Combining Lemma
3.2
and Proposition
3.3
, there is a canonical identification

\displaystyle\omega^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b,\mathrm{crys}}(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee})(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}f^{\ast}(\mathbb{D}(H_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}))(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{D}(H_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}})(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}},\Fil^{1}_{\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}}),

of naive filtered
F
-crystals.

Proposition 3.4
.

The isomorphism of naive filtered
F
-crystals on
R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}

\omega_{b,\mathrm{crys}}^{\mathrm{univ}}(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee})(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})\to(\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}},\Fil^{1}_{\mathbf{M}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}})

carries
\omega_{b,\mathrm{crys}}^{\mathrm{univ}}(\mathds{T}_{0})(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})
to
\mathds{T}_{0}^{\mathrm{Fal}}
.

Proof.

Under this identification
\omega_{b,\mathrm{crys}}^{\mathrm{univ}}(\mathds{T}_{0})(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})
constitutes a Frobenius-equivariant tensors lying in the
0^{\text{th}}
-part of the of the filtration and lifting those on
\mathbb{D}(H_{b_{0}})
. Thus, by the universality statement from above,
\omega_{b,\mathrm{crys}}^{\mathrm{univ}}(\mathds{T}_{0})(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})
must be equal to
f^{\ast}(\mathds{T}_{0}^{\mathrm{Fal},^{\prime}})=\mathds{T}_{0}^{\mathrm{Fal}}
.
∎

3.2.
Comparison to Shimura varieties

We now show that for the integral canonical model
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, the prismatic realization functor
\omega_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
recovers Ito’s universal prismatic
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-torsor with
F
-structure at the completion of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
at each point of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
. This may be seen as a prismatic refinement of
[
Kis17
, (1.3.9) Proposition]
, in the general abelian type setting.

Suppose that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type, and
x
is a point of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
. As in §
2.5
we have the associated element
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}\vcentcolon=\underline{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{x}
in
C(\mathcal{G}^{c})
. Additionally, choose an element
\mu^{c}_{h}
in our conjugacy class
\bbmu_{h}^{c}
of cocharacters
\mathbb{G}_{m,\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
.

Lemma 3.5
.

The element
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}
lies in the image of the map

\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\sigma(\mu_{h}^{c}(p))^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\to C(\mathcal{G}^{c}).

Proof.

If
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is of Hodge type this follows from
[
Kim18b
, Lemma 3.3.14]
. In the special-type case, we may assume that the torus in the Shimura datum is cuspidal following the argument given on
[
Dan22
, pp. 31–33]
. From there the claim follows from
[
KSZ21
, Proposition 4.3.14 and Corollary 4.4.12]
.

For the abelian type case, we take
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
,
(\mathbf{T},\{h\},\mathcal{T})
and
(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2})
as in Lemma
2.9
. The question is reduced to the case of
(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2})
by functoriality.
For the reduction to the case of
(\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{T},\mathbf{X}_{1}\times\{h\},\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T})
, it suffices to show the injectivity of

\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\backslash\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\to\mathcal{G}_{3}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\backslash\mathcal{G}_{3}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})/\mathcal{G}_{3}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}),

where we put
\mathcal{G}_{3}=\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T}
.
Fix a Borel pair
\mathcal{T}_{1}\subset\mathcal{B}_{1}\subset\mathcal{G}_{1}
. This gives Borel pairs
\mathcal{T}_{2}\subset\mathcal{B}_{2}\subset\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}
and
\mathcal{T}_{3}\subset\mathcal{B}_{3}\subset\mathcal{G}_{3}^{c}
by the constructions of
\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}
and
\mathcal{G}_{3}^{c}
.
The injectivity of this map of double cosets follows from the Cartan decomposition since the unipotent radials of
\mathcal{B}_{2}
and
\mathcal{B}_{3}
are same.
∎

Choose an element
b_{x}
in
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\mu^{c}_{h}(p)^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

such that
\sigma(b_{x})
maps to
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}
in
C(\mathcal{G}^{c})
.

Theorem 3.6
.

There exists an isomorphism
i_{x}\colon R_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu_{h}^{c}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}
such that

i_{x}^{\ast}(\omega_{b_{x}}^{\mathrm{univ}})\cong(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}})|_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}}.

Note while the pair
(R_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}_{h}},\omega_{b_{x}}^{\mathrm{univ}})
depends on the choice of
b_{x}
and
\mu_{h}^{c}
, the isomorphism type of the pair
(R_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}_{h}},\omega_{b_{x}}^{\mathrm{univ}})
does not, and therefore neither does the statement of Theorem
3.6
.

Proof of Theorem
3.6
.

We perform a devissage to the Hodge and special type cases.

Hodge type case

Let
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{GSp}(\mathbf{V}_{0}),\mathfrak{h}^{\pm},\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda_{0}))
be an integral Hodge embedding, and
\mathscr{A}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
the associated abelian scheme. Let
\mathds{T}_{0,p}^{\mathrm{crys}}
be the tensors on the filtered
F
-crystal

\mathcal{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\widehat{\mathscr{A}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}/\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})=\mathbb{D}(\mathscr{A}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}[p^{\infty}])

(see
[
BBM82
, (3.3.7.2)]
for this identification), as in §
2.6
. The triple
(\mathcal{G},b_{x},\mu_{h})

is of Hodge type relative to the embedding
\iota\colon\mathcal{G}\to\GL(\Lambda_{0})
. By work of Kisin, there exists an isomorphism
i_{x}\colon R_{\mathcal{G},\mu_{h}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}
together with an isomorphism
i_{x}^{\ast}(H_{b_{x}}^{\mathrm{univ}},\mathds{T}_{0}^{\mathrm{Fal}})\cong(\mathscr{A}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}[p^{\infty}],\mathds{T}_{0,p}^{\mathrm{crys}})_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}}
(see
[
Kim18b
, Proposition 4.1.6]
).
We claim that
i_{x}^{\ast}(\omega_{b_{x}}^{\mathrm{univ}})
is isomorphic to
\omega\vcentcolon=(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}})|_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}}
. We consider the following isomorphisms

\omega(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathcal{M}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}(\mathscr{A}[p^{\infty}]_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}i_{x}^{*}\mathcal{M}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}(H^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b_{x}})\xleftarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}i_{x}^{\ast}(\omega_{b_{x}}^{\mathrm{univ}})(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee}),

where the first isomorphism is from Theorem
2.12
, the second is the one obtained by applying
\mathcal{M}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
to the above isomorphism of Kisin, and the third one is obtained as follows. By
[
Ito25
, Theorem 6.2.1]
we have a canonical isomorphism
\mathcal{M}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}(H^{\mathrm{univ}}_{\iota(b_{x})^{\vee}})\xleftarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\omega_{\iota(b_{x})^{\vee}}^{\mathrm{univ}}(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee})
. Then, by Lemma
3.2
, we get the third isomorphism as the restriction of this isomorphism along

R_{\GL(\Lambda_{0}^{\vee}),\iota(\mu_{h})^{\vee}}\to R_{\mathcal{G},\mu_{h}}\xrightarrow{i_{x}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},x}}.

By
[
IKY24
, Proposition 1.28]
it suffices to show the above composite carries
\omega(\mathds{T}_{0})
to
i_{x}^{\ast}(\omega_{b_{x}}^{\mathrm{univ}})(\mathds{T}_{0})

It further suffices to show
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(\omega(\mathds{T}_{0}))(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu_{h}})
is matched to
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{crys}}(i_{x}^{\ast}(\omega_{b_{x}}^{\mathrm{univ}})(\mathds{T}_{0}))(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu_{h}})
as

\mathbf{Vect}((R_{\mathcal{G},\mu_{h}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})\to\mathbf{Vect}(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu_{h}}),\quad\mathcal{E}\mapsto\mathcal{E}^{\mathrm{crys}}(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu_{h}})

is faithful as follows from
[
dJ95
, Corollary 2.2.3]
and the second equivalence in
[
IKY24
, Equation (2.3.2)]
. But, this matching follows by combining Proposition
2.19
and Proposition
3.4
.

Special type case

Write
(\mathbf{T},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{T})
for the unramified Shimura datum. In this case
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is a disjoint union of schemes of the form
\Spec(\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}})
, for a finite unramified extension
E^{\prime}
of
E
(see
[
DY24
, Proposition 3.22]
), and so there is a tautological identification
R_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}_{h}}=\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}
. We show that under this identification that the prismatic
\mathcal{T}^{c}
-torsors with
F
-structure are matched.

By Remark
2.11
, Lemma
3.2
, and the argument given in
[
Dan22
, pp. 31–33]
we are reduced to showing the following. Let
\mathcal{T}=\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\,\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}}}
,
T\vcentcolon=\mathcal{T}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, and
\mu
the
\mathbb{Q}^{\mathrm{ur}}_{p}
-cocharacter of
T
with weights
(1,0,\ldots,0)
. Then,
b_{0}=(p^{-1},1,\ldots,1)
represents the unique class in the image of
\mathcal{T}(\breve{W})\mu^{-1}(p)\mathcal{T}(\breve{W})
.
Then, we must show that
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ\omega_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}
restricted to the inertia subgroup
\Gamma_{E^{\prime},0}
agrees with the Lubin–Tate character
\alpha_{0}\colon\Gamma_{E^{\prime},0}\to\mathcal{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(see the discussion before
[
Dan22
, Proposition 4.9]
), or equivalently that their compositions with embedding
\iota\colon\mathcal{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\GL(\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}})
are equal. But, as
(\mathcal{T},b_{0},\mu^{-1})
is the Lubin–Tate triple, we know by
[
Ito25
, Theorem 6.2.1]
that this
\omega^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b_{0}}(\iota)
is
\mathcal{M}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}(X_{\mathrm{LT}})
, if
X_{\mathrm{LT}}
is the
p
-divisible group with
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-structure over
\Spf(\breve{W})
coming from Lubin–Tate theory. Thus, the composition of the character
\Gamma_{E^{\prime},0}\to\mathcal{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\GL(\mathcal{O}_{E})
corresponding to
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ\omega_{b_{0}}^{\mathrm{univ}}
is
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathcal{M}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}(X_{\mathrm{LT}}))=T_{p}(X_{\mathrm{LT}})
(see
[
DLMS24
, Proposition 3.35]
). But, this is the composition of
\alpha_{0}
with
\mathcal{T}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\GL(\mathcal{O}_{E})
(see the proof of
[
Dan22
, Proposition 4.9]
).

Abelian type case

Let
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
be an unramified Shimura datum of Hodge type adapted to
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
. Consider the morphism of Shimura data obtained in Lemma
2.9
. Then, as the map
\alpha^{c}\colon(\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c})^{\mathrm{der}}\to(\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T}^{c})^{\mathrm{der}}
is an isogeny, it induces an isomorphism

R_{\mathcal{G}_{2},\mu_{h,2}^{c}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}R_{\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T}^{c},\mu_{h,1}^{c}\times\mu_{h,\mathcal{T}}^{c}}.

Moreover, for the same reason, for any
x_{2}
in
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{p}^{2}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
we obtain an induced isomorphism

\alpha^{c}\colon\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}},x_{2}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}\times\mathsf{K}_{\mathcal{T}}^{p}},(x_{1},x_{\mathcal{T}})},

as
\alpha_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2},\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is finite étale by Lemma
2.3
. Thus, we may use Lemma
3.2
, together with the claims in the case of Hodge and special type, to deduce the existence of an isomorphism
i_{x_{2}}\colon R_{\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c},\mu_{h,2}^{c}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}},x_{2}}

such that the prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-torsors with
F
-structure
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}|_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}},x_{2}}}
and
i_{x_{2}}^{\ast}(\omega_{b_{x_{2}}}^{\mathrm{univ}})
for
\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}
agree when pushed forward along
\alpha^{c}\colon\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}\to\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T}^{c}
. As they have further natural identifications when pushed forward to
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ab}}
, this implies they are isomorphic (see
[
DY24
, Proposition 2.1.6]
). Finally, as the morphism
\beta\colon\mathcal{G}_{2}^{\mathrm{der}}\to\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{der}}
is an isogeny, it again induces isomorphisms
R_{\mathcal{G}^{c}_{2},\mu_{h,2}^{c}}\to R_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu_{h}^{c}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}},x_{2}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}
, where
x
is the image of
x_{2}
. If
i_{x}\colon R_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}_{h}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}
is the resulting isomorphism, then there are isomorphisms between
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}|_{\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}}
and
i_{x}^{\ast}(\omega_{b_{x}}^{\mathrm{univ}})
. While arbitrary
x
may not be the image of such an
x_{2}
, we may reduce to this case by Lemma
2.4
and (
2.3.3
).
∎

We observe an important corollary of the above proof, which was used previously several times (see Theorem
2.14
and Theorem
2.18
).

Corollary 3.7
.

The prismatic realization functor
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
belongs to
\mathbf{Tors}_{\mathcal{G}^{c}}^{\varphi,-\mu_{h}^{c}}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
. In particular,
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
takes values in
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi,\mathrm{lff}}((\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that for each small open subset
\Spf(R)
of
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, and for every point
x
of the special fiber of
\Spf(R)
, there exists a
p
-adically etale neighborhood
\Spf(S)\to\Spf(R)
such that the Frobenius for
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
over
\mathfrak{S}_{S}
is in the double coset
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathfrak{S}_{S})\mu^{c}_{h}(E)^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathfrak{S}_{S})
.

By moving to an étale neighborhood if necessary, we may assume without loss of generality that the underlying
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-torsor is trivial on
\Spf(R)
. Write
g
for the element of
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\mathfrak{S}_{R}[1/E])
corresponding to the Frobenius for
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
on
\mathfrak{S}_{R}=R\llbracket t\rrbracket
. Consider the functor

F\colon\mathbf{Alg}_{R\llbracket t\rrbracket}\to\mathbf{Set},\quad A\mapsto\{(h,h^{\prime})\in\mathcal{G}^{c}(A)\times\mathcal{G}^{c}(A):hgh^{\prime}=\mu^{c}_{h}(E)^{-1}\in\mathcal{G}^{c}(A[1/E])\}.

Let
y
be the point of
\Spec(R\llbracket t\rrbracket)
equal to
(x,t)
, with the obvious meaning. Observe that we have the equality
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\Spec(R\llbracket t\rrbracket),y}=\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\Spf(R),x}\llbracket t\rrbracket
. Thus, as a result of Theorem
3.6
(and the description of the universal deformation in
[
Ito25
, Theorem 4.4.2]
), we have that
F(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\Spec(R\llbracket t\rrbracket),y})
is non-empty. The claim then follows from Artin approximation.

More precisely, first note that
R\llbracket t\rrbracket
is excellent (see
[
IKY24
, Proposition 1.12]
). Moreover, the functor
F
is clearly limit-preserving as
\mathcal{G}^{c}
is. Thus, by Artin approximation for an excellent base (see
[
AHR23
, Theorem 3.4]
), there exists some affine etale neighborhood
\Spec(B)\to\Spec(R\llbracket t\rrbracket)
containing
y
in its image and with
F(\Spec(B))
non-empty. Let
A
be the
(p,t)
-adic completion of
B
, so that
\Spf(A)\to\Spf(R\llbracket t\rrbracket)
is a
(p,t)
-adically etale neighborhood of
y
. Set
S=A/tA
. Then,
\Spf(S)\to\Spf(R)
is a
p
-adically etale map, and there is a unique deformation (by the topological invariance of the etale site of a formal scheme) to a
(p,t)
-adically etale map over
\Spf(R\llbracket t\rrbracket)
and, in fact, it must be
\Spf(S\llbracket t\rrbracket)
. Thus, in fact,
A=S\llbracket t\rrbracket
where
\Spf(S)\to\Spf(R)
is a
p
-adically etale neighborhood of
x
. Observe then that, by set-up,
F(S\llbracket t\rrbracket)
is non-empty, but this means precisely that the Frobenius is in the double coset of
\mu_{h}^{c}(E)^{-1}
over
S
as desired.
∎

3.3.
A prismatic characterization of integral models

Throughout this section we fix notation and conventions as in §
2
, and in particular fix
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
to be an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type.

3.3.1.
Characterization of integral canonical models

Throughout this subsection let us fix a neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Recall from §
2.5
that there exists a potentially crystalline locus
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\subseteq\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\mathrm{an}}
of
\nu_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
or, equivalently
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
.

Consider a smooth formal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-model
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
of
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, and a prismatic model
\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}
, i.e., an object of
\mathcal{G}^{c}\text{-}\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}((\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
with
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
isomorphic to
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}
. For
x
in
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
, there is an element
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}
in
C(\mathcal{G}^{c})
associated to the
F
-crystal with
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-structure given by
\underline{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ(\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{x}
. Fix
\mu_{h}^{c}
in
\bm{\mu}_{h}^{c}
. Then, we have the following property of
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}
.

Lemma 3.8
.

The element
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}
lies in the image of the map

\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\sigma(\mu_{h}^{c}(p))^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\to C(\mathcal{G}^{c}).

Proof.

As
\mathfrak{X}
is smooth over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, we know that the specialization map
\mathrm{sp}\colon|U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}|^{\mathrm{cl}}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
is surjective. Let
y
be a point of
|U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}|^{\mathrm{cl}}
such that
\mathrm{sp}(y)=x
. Then, a simple specialization argument shows that
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}
is equal to the element associated to the isocrystal with
\mathcal{G}
-structure associated to
\underline{\mathbb{D}}_{\mathrm{crys}}\circ(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}})_{y}
. But, the claim then follows from Lemma
3.5
.
∎

Choose an element
b_{x}
in
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\mu_{h}^{c}(p)^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

such that
\sigma(b_{x})
maps to
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}
in
C(\mathcal{G}^{c})
.

Definition 3.9
.

A
prismatic integral canonical model
of
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is a smooth and separated formal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-model
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
such that there exists a prismatic model
\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}
with the following property:
for each
\mathsf{K}^{p}
and each
x
in
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
there exists an isomorphism
\Theta_{x}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}\colon R_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}_{h}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}
such that
(\Theta_{x}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})^{*}(\omega_{b_{x}}^{\mathrm{univ}})
is isomorphic to the pullback of
\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
to
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}
.

We now aim to show that the unique prismatic integral canonical model of
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. Our proof relies on providing a slight extension of the fully-faithfulness portion of
[
GR24
, Theorem A]
to certain semi-stable formal schemes, mimicking the argument in
[
DLMS24
, Theorem 3.29]
.

To state this let us fix a complete discrete valuation ring
\mathcal{O}_{K}
with fraction field
K
and perfect residue field
k
. Set
W
to be
W(k)
, and fix a uniformizer
\varpi
of
K
.

Proposition 3.10
.

Set
R
to be
\mathcal{O}_{K}\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}\rrbracket/(x_{1}x_{2}\cdots x_{m}-\varpi)
, where
d
and
m
are integers with
1\leqslant m\leqslant d
. Then the étale realization functor

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\colon\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})\to\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(R[1/p])

is fully faithful.

As in
[
Ito23
]
, we consider the following Breuil–Kisin type prism. Let
\mathfrak{S}_{R}
be the ring
W\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}\rrbracket
equipped with a Frobenius lift
\phi
determined by
\phi(x_{i})=x_{i}^{p}
, and
E
in
\mathfrak{S}_{R}
be the polynomial
E_{\varpi}(x_{1}x_{2}\cdots x_{m})
, where
E_{\varpi}
is the minimal polynomial of
\varpi
relative to
\mathrm{Frac}(W)
. Then the pair
(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))
defines an object of
R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
.

Lemma 3.11
.

The object
(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))
covers the final object
\ast
of
\mathbf{Sh}(R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
.

Proof.

Similarly to
[
IKY24
, Proposition 1.16]
, the assertion follows from
[
IKY24
, Proposition 1.11]
(using
[
ALB23
, Proposition 5.8]
in place of
[
IKY24
, Lemma 1.15]
).
∎

Thus, we can regard a prismatic
F
-crystal on
R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
as a finite free Breuil–Kisin module equipped with a descent datum. More precisely, we let
\mathfrak{S}^{(1)}_{R}
be
(\mathfrak{S}_{R}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathfrak{S}_{R})\left\{\frac{J}{E}\right\}_{\delta}^{\wedge}
where

J\vcentcolon=\ker\left(\mathfrak{S}_{R}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathfrak{S}_{R}\to\mathfrak{S}_{R}\to\mathfrak{S}_{R}/(E)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}R\right).

As in
[
DLMS24
, Example 3.4]
, it represents the self-product of
\mathfrak{S}_{R}
over
\ast
in
\mathbf{Sh}(R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
.

For
i=1,\ldots,d
, let
\varepsilon_{i}
denote the product of
x_{j}
for
1\leqslant j\leqslant d
excluding
i
. Denote the ring

R[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varepsilon_{i}}}]_{p}^{\wedge}
by
R_{i}
, which
is a base ring in the sense in
[
IKY24
, §1.1.5]
. Using the map
\mathcal{O}_{K}\langle x_{j}^{\pm 1};j\neq i\rangle\to R_{i}
given by sending
x_{j}
to
x_{j}
as a formal framing, we obtain the relative Breuil–Kisin ring
\mathfrak{S}_{R_{i}}
which we denote by
\mathfrak{S}_{i}
. Then we have a morphism
(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))\to(\mathfrak{S}_{i},(E_{\varpi}))
sending
x_{i}
to
\tfrac{u}{\varepsilon_{i}}
and
x_{j}
to
x_{j}
for
j\neq i
.

We let
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},i}
) be the
p
-adic completion of
\mathfrak{S}_{R}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{E}}]
(resp.
\mathfrak{S}_{i}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{E_{\varpi}}}]
).
We use the following lemma to reduce Proposition
3.10
to the case of
R_{i}
.

Lemma 3.12
.

Let
\mathfrak{S}_{(i)}
denote the intersection
\mathfrak{S}_{i}\cap\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
in the ring
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},i}
. Then the inclusion
\mathfrak{S}_{R}\subseteq\bigcap_{i=1}^{d}\mathfrak{S}_{(i)}
in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
is an equality.

Proof.

We put
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}\vcentcolon=\bigcap_{i=1}^{d}\mathfrak{S}_{(i)}
. Since
\mathfrak{S}_{R}
and
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
are
p
-adically complete, it suffices to show that the containment
\mathfrak{S}_{R}\subseteq\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}
is an equality modulo
p
.
We consider the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathfrak{S}_{R}/(p)=k\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}\rrbracket\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(k\llbracket x_{j};j\neq i\rrbracket[\nicefrac{{1}}{{x_{j}}};j\neq i]\right)\llbracket u\rrbracket=\mathfrak{S}_{i}/(p)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}/(p)=k\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}\rrbracket[\nicefrac{{1}}{{x_{1}\cdots x_{m}}}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left(k\llbracket x_{j};j\neq i\rrbracket[\nicefrac{{1}}{{x_{j}}};j\neq i]\right)\llbracket u\rrbracket[\nicefrac{{1}}{{u}}]=\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},i}/(p),}

in which all the maps are injective. Setting
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{(i)}
to be the intersection of
\mathfrak{S}_{i}/(p)
and
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}/(p)
in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},i}/(p)
, we have
\mathfrak{S}_{R}/(p)=\bigcap_{i=1}^{d}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{(i)}
.
We claim that the induced surjection
\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}/(p)\to\bigcap_{i=1}^{d}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{(i)}
is an isomorphism.
This is equivalent to the equality

p\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\cap\bigcap_{i}\mathfrak{S}_{(i)}=p\cdot\bigcap_{i}\mathfrak{S}_{(i)}.

We note that the right hand side is equal to
\bigcap_{i}(p\mathfrak{S}_{(i)})
as
p
is a nonzerodivisor in
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}
. Hence, it suffices to show the equality
p\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}\cap\mathfrak{S}_{(i)}=p\mathfrak{S}_{(i)}
for all
i
. But this follows from the injectivity of the right vertical map in the above diagram.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.10
.

By the proof of Lemma
3.11
the faithfulness portion of the claim is reduced to the case of a perfectoid base, which is clear. Thus, it suffices to check fullness. Let
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
be two objects of
\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
and let
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathcal{F})\to T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\mathcal{F})
be a morphism in
\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(R[1/p])
, which, by
[
BS23
, Corollary 3.7]
, corresponds to a morphism
\mathcal{F}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}]_{p}^{\wedge}\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}]_{p}^{\wedge}
of prismatic Laurent
F
-crystals on
R_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
(cf.
[
IKY24
, §2.2]
). Let
\mathfrak{M}
and
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathcal{M}
and
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
) denote the evaluation of
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathcal{F}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}]_{p}^{\wedge}
and
\mathcal{F}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}]_{p}^{\wedge}
) at the prism
(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))
.

Since the étale realization functor

\mathbf{Vect}^{\varphi}(R_{i,{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}})\to\mathbf{Vect}(R_{i,{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}},\mathcal{O}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}]_{p}^{\wedge})\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathbf{Loc}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(R_{i}[1/p])

is fully faithful by
[
DLMS24
, Theorem 3.29 (1)]
, the restriction of
\mathcal{F}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}]_{p}^{\wedge}\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}]_{p}^{\wedge}
to
R_{i,{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
induces a morphism
\mathcal{F}|_{R_{i,{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}\to\mathcal{F}^{\prime}|_{R_{i,{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}
.
In particular, the map

\mathcal{M}_{i}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{M}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},i}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}_{i}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{M}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E}}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{E},i}

sends
\mathfrak{M}_{i}\vcentcolon=\mathfrak{M}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}\mathfrak{S}_{i}
into
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}_{i}\vcentcolon=\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathfrak{S}}\mathfrak{S}_{i}
.
Then, by Lemma
3.12
, we get that the map
\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
sends
\mathfrak{M}
into
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
.

Since the prism
(\mathfrak{S}_{R},(E))
is a cover of the final object by Lemma
3.11
, it suffices to show that the map
\mathfrak{M}\to\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
is compatible with the descent data for
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
.
To see this, we observe that the natural map
\mathfrak{S}_{R}^{(2)}\to\mathfrak{S}_{R}^{(2)}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{E}}]_{p}^{\wedge}
is injective. Indeed, it can be checked after passing modulo
p
, where it is reduced to showing that
E
is a nonzerodivisor in
\mathfrak{S}_{R}^{(2)}/(p)
, which follows from the flatness of
\mathfrak{S}_{R}\to\mathfrak{S}_{R}^{(2)}
, cf.
[
DLMS24
, Lemma 3.5]
). Thus, the assertion follows from the compatibility of the map
\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
with the descent data for
\mathcal{F}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}]_{p}^{\wedge}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{\mathcal{I}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}]_{p}^{\wedge}
.
∎

We are now ready to prove our uniqueness claim concerning prismatic integral canonical models of
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. We roughly follow the strategy employed in
[
Pap23
, Theorem 7.1.7]
, with some key differences owing to the more formal geometry and
p
-adic Hodge theory nature of our setup.

Theorem 3.13
.

The unique prismatic integral canonical model of
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
.

Proof.

That
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is a prismatic integral canonical model of
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
follows from combining Theorem
2.10
, Proposition
2.15
, Proposition
3.6
. Thus, it suffices to show that if
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
and
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
are two prismatic integral canonical models of
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, then they are isomorphic.

Denote by
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
the normalization of
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\times_{\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{E})}\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
in
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. More precisely, we set
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
to be the relative formal spectrum
\underline{\Spf}(\mathcal{A})\to\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\times_{\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{E})}\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, where
\mathcal{A}
is the integral closure of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\times_{\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{E})}\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
in
s_{\ast}(\mathcal{O}_{U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}^{+})
, where
s\colon(U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathsf{U}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})\to\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\times_{\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{E})}\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is the composition of the following map of locally ringed spaces

(U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathcal{O}_{U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}^{+})\xrightarrow{\Delta}(U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\times_{\Spa(E)}U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\mathcal{O}_{U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\times_{\Spa(E)}U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}^{+})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{sp}}\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\times_{\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{E})}\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}.

As
E
is a discrete valuation field, and therefore the local rings of each formal scheme and rigid space are excellent, these normalizations are finite over their original base and so topologically of finite type and normal (cf.
[
SP
,
Tag 0AVK
]
and
[
SP
,
Tag 035L
]
). Let
\pi\colon\mathfrak{X}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
and
\pi^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{X}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
be the natural projection maps. We show that
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
are isomorphisms.

To prove this, fix a point
x^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
and let
x
and
x^{\prime}
be their images in
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
and
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, respectively. Let
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}
and
\bm{b}_{x^{\prime},\mathrm{crys}}
be as in the definition of a prismatic integral canonical model. Observe that
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}
actually equals
\bm{b}_{x^{\prime},\mathrm{crys}}
. Indeed, from the diagram of isomorphisms

\Spec(k(x))\xleftarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\Spec(k(x^{\prime\prime}))\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\Spec(k(x^{\prime})),

and the identification of
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
and
T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\prime}
with
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}
, we obtain an isomorphism

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ(\zeta_{\mathsf{K}p})_{x^{\prime\prime}}\cong T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ(\zeta^{\prime}_{\mathsf{K}p})_{x^{\prime\prime}},

from where the claim follows by
[
GR24
, Theorem A]
. Denote this common class of
\mathbf{b}_{x}
,
\mathbf{b}_{x^{\prime}}
by
\mathbf{b}_{x^{\prime\prime},\mathrm{crys}}
, and choose an element
b_{x^{\prime\prime}}
of
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\mu_{h}^{c}(p)^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
such that
\sigma(b_{x^{\prime\prime}})
maps to
\mathbf{b}_{x^{\prime\prime},\mathrm{crys}}
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
,
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
, and
\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime}
be the complete local rings of
x
,
x^{\prime}
, and
x^{\prime\prime}
of their respective formal schemes. By excellence each of these complete local rings is normal and formally of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{E}

(see
[
SP
,
Tag 0C23
]
). Choose isomorphisms
\Theta_{x}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
and
\Theta^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}_{x^{\prime}}
as in the definition of a prismatic integral canonical model. We claim that the following diagram commutes:

(3.3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spf(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spf(\mathcal{O})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spf(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spf(R_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu_{h}^{c}}).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{x}^{{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{x^{\prime}}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}

To prove this, we first make the following observation.

Claim:
There exists an epimorphism of formal schemes of the form
\Spf(R)\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})
, where

R=\mathcal{O}_{K}\llbracket t_{1},\ldots,t_{n},x_{1},\ldots,x_{m}\rrbracket/(x_{1}\cdots x_{m}-\varpi),

with notation as in Proposition
3.10
.

Proof.

Let
\Spf(A)
be an affine open neighborhood of
x^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. As
\Spf(A)_{\eta}
is an open subset of the smooth rigid space
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, it is smooth, and so by Elkik’s algebraization theorem (see
[
Elk73
, Théorème 7]
) there exists some finite type smooth morphism
\Spec(B)\to\Spec(\mathcal{O}_{E})
such that
A
is isomorphic to the
p
-adic completion of
B
over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. Let
f\colon Y\to\Spec(B)
be a strictly semi-stable over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
(for some finite extension
K
of
E
) alteration of
\Spec(B)
as in
[
dJ96
, Theorem 6.5]
, and choose any closed point
y
of of
Y
mapping to
x^{\prime\prime}
. Then
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y,y}
is isomorphic to

\mathcal{O}_{K}\llbracket x_{1},\ldots,x_{d}\rrbracket/(x_{1}\cdots x_{m}-\varpi)

over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
for some integers
d
and
m
with
1\leqslant m\leqslant d
. We then claim that the induced map
f\colon\Spf(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y,y})\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})
is an epimorphism, from where the conclusion will follow. But, the map
f\colon\Spec(\mathcal{O}_{Y,y})\to\Spec(B_{x})
is dominant by assumption, and thus induces an injection
B_{x}\to\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}
. As both the source and target are regular local rings we deduce from
[
GD71
, I, Corollaire 3.9.8]
that
\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y,y}
is an injection. Since
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y,y}
and
\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime}
are complete local rings, the claim follows.
∎

To prove that Equation (
3.3.1
) commutes, it thus suffices to show that the outer square of the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spf(R)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spf(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spf(\mathcal{O})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spf(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spf(R_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu_{h}^{c}}),}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{x}^{{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{x^{\prime}}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}

where
f
and
f^{\prime}
are defined to make the triangle diagrams they sit in commute. But, observe that as
R
is a complete regular local ring, it suffices by the universality condition of
\Spf(R_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu_{h}^{c}})
, and our definition of a prismatic integral canonical models, to show that
f^{\ast}(\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})
is isomorphic to
(f^{\prime})^{\ast}(\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\prime})
. But, by setup we know that

T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ f^{\ast}(\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})\cong T_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}\circ(f^{\prime})^{\ast}(\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\prime}),

and so the claim follows from Proposition
3.10
.

Given the commutativity of (
3.3.1
), we can now argue as in
[
Pap23
, Proposition 6.3.1 (b)]
to show that
\pi\colon\Spf(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})\to\Spf(\mathcal{O})
and
\pi^{\prime}\colon\Spf(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
are isomorphisms. Indeed, the commutativity of (
3.3.1
) is equivalent to
\pi\otimes\pi^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{O}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\breve{E}}}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime}
factorizing through the map

\mathcal{O}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}_{\breve{E}}}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{a\otimes\id_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\widehat{\otimes}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{\breve{E}}}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\Delta}\mathcal{O}^{\prime},

where
a
denotes the isomorphism
(\Theta^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}_{x^{\prime}})^{-1}\circ\Theta^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}_{x}
. Let
R
denote the image of
\pi\otimes\pi^{\prime}
. Then, we see that this factorization gives rise to a surjection
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\to R
.

We claim that
\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime}
has the same Krull dimension as
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
. Observe that
\dim(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})=\dim(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime\prime}})
and
\dim(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})=\dim(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}^{\prime},x^{\prime}})
. As these are closed points on integral formal schemes of finite type over
\mathcal{O}_{K}
, they have the same dimension as
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathfrak{X}^{\prime}
, respectively. But,
\dim(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime\prime})
and
\dim(\mathfrak{X}^{\prime})
each decrease by
1
when passing to the rigid generic fiber, but these generic fibers are isomorphic.

On the other hand, the dimension of
R
is equal to the dimension of
\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime}
, as
R\to\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime}
is an integral embedding (see
[
SP
,
Tag 00OK
]
). Thus, combining these two claims we deduce that the dimension of
R
and
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
are the same. Thus, the surjection
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\to R
must be an isomorphism, being a surjection of integral domains of the same finite dimension.

We then get a finite map
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}R\to\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime}
. We claim that this map is an isomorphism. This follows from taking
A=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}^{\prime},x^{\prime}}
and
B=\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}^{\prime\prime},x^{\prime\prime}}
in the following lemma.

Lemma 3.14
.

Let
(A,\mathfrak{m})
and
(B,\mathfrak{n})
be normal local Noetherian rings flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
. Suppose that
(A,\mathfrak{m})\to(B,\mathfrak{n})
satisfies: (1)
\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}\to\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is finite, (2)
A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to B[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
is an isomorphism. Then,
\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}\to\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

As
\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}\to\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is a finite map between normal domains, it suffices to show that the map
\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
is an isomorphism. Let us begin by observing that the map
A\to B
is automatically injective as the source and target are both
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
-flat and the map
A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to B[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
is injective. As
(A,\mathfrak{m})
is a normal domain, we deduce from
[
GD71
, I, Corollaire 3.9.8]
that
\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}\to\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is injective, and thus that
\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
is injective. Thus, it suffices to show that
\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
is surjective. But, observe that as
A[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to B[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
is an isomorphism that the map
\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to(\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{A}B)[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]
is an isomorphism. As one has a factorization

\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to(\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{A}B)[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}]\to\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}],

with the second map being the obvious one, it suffices to show that the map
\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{A}B\to\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is surjective. But, by Nakayama’s lemma, using the fact that
\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is a finite
\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}
-module, it suffices to show this surjectivity modulo
\mathfrak{m}
. But, as
\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is a finite
\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}
-module, its topology agrees with the
\mathfrak{m}
-adic one. Thus, one has that
B/\mathfrak{m}B
is naturally equal to
\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}/\mathfrak{m}\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}
, and thus the surjectivity of
\widehat{A}_{\mathfrak{m}}\otimes_{A}B\to\widehat{B}_{\mathfrak{n}}
modulo
\mathfrak{m}
is clear.
∎

From the above we deduce that the map
\pi^{\prime}\colon\Spf(\mathcal{O}^{\prime\prime})\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}^{\prime})
is an isomorphism and, by symmetry, the same holds for
\pi
. We are then done by Lemma
3.15
below.
∎

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
\alpha\colon\mathfrak{Y}_{1}\to\mathfrak{Y}_{2}
be a morphism of finite type flat formal
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-schemes such that: (a)
\alpha_{\eta}
is an isomorphism of rigid
E
-spaces, (b) for every point
y_{1}
of
\mathfrak{Y}_{1}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
with
y_{2}=\alpha(y_{1})
the induced map
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{2},y}\to\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathfrak{Y}_{1},y_{1}}
is an isomorphism. Then,
\alpha
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

For each
n\geqslant 0
let
\alpha_{n}\colon\mathfrak{Y}_{1,n}\to\mathfrak{Y}_{2,n}
denote the reduction of
\alpha
modulo
p^{n+1}
. It suffices to show that
\alpha_{n}
is an isomorphism for all
n
. Indeed, we first observe that as the flat locus of each
\alpha_{n}
is open, and contains every closed point of the scheme
\mathfrak{Y}_{1,n}
, which are evidently dense by consideration of the variety
\mathfrak{Y}_{1,0}
, we deduce that
\alpha_{n}
is flat. Thus, to prove that it’s étale, it suffices to prove this claim for
\alpha_{0}
(see
[
SP
,
Tag 06AG
]
). But, in this case the fact that (b) implies
\alpha
is étale is classical. To prove that
\alpha_{0}
, and thus each
\alpha_{n}
(see loc. cit.), is an isomorphism it suffices to show that the fiber over each
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-point
y_{2}
of
\mathfrak{Y}_{2}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
is a singleton (see
[
SP
,
Tag 02LC
]
). But, as
\mathfrak{Y}_{2}
is a finite type and flat over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, the specialization map
\mathrm{sp}\colon|(\mathfrak{Y}_{2})_{\eta}|^{\mathrm{cl}}\to\mathfrak{Y}_{2,0}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
is surjective. Thus, there exists some finite extension
E^{\prime}
of
E
and a morphism
\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}})\to\mathfrak{Y}_{2}
whose special fiber is the underlying point of
y_{2}
. As
\alpha
is étale, we know that
\alpha^{-1}(y_{2})
is a disjoint union of copies of
\Spec(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
, and so by the topological invariance of the étale site, this implies that
\mathfrak{Y}_{1}\times_{\mathfrak{Y}_{2}}\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}})
is a disjoint union of copies of
\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{E^{\prime}})
. As
\alpha_{\eta}
is an isomorphism though, this number of copies must be one. The claim follows.
∎

There is also a characterization of the scheme
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
itself, using the notion of a prismatic
F
-crystal
(\omega^{\prime},\zeta,\iota)
on a
p
-adic scheme as in
[
IKY24
, §3.3.4]
, which we use freely below.

Definition 3.16
.

Let
X
be a finite type separated
E
-scheme,
U\subseteq X^{\mathrm{an}}
an open adic subspace, and
\omega_{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
a
\mathcal{G}
-object in de Rham
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local systems on
X^{\mathrm{an}}
(resp.
X
).

•

A locally of finite type separated flat
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-scheme
\mathscr{X}
is a
model
of
(X,U)
if there is an isomorphism
X\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathscr{X}_{E}
carrying
U
isomorphically onto
\widehat{\mathscr{X}}_{\eta}
.

•

A
prismatic model (of type
\bm{\mu}
)
of
\omega
(resp.
\omega^{\mathrm{an}}
) is a
\mathcal{G}
-object in prismatic
F
-crystals (one of type
\bm{\mu}
)
(\omega^{\prime},\zeta,\iota)
on
\mathscr{X}
with
\omega^{\prime}
isomorphic to
\omega
(resp.
\omega^{\mathrm{an}}
).

We often identify a prismatic model
(\omega^{\prime},\zeta,\iota)
of
\omega
with just its prismatic
F
-crystal component on
\widehat{\mathscr{X}}
, i.e., with just
\zeta
. So, we informally speak of
\zeta
being a prismatic
F
-crystal model of
\omega
.

Definition 3.17
.

A smooth and separated
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-model
\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is called a
prismatic integral canonical model
if it is a model of
(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}},U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})
and there exists a prismatic model
\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
of
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
such that for every point
x
of
\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
there exists an isomorphism
\Theta_{x}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}\colon R_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu^{c}_{h}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}
such that
(\Theta_{x}^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})^{*}(\omega_{b_{x}}^{\mathrm{univ}})
is isomorphic to the pullback of
\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
to
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x}
.

Said differently, a smooth separated
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-model
\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is a prismatic integral canonical model if
\widehat{\mathscr{X}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is a prismatic integral canonical model of
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
.

The following is an immediately corollary of
[
IKY24
, Proposition 3.6]
and Theorem
3.13
.

Corollary 3.18
.

The unique prismatic integral canonical model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
.

3.3.2.
Relationship to work of Pappas and Rapoport

We now discuss the relationship between our work and that in
[
PR24
]
. Below we shall refer to the conjunction of
[
PR24
, Conjecture 4.2.2]
and
[
Dan22
, Conjecture 4.5]
as the
Pappas–Rapoport conjecture
.

We begin by formulating a version of a prismatic integral canonical model as in Definition
3.17
, but for the entirety of the system
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. Namely, by a
smooth
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-model
of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}\mathsf{K}_{0}}\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
, we mean a collection
\{\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
of separated smooth
\mathcal{O}_{E}
-schemes together with finite étale morphisms
f_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathsf{K}^{p^{\prime}}}(g^{p})
modeling
t_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathsf{K}^{p^{\prime}}}(g^{p})
.

Definition 3.19
.

A
prismatic integral canonical model
of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is a smooth
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
-model
\{\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
such that
\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is a prismatic integral canonical model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
for all
\mathsf{K}^{p}
.

We have the following which is an essentially trivial corollary of Theorem
3.13
.

Theorem 3.20
.

The system
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is the unique prismatic integral canonical model of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
.

That said, independent of Theorem
3.13
we can show a prismatic integral canonical model satisfies the conditions of the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture.

Proposition 3.21
.

Suppose
\{\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is a prismatic integral canonical model of
\{\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}\mathsf{K}_{0}}\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. Then,
\{\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
satisfies the conditions of the Pappas–Rapoport conjecture.

Proof.

That condition (a) of
[
Dan22
, Conjecture 4.5]
holds for
\{\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
follows by combining
[
IKY24
, Proposition 3.6]
and the Néron–Ogg–Shafarevich criterion (see Lemma
3.22
below). To show that condition (b) of loc. cit. holds, set
(\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},\varphi_{\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}})=T_{\mathrm{sht}}(\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})
, with notation as in
[
IKY24
, §3.3]
, an object of
\mathcal{G}^{c}\text{-}\Sht_{\bm{\mu}_{h}^{c}}(\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})
. By definition, we have that
(\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{E}=U_{\mathrm{sht}}(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}^{\mathrm{an}})\cong\mathscr{P}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},E}
, and thus condition (b) is satisfied. Finally, to verify condition (c) of loc. cit., it suffices by setup to show that there exists an isomorphism

\Theta_{x}\colon\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{(\mathcal{G}^{c},b_{x},\mu_{h}^{c})/x_{0}}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\Spd(R_{\mathcal{G}^{c},\mu_{h}^{c}}),

with the property that
T_{\mathrm{sht}}(\Theta_{x}^{\ast}(\omega_{b_{x}}^{\mathrm{univ}}))
is isomorphic to the universal shutka. Here
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{(\mathcal{G}^{c},b_{x},\mu_{h}^{c})}
is the integral moduli space of shtukas as in
[
SW20
, Definition 25.1]
, and
\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{(\mathcal{G}^{c},b_{x},\mu_{h}^{c})/x_{0}}
is the completion at the neutral point
x_{0}
in the sense of
[
Gle22
]
. But, the existence of such an isomorphism follows from
[
Ito25
, Theorem 5.3.5]
and its proof.
∎

Lemma 3.22

(Néron–Ogg–Shafarevich criterion)
.

Let
R
be a discrete valuation ring over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
of mixed characteristic
(0,p)
. For an element
\{x_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}
of
\varprojlim_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K^{p}}}(R[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])
, each induced morphism

x_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\mathrm{an}}\colon\Spa(R[\nicefrac{{1}}{{p}}])\to\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\mathrm{an}}

factorizes through
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
.

Proof.

As
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}
satisfies the extension property, there exists a unique element
\{y_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}
in
\varprojlim_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R)
with
(y_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\eta}=x_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. Let
\widehat{y}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon\Spf(\widehat{R})\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
denote the completion of
y_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. Then, we observe that
x_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}^{\mathrm{an}}=(\widehat{y}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\eta}
. But,
(\widehat{y}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\eta}
takes values in
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
by Proposition
2.15

∎

Remark 3.23
.

The usage of the system
\{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\}
and its extension property in the proof of Lemma
3.22
is not strictly necessary. One could also use the method in the proof of Proposition
2.15
to reduce to the Siegel-type case, and thus to the classic Néron–Ogg–Shafarevich theorem.

3.4.
A syntomic characterization of integral models and Serre–Tate theory

We now discuss how the material from §
3.2
can be upgraded to the realm of prismatic
F
-gauges. We then use this to realize an expectation of Drinfeld and in doing so produce a syntomic characterization of integral canonical models and an analogue of the Serre–Tate theorem.

3.4.1.
The stack
\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}

We begin by recalling Gardner–Madapusi–Mathew’s representability of the moduli space of prismatic
F
-gauges with
\mathcal{G}
-structure of type
\mu
, which is based off of previous ideas of Drinfeld as in
[
Dri24a
]
.

Notation 3.24
.

Fix notation as in Notation
3.1
and additionally set
P_{-\mu}\subseteq\mathcal{G}
to be the parabolic given by the dynamic method for the cocharacter
\mu^{-1}
(see
[
Con14
, Theorem 4.1.7]
).

For a bounded
p
-adic ring
R
and an element
n
of
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
, the notion of an
n
-truncated prismatic
F
-gauge with
\mathcal{G}
-structure of type
\mu
over
R
is defined as in Definition
1.32
.

Definition 3.25

(cf.
[
GM24
]
)
.

A
p
-adically complete ring
R
has
p
-finite differentials
if the
R/p
-module
\Omega^{1}_{(R/p)/\mathbb{F}_{p}}
is finitely generated.

For example, this condition is satisfied when
R
is a base
W
-algebra (see
[
dJ95
, Lemma 1.3.3]
),
R
is an object of
\mathcal{C}_{W}
,
k
is a characteristic
p
field with finite
p
-basis (see loc. cit. for the definition), or
R
is perfectoid. We let
\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{p\text{-fin}}
denote the category of
p
-adically complete rings with
p
-finite differentials, which we equip with the flat topology.

Definition 3.26

(cf.
[
GM24
]
)
.

For
n
in
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
we consider the derived prestack

\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{n}\colon\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{p\text{-fin},\mathrm{op}}\to\mathbf{Grpd}_{\infty},\qquad R\mapsto\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{n}(R),

of
n
-truncated prismatic
F
-gauges with
\mathcal{G}
-structure of type
\mu
.

We then have the following remarkable theorem of Gardner–Mathew. In the following, we use the notion of Weil restriction as in
[
GM24
]
: for a prestack
Y
on
\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{p\text{-fin}}
, set
Y^{(n)}
to be the prestack on
\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{p\text{-fin}}
given by
R\mapsto Y(R\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
.

Theorem 3.27

(
[
GM24
]
)
.

Fix
n
in
\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}
.

(1)

If
n
is finite, the prestack
\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{n}
is a quasi-compact smooth
p
-adic formal Artin stack of dimension
0
with affine diagonal.

(2)

For finite
n
, the natural map
\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{n+1}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{n}
is smooth and surjective.

(3)

[Grothendieck–Messing theory]
For
(R^{\prime}\to R,\gamma)
a (nilpotent) divided power thickening in
\mathbf{Alg}^{p\emph{-fin}}_{W}
there is a Cartesian diagram of groupoids

(3.4.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{n}(R^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{BP_{-\mu}^{(n)}(R^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{n}(R)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X^{\mu}_{n}(R^{\prime}\to R)}}

where
X^{\mu}_{n}(R^{\prime}\to R)\vcentcolon=B\mathcal{G}^{(n)}(R^{\prime})\times_{B\mathcal{G}^{(n)}(R)}BP_{-\mu}^{(n)}(R)
.

Proof.

The only thing not addressed by
[
GM24
]
is Grothendieck–Messing theory for
n=\infty
. But, as a limit of Cartesian diagrams is a Cartesian diagram, this follows by passing to the limit: for a(n affine smooth)
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-group scheme
\mathcal{H}
and a
p
-complete ring
R
, the natural maps
B\mathcal{H}(R)\to B\mathcal{H}(\Spf(R))\to\lim_{n}B\mathcal{H}^{(n)}(R)
are isomorphisms (see
[
HLP23
, Proposition 2.1.4]
).
∎

In diagram (
3.4.1
), the vertical maps are the natural ones, the top horizontal map is the restriction along the Nygaard de Rham point (see e.g., see
[
IKY25
, Definition 1.11]
), and the lower horizontal map is such that the projection, denoted
\alpha_{\gamma}
, to
B\mathcal{G}^{(n)}(R^{\prime})
is constructed using the map
\widetilde{x}_{\mathrm{dR},\gamma}\colon\Spec(R^{\prime})\to R^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
from
[
GM24
, Lemma 6.8.1]
. The map
\widetilde{x}_{\mathrm{dR},\gamma}\colon\Spf(R^{\prime})\to R^{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
is an extension of prismatic de Rham point (e.g., see
[
IKY25
, Definition 1.11]
). The extension
\widetilde{x}_{\mathrm{dR},\gamma}
depends on
\gamma
.

From Theorem
3.27
we see that
\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}=\varprojlim\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{n}
is the projective limit of smooth quasi-compact
p
-adic formal Artin stacks, with smooth surjective transition maps. This is just as in the case of the formal
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-stack
\mathrm{BT}^{h,d}_{p,\infty}
of
p
-divisible groups of height
h
and dimension
d
, which is the limit of the smooth Artin formal stack over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
s
\mathrm{BT}_{p,n}^{h,d}
of
n
-truncated
p
-divisible groups of height
h
and dimension
d
(cf.
[
Wed01
, §1]
). This is reasonable as the following result shows.

Theorem 3.28

(
[
GM24
, Theorem A]
,
[
Mon24
, Theorem 1.11]
)
.

Let
\mu_{d}
be the cocharacter
(1^{d},0^{h-d})
of
\GL_{h,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
. Then, there is a canonical isomorphism

\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{syn}}\colon\mathrm{BT}^{h,d}_{p,\infty}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.68887pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathrm{BT}^{\GL_{h,\mathbb{Z}_{p}},\mu_{d}}_{\infty}

which agrees with Anschutz–Le Bras’s filtered Dieudonné functor
\mathcal{M}_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}}
(cf.
[
ALB23
]
and specifically
[
ALB23
, Remark 1.9]
, as well as
[
Mon24
, Proposition 3.45]
) on qrsp rings
R
. Moreover, the functor
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{syn}}
preserves duals.

Example 3.29

(see
[
GM24
, §11.6]
)
.

Let us say that
(\mathcal{G},\mu)
is of
Siegel type
if it is of the form
(\mathrm{GSp}(\Lambda_{0}),\mu_{g})
for some symplectic
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice
\Lambda_{0}
of rank
2g
. For a
p
-nilpotent ring
R
, we then define
p\text{-}\mathrm{Div}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}(R)
to be the groupoid of
quasi-polarized
p
-divisible groups
over
R
. By definition, such a quasi-polarized
p
-divisible group is a triple
(H,L,\lambda)
where
H
is a
p
-divisible group over
R
,
L
is a rank
1

\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system on
R
, and
\lambda\colon H\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}H^{\vee}\otimes L
such that under the canonical double-duality isomorphism
H\simeq H^{\vee\vee}
we have that
\lambda^{\vee}
corresponds to
-\lambda
.
22
22

22

Note that precisely
H\otimes L
denotes the tensor product in fppf
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-modules, which is still a
p
-divisible group as this can be checked locally, which reduces us to the case when
L
is trivial.

One may then deduce from from Theorem
3.28
that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{syn}}
induces an equivalence of categories
\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}(R)\to p\text{-}\mathrm{Div}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}(R)
given by
\mathcal{P}\mapsto(H,L,\lambda)
. More precisely,
(H,L)
is uniquely defined so that
f_{\ast}\mathcal{P}=(\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{syn}}(H),\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{syn}}(L))
where
f\colon\mathcal{G}\to\GL(\Lambda_{0})\times\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
is the tautological map, and where in the expression
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{syn}}(L)
we are abusing notation and implicitly identifying
L
with its associated étale
p
-divisible group.

3.4.2.
Deformation theory of prismatic
\mathcal{G}
-
F
-gauges

We now show that Ito’s universal deformation
(\mathcal{A}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}})
from §
3.1.1
is universal not only for
\mathcal{C}_{W}^{\mathrm{reg}}
but the larger category
\mathcal{C}_{W}
, when interpreted using prismatic
F
-gauges with
\mathcal{G}
-structure of type
\mu
.

We continue to use the notation from Notation
3.24
and further fix an element
b
of
\mathcal{G}(W)\mu(p)^{-1}\mathcal{G}(W)
. The pair
(\mathcal{G}_{W},\varphi_{b})
, where
\varphi_{b}
corresponds to left multiplication by
b
, defines an element of
\mathbf{Tors}^{\varphi,-\mu}_{\mathcal{G}}(k_{{{{{\mathbbl{\Delta}}}}}})
which by Proposition
1.39
is equivalent to an object
\mathcal{P}_{b}
of
\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(k)
.

Given a functor of groupoids
F\colon\mathscr{G}_{1}\to\mathscr{G}_{2}
and an object
x
of
\mathscr{G}_{2}
we denote by
\mathrm{fib}(\mathscr{G}_{1}\to\mathscr{G}_{2};x)
the
fiber
over
x
which, by definition, means the groupoid of pairs
(y,\iota)
where
y
is an object of
\mathscr{G}_{2}
and
\iota\colon F(y)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}x
is an isomorphism, where a morphism
(y,\iota)\to(y^{\prime},\iota^{\prime})
is an isomorphism
\sigma\colon y\to y^{\prime}
such that
\iota^{\prime}\circ F(\sigma)=\iota
.

Definition 3.30
.

Let
\mathcal{Y}
be a formal stack over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Let
y
a point of
\mathcal{Y}(k)
for some
k
perfect extension of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
. Set
W=W(k)
. Define the
deformation functor
associated to the pair
(\mathcal{Y},y)
to be the contravariant functor

\mathrm{Def}_{y}\colon\mathcal{C}_{W}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathbf{Grpd},\quad R\mapsto\mathrm{fib}(\mathcal{Y}(R)\to\mathcal{Y}(k);y)

We say
\mathcal{Y}
has
discrete deformation theory
if for each
p
-nilpotent ring
R
, nilpotent ideal
I
of
R
, and point
y
of
\mathcal{Y}(R/I)
, the fiber
\mathrm{fib}(\mathcal{Y}(R)\to\mathcal{Y}(R/I);y)
is a discrete groupoid.

For
\mathcal{Y}=\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}
and
b
as above, we shorten the notation
\mathrm{Def}_{\mathcal{P}_{b}}
to
\mathrm{Def}_{b}
. We then have the following result which underlies the rest that follows.

Proposition 3.31
.

The formal stack
\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
has discrete deformation theory.

Before we prove Proposition
3.31
we give the following interpretation of Grothendieck–Messing theory that will be used many times below. Fix a PD thickening
(R^{\prime}\twoheadrightarrow R,\gamma)
of objects of
\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{p{\text{-fin}}}
. Let
x^{\prime}
be an object of
B\mathcal{G}(R^{\prime})
and set

\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R^{\prime})_{x^{\prime}}\vcentcolon=\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R^{\prime})\times_{x_{\mathrm{dR},R^{\prime}}^{\ast},B\mathcal{G}(R^{\prime})}x^{\prime},\quad\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R)_{x^{\prime}}\vcentcolon=\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R)\times_{\alpha_{\gamma},B\mathcal{G}(R^{\prime})}x^{\prime}.

Then, we have the following natural Cartesian diagram

(3.4.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R^{\prime})_{x^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}/P_{\mu}(R^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R)_{x^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}/P_{\mu}(R)}}

Here
\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}
is our shorthand for the
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
\alpha_{\gamma}(x^{\prime})
on
R^{\prime}
, which carries a natural action of
P_{\mu}
.
Here we are interpreting
\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}/P_{\mu}
as a quotient stack. We observe that (
3.4.2
) being Cartesian implies a natural isomorphism of groupoids

(3.4.3)

\mathrm{fib}\left(\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R^{\prime})\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R);z\right)\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-1.59442pt}{\smash{$\sim$}}}\mathrm{fib}\left((\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}/P_{\mu})(R^{\prime})\to(\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}/P_{\mu})(R);t_{z}\right),

for
z
in
\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}(R)_{x^{\prime}}
and
t_{z}
the image of
z
under the bottom arrow in (
3.4.2
).

This is quite helpful as
\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}/P_{\mu}
is actually a smooth algebraic space (cf.
[
SP
,
Tag 06PH
]
and
[
SP
,
Tag 0AHE
]
). Moreover, by
[
SP
,
Tag 06GE
]
, every surjective morphism
R\to R^{\prime}
of Artinian objects in
\mathcal{C}_{W}
may be factorized as a sequence of small surjections in the sense of
[
SP
,
Tag 06GD
]
. But, such a small surjection is, in particular, a square-zero thickening and so has a unique (and so canonical) PD structure
(\gamma_{i})
with
\gamma_{i}=0
for
i\geqslant 2
, which we denote by
\gamma
in all cases.

Proof of
3.31
.

Applying
[
SP
,
Tag 06GE
]
to
R\to R/I
it suffices to show that if
R^{\prime}\to R
is a small extension and an object
x
of
\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R)
, then
\mathrm{fib}\left(\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R^{\prime})\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R);x\right)
is discrete. But, given (
3.4.3
) this is clear as the right-hand side is a discrete groupoid.
∎

Consider the ring
R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
from §
3.1.1
. We abuse notation and conflate
\Spf(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})
(where
R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
is endowed with the topology defined by the maximal ideal) with the natural functor
\mathcal{C}_{W}^{\mathrm{op}}\to\mathbf{Set}
it represents. Using Proposition
1.48
, the pair
(\mathcal{A}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b}})
from §
3.1.1
upgrades to a deformation
(\mathcal{A}_{b,\mathrm{syn}}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b,\mathrm{syn}}})
of
\mathcal{P}_{b}
and so defines a morphism
\rho_{b}\colon\Spf(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})\to\mathrm{Def}_{b}
.

Proposition 3.32
.

The morphism

\rho_{b}\colon\Spf(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu})\to\mathrm{Def}_{b}

is an isomorphism. In particular, the pair
(\Spf(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}),(\mathcal{A}_{b,\mathrm{syn}}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}_{b,\mathrm{syn}}^{\mathrm{univ}}}))
represents
\mathrm{Def}_{b}
.

Proof.

We use the results of
[
GM24
]
to show that
\mathrm{Def}_{b}
is represented by an object
R_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}
of
\mathcal{C}_{W}^{\mathrm{reg}}
. This is sufficient, as the morphism
\rho_{b}
induces a bijection when evaluated on any object
R
of
\mathcal{C}_{W}^{\mathrm{reg}}
by the universal property of the pair
(\Spf(R_{\mathcal{G},\mu}),\mathcal{A}_{b,\mathrm{syn}}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b,\mathrm{syn}}})
(again utilizing Proposition
1.48
). As the source and target of
\rho_{b}
belong to
\mathcal{C}_{W}^{\mathrm{reg},\mathrm{op}}
, this implies that
\rho_{b}
is an isomorphism by the Yoneda lemma. To show that
\mathrm{Def}_{b}
is (pro)representable it suffices by Schlessinger’s criterion (cf.
[
SP
,
Tag 06JM
]
) to show that
\mathrm{Def}_{b}
satisfies condition
\mathrm{(RS)}
as in
[
SP
,
Tag 06J2
]
and that
\dim_{k}T\mathrm{Def}_{b}
is finite, where this tangent space is as in
[
SP
,
Tag 06I2
]
.
23
23

23

Note that condition (a) in Schlessinger’s criterion is equivalent to condition
\mathrm{(RS)}
by definition, as evidently
\mathrm{Def}_{b}
is a predeformation category in the sense of
[
SP
,
Tag 06GT
]
as
\mathrm{Def}_{b}(k)
is a singleton. Moreover, condition (c) is vacuous as
W
surjects onto
k
.

To show that
\mathrm{Def}_{b}
satisfies condition
\mathrm{(RS)}
we show that whenever we have a diagram of Artinian objects of
\mathcal{C}_{W}

R_{2}\rightarrow R\leftarrow R_{1}
such that the left arrow is a small surjection, that

(3.4.4)

\mathrm{Def}_{b}(R_{1}\times_{R}R_{2})\to\mathrm{Def}_{b}(R_{1})\times_{\mathrm{Def}_{b}(R)}\mathrm{Def}_{b}(R_{2})

is a bijection of sets. This is indeed sufficient by
[
SP
,
Tag 06J5
]
. As (
3.4.4
) is a map of sets over
\mathrm{Def}_{b}(R_{1})
it suffices to show that for each
s
in this set that the map in (
3.4.4
) induced on fibers over
s
is a bijection. But, this is naturally interpreted as the map of sets

\mathrm{fib}\left(\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R_{1}\times_{R}R_{2})\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R_{1});s\right)\to\mathrm{fib}\left(\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}_{\infty}(R_{2})\to\mathrm{BT}_{\infty}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu}(R);\overline{s}\right),

where
\overline{s}
is the image of
s
in
\mathrm{Def}_{b}(R)
. But, note that
R_{1}\times_{R}R_{2}\to R_{1}
is a small surjection by
[
SP
,
Tag 06GH
]
. So, using (
3.4.3
), if
x^{\prime}=\alpha_{\gamma}(s)
and
\overline{x}^{\prime}=\alpha_{\gamma}(\overline{s})
, we must show that the natural map

\mathrm{fib}\left((\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}/P_{\mu})(R_{1}\times_{R}R_{2})\to(\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}/P_{\mu})(R_{1});t_{s}\right)\to\mathrm{fib}\left((\mathcal{Q}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}/P_{\mu})(R_{2})\to(\mathcal{Q}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}/P_{\mu})(R);t_{\overline{s}}\right)

is a bijection, where we are using the fact that
\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}
base changes to
\mathcal{Q}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}
. But, this is clear as
\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}/P_{\mu}
is a smooth algebraic space.

For
\dim_{k}T\mathrm{Def}_{b}<\infty
, it suffices to observe that, as a special case of (
3.4.3
), we have an isomorphism of
k
-spaces
T\mathrm{Def}_{b}\to T\mathrm{Def}_{t_{b}}
, where
\mathrm{Def}_{t_{b}}
is the deformations of
t_{b}
in
(\mathcal{Q}_{b}/P_{\mu})(k)
inside
\mathcal{Q}_{b}/P_{\mu}
, with
\mathcal{Q}_{b}\vcentcolon=x_{\mathrm{dR},k}^{\ast}\mathcal{P}_{b}
. But, as
\mathcal{Q}_{b}/P_{\mu}
is a smooth algebraic space over
k
this is clear.

Finally, to show that the universal deformation ring
R_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}}
is an object of
\mathcal{C}_{W}^{\mathrm{reg}}
, it suffices by
[
SP
,
Tag 0DYL
]
to show that
\mathrm{Def}_{b}
is unobstructed in the sense of
[
SP
,
Tag 06HP
]
. But, by
[
SP
,
Tag 06HH
]
, we may restrict to small extensions. We may then apply (
3.4.3
) again to deduce unobstructedness as each
\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}/P_{\mu}
is a smooth algebraic space.
∎

Remark 3.33
.

Given Proposition
3.32
we shall often conflate
(\mathcal{A}_{b}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b}})
and
(\mathcal{A}_{b,\mathrm{syn}}^{\mathrm{univ}},\varphi_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{univ}}_{b,\mathrm{syn}}})
, only using the extra decoration when clarity is needed.

3.4.3.
Serre–Tate theory in the abelian-type case

We now verify an expectation of Drinfeld using results obtained so far. This allows us to further establish an analogue of the Serre–Tate theorem in the setting of abelian-type Shimura varieties at hyperspecial level.

Throughout this section we adopt the notation and conventions of Notation
2.1
and §
3.2
. In particular, we fix
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
to be an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type. We further fix an element
\mu^{c}_{h}
of the conjugacy class from
\bbmu_{h}^{c}
from §
2.1
.

3.4.3.1
Serre–Tate theory

Let us begin by observing that the syntomic realization functor
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}
on
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
as constructed in §
2.4
is a prismatic
F
-gauge with
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-structure of type
\mu_{h}^{c}
by Corollary
3.7
and Proposition
1.39
. Thus, we obtain a canonical morphism

\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty},

of formal stacks over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
The existence of such a morphism was expected by Drinfeld (see
[
Dri24b
, §4.3]
).
The main theorem of this section is the following result.

Theorem 3.34

(Serre–Tate theory)
.

The morphism

\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}

is formally étale, i.e., if
R
is a
p
-nilpotent ring and
R\to R/I
a nilpotent thickening, then

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}(R)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R/I)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}(R/I)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}}

is Cartesian.

To prove this theorem we need some preliminary setup. To this end, fix
x
to be a point of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
. As in §
3.3.1
we have the associated element
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}
in
C(\mathcal{G}^{c})
. Additionally, recall that we have the associated conjugacy class
\bbmu_{h}^{c}
of cocharacters
\mathbb{G}_{m,\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
. Then, by Lemma
3.5
the element
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}
lies in the image of the map

\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\sigma(\mu_{h}^{c}(p))^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\to C(\mathcal{G}^{c}).

Fix
b_{x}
in
\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})\mu^{c}_{h}(p)^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{c}(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})

with
\sigma(b_{x})
mapping to
\bm{b}_{x,\mathrm{crys}}
in
C(\mathcal{G}^{c})
, e.g.,
b_{x}=\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(x)
.

Consider the functor

\mathrm{Def}_{x}\colon\mathcal{C}_{W}\to\mathbf{Set},\qquad\mathrm{Def}_{x}\vcentcolon=\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}|_{\mathcal{C}_{W}}.

Note that
\mathrm{Def}_{x}
parameterizes the deformations of
x
within
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
and is (pro)represented by the formal spectrum
\Spf(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}},x})=\Spf(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}}^{p},x})
.

The following is an immediate consequence of Theorem
3.6
and Proposition
3.32
. Indeed, Proposition
1.39
implies that (with notation as in these results) that
\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p},_{x}}=\rho_{b_{x}}\circ i_{x}
, as both pull back the universal object over
\mathrm{Def}_{b_{x}}
to isomorphic deformations of
\mathcal{P}_{b_{x}}=(\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},{\mathrm{syn}}})|_{x}
.

Lemma 3.35
.

The induced map

\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p},x}\colon\mathrm{Def}_{x}\to\mathrm{Def}_{b_{x}}

is an isomorphism.

Proof of Theorem
3.34
.

By inducting on the minimal
n\geqslant 2
such that
I^{n}=(0)
, we may assume without loss of generality that
n=2
.

So, suppose that
R
is a
p
-nilpotent ring and
I\subseteq R
is square-zero ideal, then we must show that for any
R/I
-point
x
of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
that the natural map

(3.4.5)

\mathrm{fib}\left(\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R)\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R/I);x\right)\to\mathrm{fib}\left(\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}(R)\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}(R/I);\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(x)\right),

is a bijection of sets (where the right-hand is discrete by Proposition
3.31
). The proof will proceed in several steps. But, before we start those steps, we make the following observation.

Lemma 3.36
.

Suppose that
(\mathcal{G},\mu)
is as in Notation
3.1
. Then if
R=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{i}R_{i}
is a filtered colimit of rings and
I=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits I_{i}
an ideal with
I
and each
I_{i}
square-zero. Then the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{i}\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}(R_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}(R)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{i}\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}(R_{i}/I_{i})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}(R/I)}

is Cartesian.

Proof.

The natural map from
\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{i}\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}(R_{i})
to the fiber product is evidently injectivity. To show surjectivity, fix an object
\mathcal{P}
of
\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}(R)
and consider the
\mathcal{G}
-torsor
x=x_{\mathrm{dR},R}^{\ast}\mathcal{P}
which is the image of some
x_{i}
in
B\mathcal{G}(R_{i})
for sufficiently large
i
(see
[
Čes15
, Lemma 2.1]
). Consider

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\displaystyle\twocolim_{j\geqslant i}\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}(R_{j})_{x_{i}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}(R)_{x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{Q}_{x}/P_{-\mu})(R)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\displaystyle\twocolim_{j\geqslant i}\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}(R_{j}/I_{j})_{x_{i}}}}
