{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},\mu}_{\infty}(R/I)_{x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{Q}_{x}/P_{-\mu})(R/I).}}

Here the left-hand square is the obvious ones, and the right-hand square is the Cartesian square as in (
3.4.2
). Observe that
\mathcal{Q}_{x}/P_{-\mu}=(\mathcal{Q}_{x_{i}}/P_{-\mu})_{R}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{x_{i}}/P_{-\mu}
is of finite presentation over
R_{i}
, we have that the outer rectangular diagram (ignoring the central nodes) is obtained by passing to the
2
-colimit over
j\geqslant i
of the Cartesian diagram as in (
3.4.2
) applied for
R_{j}\twoheadrightarrow R_{j}/I_{j}
. As
2
-colimits of Cartesian diagrams of groupoids is Cartesian, we deduce the outer rectangle is also Cartesian. Thus, we deduce the left-hand square is Cartesian as desired.
∎

Step 1: restriction to
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebras

Observe that it suffices to show that
(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}}_{\infty}
is formally étale. Indeed, this follows from the following general observation
as well as the fact that
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
is ind-étale modulo
p^{n}
for all
n
.

Lemma 3.37
.

Let

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

be a diagram of fpqc-stacks, where
X
and
Y
are schemes. If
f
is surjective and pro-étale and
p
is formally étale, then
q
is formally étale.

Proof.

We begin by making the following observation. Consider a commutative diagram

(3.4.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(R/I)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(R).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}

As
f
is surjective and pro-étale, there exists an ind-étale cover
\overline{c}\colon R/I\to\overline{C}
and a map
\gamma\colon\Spec(\overline{C})\to X
such that
f\circ\gamma=a\circ\overline{c}
. By
[
SP
,
Tag 097P
]
there exists an ind-étale
R
-algebra
C
such that
\overline{C}=C/IC
, which necessarily induces a surjective map
c\colon\Spec(C)\to\Spec(R)
. We thus obtain the following commutative diagram

(3.4.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(R/I)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(R)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(C/IC)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Spec(C).}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{c}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

We use of this diagram twice below.

We first show that
q
is formally unramified. Suppose that
\alpha,\beta\colon\Spec(R)\to Y
are two morphisms whose addition to (
3.4.7
) preserves commutativity. Using the fact that
f
is pro-étale and surjective we may (after possibly enlarging
C
) produce
\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}\colon\Spec(C)\to X
whose addition to (
3.4.7
) preserves commutativity. But, by the formal unramifiedness of
p
we deduce that
\alpha^{\prime}
equals
\beta^{\prime}
, and thus that

\alpha\circ c=f\circ\alpha^{\prime}=f\circ\beta^{\prime}=\beta\circ c.

As
c
is an epimorphism of schemes, we deduce that
\alpha
equals
\beta
as desired.

We now show that
q
is formally étale. As we know that
q
is formally unramified, it suffices to show that there exists a map
\alpha\colon\Spec(R)\to Y
such that
\alpha\circ i=a
and
q\circ\alpha=b
.
Now, as
p
is formally étale there exists a unique morphism
\delta\colon\Spec(C)\to X
whose addition to (
3.4.7
) preserves commutativity. By a simple diagram chase, it suffices to show that the map
f\circ\delta\colon\Spec(C)\to Y
descends to a morphism
\Spec(R)\to Y
. As
\Spec(C)\to\Spec(R)
is an fpqc cover and
Y
is a sheaf for the fpqc topology on
S
, it suffices to show that
f\circ\delta
equalizes the two natural maps
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\colon\Spec(C\otimes_{R}C)\to\Spec(C)
. But, reducing
f\circ\delta\circ\pi_{i}
modulo
I
gives the same map, as the map
a\circ\overline{c}\colon\Spec(C/IC)\to Y
does descend to a map
\Spec(R/I)\to Y
. Thus, by the formal unramifiedness of
Y\to\mathcal{F}
it suffices to show that
q\circ f\circ\delta\circ\pi_{1}=q\circ f\circ\delta\circ\pi_{2}
. But, this is clear as the map
q\circ f\circ\delta
does descend to a map
\Spec(R)\to\mathcal{F}
(namely
b
).
∎

Moreover, let us observe that if
\Spec(R/I)\to(\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
is a morphism, then we have a map
\Spec(R/I)\to\Spec(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
, and by the formal étaleness of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{n}\to\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}/p^{n}
for all
n
there exists a unique extension to a map
\Spec(R)\to\Spec(\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p})
. Thus, we see that without loss of generality, we may assume that
R
is a
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebra.

Step 2: reduction to finitely generated
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebras

Suppose that
R
is an arbitrary
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebra and
I
is a square-zero ideal. Observe that we may write
R
as a filtered colimit
R=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{i}R_{i}
where
R_{i}
ranges over all finitely generated
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-subalgebras of
R
. If
I_{i}\vcentcolon=I\cap R_{i}
then
I=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{i}I_{i}
. Moreover,
I_{i}^{2}\subseteq I^{2}=(0)
, so
I_{i}^{2}
is also square-zero. Suppose that we have shown that the map in (
3.4.5
) is a bijection for all finite-type
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebras. Then, in particular

\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{i}\mathrm{fib}\left(\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R_{i})\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R_{i}/I_{i});x\right)\to\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{i}\mathrm{fib}\left(\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}(R_{i})\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}(R_{i}/I_{i});\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(x)\right)

is a bijection. But, the source is
\mathrm{fib}\left(\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R)\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(R/I);x\right)
by the finite presentation of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
and the latter is
\mathrm{fib}\left(\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}(R)\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}(R/I);\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(x)\right)
by Lemma
3.36
. The claim follows.

Step 3: the Hodge-type case

We now assume that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is of Hodge type. We will then apply the following lemma for an integral Hodge embedding.

Lemma 3.38
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{Y}
be a closed embedding of topologically of finite type formal
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-schemes and let
R
be a
p
-nilpotent finite type
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebra. Then a morphism
\Spec(R)\to\mathfrak{Y}
factorizes through
\mathfrak{X}
if and only if for every maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
of
R
the composition
\Spf(\widehat{R}_{\mathfrak{m}})\to\Spec(R)\to\mathfrak{Y}
factorizes through
\mathfrak{X}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{J}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{Y}}
be the ideal sheaf corresponding to
\mathfrak{X}
. It suffices to show that
\mathcal{J}\mathcal{O}_{\Spec(R)}
is zero. But, for this it suffices to check this vanishing on each
R_{\mathfrak{m}}
. Moreover, as
R_{\mathfrak{m}}\to\widehat{R}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is faithfully flat (see
[
SP
,
Tag 00MC
]
) it further suffices to check that
\mathcal{J}\mathcal{O}_{\Spf(\widehat{R}_{\mathfrak{m}})}
is zero. But, this follows from the existence of a factorization of
\Spf(\widehat{R}_{\mathfrak{m}})\to\Spec(R)\to\mathfrak{Y}
through
\mathfrak{X}
.
∎

Choose now an integral Hodge embedding
\iota\colon(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})\hookrightarrow(\mathbf{H},\mathfrak{h}^{\pm},\mathcal{H})
and let
\mu_{h}^{\mathrm{s}}
be a (choice of) integral Hodge cocharacter for the Siegel-type Shimura datum base changed to
W
. By
[
Xu20
, Theorem 1.1.1]
this integral Hodge embedding induces a closed embedding

\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\mathbf{H},\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})

for an appropriately chosen neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{L}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. By
Step 2
it suffices to prove (
3.4.5
) for
R
finite type over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. But using Example
3.29
we may interpret the composition

\Spec(R)\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu_{h}}_{\infty}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{H},-\mu_{h}^{\mathrm{s}}}_{\infty}

as a quasi-polarized
p
-divisible group
(H,L,\lambda)
over
R
deforming
(A_{0}[p^{\infty}],L_{0},\lambda_{0})
, where we write
(A_{0},L_{0},\lambda_{0},\alpha_{0})
for the point of
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\mathbf{H},\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})(R/I)
corresponding to the composition

\Spec(R/I)\to\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\mathbf{H},\mathfrak{h}^{\pm}),

where
\alpha_{0}
denotes the
\mathsf{K}^{p}
-level structure. By the classical version of Serre–Tate theory (e.g., see
[
Kat81
, §1]
) there exists a unique deformation
(A,L,\lambda,\alpha)
in
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\mathbf{H},\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})(R)
of
(A_{0},\lambda_{0},L_{0},\alpha_{0})
such that
(A[p^{\infty}],L,\lambda)=(H,L,\lambda)
.
By Lemma
3.38
we will be done if we can show that for any maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}
of
R
the restriction of
(A[p^{\infty}],L,\lambda)
is in the image of

\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})(\widehat{R}_{\mathfrak{m}})\hookrightarrow\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\mathbf{H},\mathfrak{h}^{\pm})(\widehat{R}_{\mathfrak{m}})

Note that
\widehat{R}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is an object of
\mathcal{C}_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
. As we have a morphism
\Spf(\widehat{R}_{\mathfrak{m}})\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G},-\mu_{h}}_{\infty}
extending the map
\Spec(R/\mathfrak{m})\to\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
we deduce from Lemma
3.35
that such an extension exists.

Step 4: the special type case

Suppose now that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is of special type. Then the bijectivity of (
3.4.5
) may be checked by hand. Indeed, in this case
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is isomorphic to a disjoint union of the form
\Spec(\mathcal{O}_{F})
, where
F
is an an unramified extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
(e.g., see
[
DY24
, Proposition 3.22]
). Working component by component, we may replace
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
with
\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{F})
. As
\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{F})\to\Spf(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is formally étale, we see that the morphism
\Spec(R/I)\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{F})
for the
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebra
R
, uniquely lifts to a morphism
\Spec(R)\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{F})
. We will thus be done if we can show that the composition
\Spec(R)\to\Spf(\mathcal{O}_{F})\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}
agrees with the given one from (
3.4.5
). But, it’s clear that the two maps induce the same map
\Spec(R/I)\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}
. So, the claim follows from (
3.4.3
) as, in this case, we have that
\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}/P_{\mu_{h}^{c}}
is trivial, as
\mathcal{G}^{c}
is a torus, and so there is a unique lift of this
\Spec(R/I)\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}
to a point
\Spec(R)\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}
.

Step 5: the abelian type case

Suppose now that
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
is of abelian type. Consider the objects as in Lemma
2.9
. For an appropriate neat compact open subgroup and
\mathsf{M}^{p}\subseteq\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times\mathbf{T}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
the map
\alpha
induces a closed embedding

(3.4.8)

\alpha\colon\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}}(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2})\hookrightarrow\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{M}^{p}}(\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{T},\mathbf{X}_{1}\times\{h\}).

To prove this it suffices to pass to
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. Now, it’s evident that
(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1},\mathcal{G}_{1})
is an adapted Hodge type datum for both
(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2})
and
(\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{T},\mathbf{X}_{1}\times\{h\},\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T})
. Thus, by construction of integral canonical models as in
[
Kis10
, (3.4.11)]
, we have that at infinite level, the choice of a connected component
\mathscr{S}^{+}
of
(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p,1}})_{\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}}
gives an identification of the map
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p,2}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{p,1}\times L_{p}}
(base changed to
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
) with the map

[\mathscr{S}^{+}\times\mathscr{A}(\mathcal{G}_{2})]/\mathscr{A}(\mathcal{G}_{2})^{\circ}\to[\mathscr{S}^{+}\times\mathscr{A}(\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T})]/\mathscr{A}(\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T})^{\circ},

with notation as in loc. cit. We claim this map is a closed embedding. Indeed, from the fact that
\mathcal{G}_{2}\to\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T}
and
\mathbf{G}_{2}\to\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{T}
are closed embeddings inducing isomorphisms on derived subgroups (and so induce isomorphisms on adjoint groups and has the property that
Z(\mathbf{G}_{1})=Z(\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{T})\cap\mathbf{G}_{1}
and the integral analogue) we see that the map
\mathscr{A}(\mathcal{G}_{2})\to\mathscr{A}(\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T})
is a closed embedding. We are then done as the group
\mathscr{A}(\mathcal{B})^{\circ}
only depends on
\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{der}}
(e.g., see
[
KP18
, Lemma 4.6.4 (2)]
), so that
\mathscr{A}(\mathcal{G}_{2})^{\circ}=\mathscr{A}(\mathcal{G}_{1}\times\mathcal{T})^{\circ}
. We then deduce the existence of the desired
\mathsf{M}^{p}
by arguing as in
[
Del71
, Proposition 1.15]
.

Fix a finite index subgroup of
\mathsf{M}^{p}
of the form
\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}\times\mathsf{L}^{p}
, and so we obtain a finite étale morphism

\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{1}}(\mathbf{G}_{1},\mathbf{X}_{1})\times\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\mathbf{T},\{h\})\simeq\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}_{1}^{p}\times\mathsf{L}^{p}}(\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{T},\mathbf{X}_{1}\times\{h\})\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{M}^{p}}(\mathbf{G}_{1}\times\mathbf{T},\mathbf{X}_{1}\times\{h\}).

As we have already verified formal étaleness for Hodge and special type, it follows that formal étaleness holds also for the source. Applying Lemma
3.37
we deduce also that formal étaleness holds at level
\mathsf{M}^{p}
. One may then apply the same argument as in
Step 3
to the closed embedding in (
3.4.8
) to deduce the formal étaleness result holds for
(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2},\mathcal{G}_{2})
.

Finally, to deduce the result for
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
, we observe that we have a finite étale morphism

\beta\colon\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}}(\mathbf{G}_{2},\mathbf{X}_{2})\to\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X}),

for an appropriate choice of neat compact open subgroup
\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}\subseteq\mathbf{G}_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Moreover, we know that
\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\circ\beta
agrees with
\pi\circ\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}}
where
\pi\colon\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c},-\mu_{h2}^{c}}_{\infty}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}
is the natural map. We already know that
\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}}
is formally étale, and we claim that
\pi
is as well. Indeed, this follows from the identification in (
3.4.3
) as
\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}
is a central isogeny, and so the natural morphism
\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}_{2}}/P_{\mu_{h2}^{c}}\to\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}/P_{\mu_{h}^{c}}
, where the notation has the obvious meaning is an isomorphism. Indeed, this may be checked over an étale cover, in which case we may assume that
\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}_{2}}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\prime}}
are trivial, so that this is the natural map
\mathcal{G}_{2}^{c}/P_{\mu_{h2}^{c}}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}/P_{\mu_{h}^{c}}
. But, this is obviously an isomorphism as the flag variety does not change under central isogenies.
24
24

24

Indeed, let
f\colon\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
be a central isogeny of reductive group schemes over a base scheme
S
and let
Z\subseteq Z(\mathcal{G})
be its kernel. Then, for a cocharacter
\mu
of
\mathcal{G}
the morphism
P_{f\circ\mu}\to\mathcal{H}
is the result of quotienting
P_{\mu}\to\mathcal{G}
by
Z
. Thus, as
\mathcal{G}/P_{\mu}=(\mathcal{G}/Z)/(P_{\mu}/Z)
this implies the desired claim.

We deduce that
\pi\circ\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}_{2}}
is formally étale, and so
\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is formally étale by Lemma
3.37
on the connected components lying in
\mathrm{im}(\beta)
. But, we may then deduce the formal étaleness of
\rho_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
over all connected components, using a translation argument (see the proof of Theorem
2.10
).
∎

3.4.3.2
Syntomic characterization

We now formulate a more conceptual, but a priori stronger, version of the notion of prismatic integral model from Definition
3.17
. We use the notion of a prismatic
F
-gauge with
\mathcal{G}
-structure of type
\bm{\mu}
modeling a
\mathcal{G}
-object in de Rham local systems on
\mathscr{X}_{E}
, for a (formal) scheme
\mathscr{X}
over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. This is a straightforward generalization of that in Definition
3.17
, and we follow similar conventions as in the prismatic
F
-crystal setting.

Definition 3.39
.

A smooth separated model
\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
(resp.
\mathscr{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
) of
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
(resp.
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
) is called a
syntomic integral canonical model
if there exists a prismatic
F
-gauge with
\mathcal{G}^{c}
-structure of type
-\mu_{h}^{c}
modeling
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{an}}
(resp.
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}
) such that the induced map

\mathfrak{X}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty},\quad\bigg(\text{resp. }\widehat{\mathscr{X}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathrm{BT}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}\bigg)

is formally étale (resp. and is also a model of
(\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}},U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}})
in the sense of Definition
3.16
).

From Proposition
1.39
and Proposition
3.32
any syntomic integral canonical model is a prismatic integral canonical model. So, the following is a consequence of Theorem
3.13
and Theorem
3.20
.

Theorem 3.40
.

Let
(\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X},\mathcal{G})
be an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type and
\mathsf{K}^{p}
a neat-compact open subgroup of
\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Then
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
(resp.
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
) is the unique syntomic integral canonical model of
\mathrm{Sh}_{\mathsf{K}_{0}\mathsf{K}^{p}}
(resp.
U_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
).

3.5.
Applications to the theory of
\mathcal{G}
-zips

In
[
Oor01
]
, Oort defined stratifications of the special fiber of integral canonical models of Siegel-type Shimura varieties in terms of the group-theoretic properties of the
p
-torsion of the universal abelian scheme. To generalize this idea to integral canonical models
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
for other unramified Shimura data, in
[
PWZ15
]
, the authors defined the Artin stack
\mathcal{H}\text{-}\mathsf{Zip}^{\mu}
of
(\mathcal{H},\mu)
-zips
for a reductive group
\mathcal{H}/\mathbb{F}_{q}
with cocharacter
\mu
.

The generalization of Oort’s ideas should take the form of a map
\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon\overline{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}\text{-}\mathsf{Zip}^{-\mu_{h}^{c}}
where
\overline{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is the reduction modulo
p
of
\mathscr{S}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
. Such a map was constructed by Viehmann–Wedhorn in
[
VW13
]
for PEL-type Shimura varieties, and by Zhang in
[
Zha18
]
for Hodge-type Shimura varieties. In fact, Zhang was able to show that the map
\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}
is smooth.

One can use the syntomic realization functor
\omega_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}
to generalize such results to the abelian-type setting. Namely, by reducing modulo
p
we obtain a formally étale

(3.5.1)

\overline{\omega}_{\mathsf{K}^{p},\mathrm{syn}}\colon\overline{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\overline{\mathrm{BT}}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty},

where again we are using
\overline{\mathrm{BT}}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}
to denote the reduction of
{\mathrm{BT}}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}
modulo
p
. By passing to the
1
-truncation (i.e., pulling back a
\mathcal{G}
-bundle along
R^{\mathrm{syn}}\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}\to R^{\mathrm{syn}}
), we obtain a morphism

(3.5.2)

\overline{\mathrm{BT}}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}_{\infty}\to\overline{\mathrm{BT}}_{1}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}

which is formally smooth by
[
GM24
, Theorem D]
. By
[
GM24
, Theorem E]
there is a morphism

(3.5.3)

\overline{\mathrm{BT}}_{1}^{\mathcal{G}^{c},-\mu_{h}^{c}}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}\text{-}\mathsf{Zip}^{-\mu_{h}^{c}},

which, in fact, is a gerbe for an explicit group scheme (and so smooth). Composing the above maps, we arrive at the following result.

Theorem 3.41
.

Suppose that
p>2
and
(\mathcal{G},\mathbf{G},\mathbf{X})
is an unramified Shimura datum of abelian type. Then, composing (
3.5.1
), (
3.5.2
), and (
3.5.3
) gives a smooth morphism

\zeta_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\colon\overline{\mathscr{S}}_{\mathsf{K}^{p}}\to\mathcal{G}^{c}\text{-}\mathsf{Zip}^{-\mu_{h}^{c}}.
